UNION SUITE / AUTHOR HANDBOOK

Build consistent
iMIS pages.

Choose a component, apply its classes in the right place, and use the shared templates. Branding flows from one set of theme tokens.

What is available today?

Local implementation status. Installing or uploading these files is a separate step.

In shared theme

Branding & IQA reports

Seeded colours, design tokens, report styling, filter disclosure, native Export and expanded report view.

Use a report →
In shared theme

Reusable banners

Shared theme CSS and JavaScript supply the banner styling, sticky/condensing behaviour and Actions disclosure. Tab switching remains deferred.

Use a banner →
In shared theme

Lists & cards

A shared item shell for staff bulletins, member notes, compact task rows and chronological history. Optional content slots and class-based header search.

Use a list →
Planned

Connected features

Banner tab switching, named business actions and the combined activity feed still need their adapters and handlers.

Read the boundaries →
Custom component classes generally use us-. Use us-banner-sticky, not ut-banner-sticky. The shared welcome header retains its existing home-welcome and home-welcome-date names. Existing iMIS buttons use their native names, including TextButton and btn. Enter classes without leading dots in iMIS configuration fields.

How to use this handbook: each implemented component has a visual example and placement instructions. Click a class chip to copy its exact text; use the Copy button on a code block for HTML. Examples in their own frames resize to show their content, except the deliberately scrollable banner, combined preview, IQA and action builder.

02 / SETUP

Install the right layer

Nested navigation expanders: the native .sub-nav-body .RadTreeView_Orion .rtPlus and .rtMinus circles now use --nav-bg (falling back to --brand-800 and then the original Orion colour). This keeps secondary branches such as Admin aligned with client sidebar colours in collapsed and expanded states, including hovered or selected rows. Top-level expanders retain their transparent treatment; native caret icons, sizing and expand/collapse handlers remain unchanged. Upload updated 99-Orion.css; no author class or JavaScript change is needed.

Header logo and app menu: in the native #hd > #masterTopBarAuxiliary > .navbar-left structure, the direct .app-menu-wrapper is hidden. At desktop/tablet widths (768px and above), .wrapper.nav-expanded centres the logo above the expanded sidebar using --sidebar-w-open (250px). Without that class the logo is left-aligned; smaller mobile headers retain left alignment. The native .navbar-toggle.primary-nav-toggle still controls navigation state; no new JavaScript or author class is needed. Logo artwork and dimensions stay in the client stylesheet. Upload updated 99-Orion.css.

Collapsed sidebar on tablets: from 768px to 991px, the collapsed sidebar is a slim 42px full-height rail holding the expand button, and the page content starts after it. Native Orion floated that button as a 42px tab over the page, so a pinned record banner or sticky header slid underneath it. The header keeps its existing 42px indent beside the rail. From 992px the native 62.5px icon rail stays. Upload updated 99-Orion.css; no class or script change is needed.

No sideways page scroll: the page wrapper clips horizontal overflow whether the sidebar is open or collapsed (previously only when open). Layout rows keep Bootstrap's negative side margins, and a full-width section that reaches the window edge — a CCO page beside the collapsed sidebar, for example — otherwise gave the page a horizontal scrollbar. The clip uses overflow-x: clip, which creates no scroll container, so sticky headers and rails keep working; wide grids still scroll inside their own report. Upload updated 99-Orion.css.

Files and their responsibilities
FilePurposeWhen needed
99-Orion.css + native assetsProject-owned iMIS theme foundation, 24px page-layout gutters, navigation, control skins, icons and default Query Template Display card styling.Deploy the updated foundation with its native dependencies. Standard layout spacing updates automatically; no iPart class is needed.
Tabler.css + Tabler/Root stylesheet shim, Tabler 3.31.0 icon CSS, WOFF2 font and licence.Staff navigation font icons and authored ti icons. Preserve the relative folder structure.
zUnionSuite.cssShared tokens, staff navigation icon mappings, existing native button colours, IQA report CSS, shared welcome header, list aliases/authored shells/optional arrangements, native V5/H2 tabs, and separate tab/banner page-layout and component sections.Every page using Union Suite components. Native buttons, supported tab displays and configured staff icons update automatically. Native Query Template Display cards are automatic; us-list is needed only for authored list wrappers.
UnionSuite-Client/Branding.cssGenerated branding CSS copied from Theme-Config.html. Replace the whole file when applying a new configuration.Load after the shared core theme CSS.
UnionSuite-Client/Override.cssEditable client-specific CSS rules that override the base theme, including custom styling and logos.Load after Branding.css. Keep custom rules here when replacing generated branding.
UnionSuite-Client/Config.jsEditable client JavaScript settings, including pipGreeting: true to enable Biscuit or false to remove him and his reserved space, and UnionSuiteCcoSwitchConfig.enabled for CCO tab switching (false restores native page reloads).Load before Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js; reload the page after changing the setting.
zzDarkMode.cssDark palette, mode-specific native/component overrides and taskbar appearance-switch styling.Load last, after all shared and client CSS. See dark mode installation.
index.jsTheme loader: the site's one header include (confirmed 3 October 2026). Loads the theme scripts in dependency order; its RELEASE makes browsers fetch changed files.Add once to the site header (snippet below). Bump RELEASE in every release. The client Config.js and Actions.js are not loaded by it yet: put settings inline before the include.
zUnionSuite.jsDocument appearance preference, IQA filters, Query Template Display search, Export relocation, expanded view, action slot, banner sticky/collapse, Actions disclosure, icon-button tooltips and in-place CCO tab switching.Loaded by index.js. Themed iframe and popup documents need the loader include too.
Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.jsApproved header taskbar and Biscuit: daily greeting, five-minute visit, 15-second idle reactions and wave/hop/goodbye clicks.Loaded by index.js; settings go inline before the loader include. Keep native header search markup and remove the previous taskbar injection. See Biscuit installation.
Forms/Popup forms: the forms runtime (UnionSuiteForms.js), the forms' own stylesheet (UnionSuiteForms.css) and one file per form (TaskForm.js). Works without the theme's CSS or zUnionSuite.js.Loaded by index.js on pages with a data-us-form placeholder; a page without the theme includes Forms/UnionSuiteForms.js directly. Upload the whole folder.
Banner-Shared-Styles.htmlGenerated banner-only fallback for older installations; no CSS.Only when shared theme JS is unavailable. Omit from new installations.
Banner-Behaviour.jsGenerated copy of the shared theme's banner block.Legacy external fallback, not an additional theme include.
  1. Load the styles in order. Native Orion → zUnionSuite.css → UnionSuite-Client/Branding.css → UnionSuite-Client/Override.css → zzDarkMode.css. Dark mode must be last. Keep native icon, font and control assets. Uploading a separate theme/client ZIP does not itself establish stylesheet order.
  2. Load the theme JavaScript through the loader. One header include, index.js, loads zUnionSuite.js, Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js, the taskbar and its bookmarks, and the page-specific scripts (sign-in page, IQA and Business Object designers) in dependency order. Deploy it once; later releases ship in the theme folder alone, with the loader's RELEASE bumped so browsers fetch the new files. Confirmed on the dev tenant, 3 October 2026. The client Actions.js is not loaded by the loader yet; until it is, client registrations wait for UnionSuiteLoader.ready. See action installation and migration. Merely placing a JS file in the theme folder does not load it.
  3. Install the approved taskbar and Biscuit. The theme archive includes Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js and its shared drawing/animation CSS. Load the client Config.js first, then the taskbar script once with defer. Keep pipGreeting: true to enable Biscuit. The taskbar include uses the existing header; no mascot HTML or preview controls need copying.
  4. Use the same include for banners. The updated zUnionSuite.js contains sticky/collapse and Actions behaviour. Keep the visible banner HTML in its own content iPart. No separate banner script iPart is needed.
  5. Apply the component classes. Use the class reference below. Check the rendered page outside Easy Edit, including a real partial refresh.
  6. Retire older includes after migration. Once the updated shared CSS/JS are deployed and loaded, remove old report and banner style/script embeds and any separate Banner-Behaviour.js include. Keep the visible banner HTML and classes, reload, and verify scrolling, Actions and a real partial refresh.
Theme loader — the site header include
<script>
  // Optional settings go here, before the loader, e.g.
  // window.UnionSuiteTaskbarConfig = { pipGreeting: false };
  (function () {
    var s = document.createElement('script');
    // 20-minute bucket: App_Themes is cached for a week, so a stable URL
    // would hold a release back for up to a week.
    s.src = '/App_Themes/UnionSuite-Core/index.js?t=' + Math.floor(Date.now() / 1200000);
    s.async = true;
    document.head.appendChild(s);
  })();
</script>

Change UnionSuite-Core to the folder that holds the theme JavaScript and adjust the application path if necessary. The loader finds its other files beside itself, so only this one path is configured. Use a web URL, never a local Windows file path. In the console, UnionSuiteLoader.status() lists each file and whether it loaded.

Where settings land in iMIS

Authoring fieldRendered locationUse it for
Page CSS class<body class="…">Page-wide layout, such as us-banner-page.
Zone CSS classAdded to .WebPartZoneGrouping a zone. No extra zone class is required for current banners or reports.
iPart CSS classAn extra wrapper inside .ContentItemContainerEnabling a component and its behaviour.
HTML / query templateAuthored content inside the iPart outputSlots such as us-banner__title, facts and optional controls.

A zone title can be rendered outside its zone. Panels and zones can nest inside a Content Collection Organizer (CCO). Preserve the native layout and use the documented component hooks instead of guessing generated IDs.

Adding an iPart class adds a div

Check this when a shell or header action disappears after adding a class. The iPart's CSS class field makes iMIS insert an extra div inside ContentItemContainer, around the native output. The class is on that new div, not on ContentItemContainer or the panel. This is a general iPart configuration rule; Staff Bulletin is one example.

For an iPart that renders a panel, these are the two structures to support. They illustrate generated output only: do not paste these wrappers into Content HTML or the Query Template field.

Generated output — no iPart class, direct panel
<div class="ContentItemContainer">
  <div class="panel">…native heading and body…</div>
</div>
Generated output — iPart CSS class = us-staff-bulletin
<div class="ContentItemContainer">
  <div class="us-staff-bulletin">
    <div class="panel">…native heading and body…</div>
  </div>
</div>

Multiple space-separated classes share the new div. Some native output retains an empty <div class="">, so inspect the rendered structure even when the field is blank. Some iParts render no panel at all. Page and zone class fields use the locations in the table above.

For authors: keep the class in the iPart's CSS class field and use only the supplied inner template. In a Query Template Display, that template repeats once per record; the generated class wrapper encloses the whole iPart. Moving the class into each result will not repair outer-shell detection.

Selector and preview rules For theme maintainers

.ContentItemContainer > .panel covers only the direct form. Support both the direct panel and .ContentItemContainer > div > .panel, then validate the component's own native contents. Keep header/body selectors relative to that panel's immediate owner. A blanket .ContentItemContainer .panel selector can also style nested iParts and CCO panels.

Runtime markers and header-action class lookup belong on the matched immediate owner. Preview generators must reproduce the added div, not put the class on ContentItemContainer or supply runtime attributes in advance. Check direct, configured-class, empty-wrapper, nested and no-styling cases; check partial replacement for JavaScript adapters. The Staff Bulletin and data-panel fixes cover these structures, but other component adapters still need their own validation.

03 / FOUNDATIONS

Brand once. Reuse everywhere.

Five brand seeds feed palette ramps. Semantic tokens name each colour's purpose; component aliases consume those tokens.

Client branding workspace: open Theme-Config.html — Brand studio for one set of five editable seed swatches, optional searchable overrides and one component example at a time. The thin strip below the controls shows the derived palette. Member overview, dummy IQA, native CCO tabs, forms/feedback and Actions all use the shared theme. This implemented, standalone editor is separate from the production theme runtime; persistence and deployment are deferred.

The config page works offline as a single HTML file in the top-level THeme/UnionSuite folder. Edit a colour picker or hex field for immediate styling. In Fine-tune your theme, empty fields follow the source defaults; an entered value pins a token. Clear a field or use its reset button to restore inheritance. Individual overrides cover palette/status colours, semantic aliases, typography, spacing, layout, checkbox/radio colours, and banner/IQA/Actions aliases. Semantic pins intentionally detach from their palette; prefer seeds and palette stops for normal branding.

Sample links stay inside their component example: fragment links scroll locally, unconnected destinations do not navigate, and the dummy IQA can still download its CSV. Preview-only navigation handling prevents the config file from opening inside its own frame. If an old preview has already done this, reload Theme-Config.html after rebuilding.

All config examples receive the same validated stylesheet, including hidden or later-loaded frames. Invalid CSS or circular/unknown token references retain the last valid preview and show a field error. Root tokens apply on :root; banner aliases apply to .us-banner, IQA aliases to report/native-panel wrappers, and Actions aliases to .us-actions, after shared CSS. The Your branding CSS block includes only active valid overrides, with Copy. Nothing is stored, submitted or written to the client stylesheet. Reload discards edits; Reset colours, Clear overrides and Reset everything provide separate reset scopes.

Preview options demonstrate us-banner-sticky or us-banner-collapsible on the overview banner wrapper, us-filters-collapsed and optional us-report-expandable on the dummy report wrapper. These switches call shared refresh APIs and are excluded from the CSS output. Checkbox/radio selectors instead write --checkbox-colour / --radio-colour as var(--accent) or var(--brand-600). CCOs use implemented V5/H2 styling; fixture panel switching and dummy IQA data remain in guide sources. Preview tabs support arrows, Home/End and keyboard focus; native/shared controls retain their accessibility and reduced-motion behaviour. Layout tokens affect only actual consumers, and this is not a full staff-site shell or a live iMIS integration test.

Client seeds→Palette ramps→Semantic tokens→Components

For this installation, THeme/UnionSuite is the shared core and THeme/UnionSuite-Client contains Branding.css and Override.css. In Theme-Config.html, choose your colours, then copy Your branding CSS into the entire contents of Branding.css. Clients may also edit Override.css for custom CSS rules that override the base theme; keep these rules separate from generated branding.

Apply your branding in iMIS: open Staff site → Theme Builder → Themes, select UnionSuite-Client and click Download. Extract the ZIP, replace and save Branding.css, and optionally edit Override.css. Zip the files again with the original folder structure. Back in Themes, select UnionSuite-Client → Edit, choose the ZIP under Upload new version and click Save. Refresh your site to check the result. Open the illustrated step-by-step instructions. The selected client theme must already be connected to the site's core theme.

Keep semantic aliases such as --brand-800 and --text-link connected to the palette for a normal rebrand. To pin one exact shade, override its primitive token, for example --teal-500. Despite the historic name, the --teal-* ramp follows the chosen primary colour.

Edit and preview the five seeds

Choose a colour or enter a hex value. Changes immediately update this page and every component preview, including the combined example below. Copy or download the CSS to apply it to a site.

Source colours · preview changes are not saved
Copy or download the seed CSS Apply in UnionSuite-Client/Branding.css

:root {
  --seed-primary: #006f94;
  --seed-primary-light: #5acbf8;
  --seed-accent: #ff9947;
  --seed-accent-alt: #ff734f;
  --seed-neutral: #545962;
}
All theme componentsScroll inside to see the banner condense and inspect the report and palette
{{BRAND_DEMO}}

Preview colours follow the source snapshot used to build this guide. Rebuild after changing client overrides. Existing pinned palette stops still take precedence over seeds.

How to update the brand

  1. Use Theme-Config.html to generate branding CSS, or edit the :root seed block in UnionSuite-Client/Branding.css. Keep separately maintained client rules in UnionSuite-Client/Override.css.
  2. Update the existing a.logo rule with the client's image URL, width and height. The logo is an image asset, not a colour token.
  3. Keep status colours deliberate. Success, warning, danger and info are independently defined, so changing the brand does not change their meaning.
  4. Check text, links, focus rings, status badges and hover states on their actual backgrounds. Seed derivation does not guarantee accessible contrast for an arbitrary palette.
  5. Deploy the changed client stylesheet, refresh cached assets, verify the rendered site, and rebuild this guide.

Browsers supporting color-mix() calculate the ramps from the seeds. Older browsers use the predefined Orion palette. The current client file has a logo rule and no active colour overrides.

Tokens affect consumers of those tokens. Current reports, banners and the documented native action buttons use them. Other legacy or native rules with hard-coded colours do not automatically rebrand merely because the tokens exist.

Standard page-layout spacing

Implemented automatically in 99-Orion.css. Standard RiSE rows arranging iPart zones use a 24px horizontal gutter across the site. No page, zone or iPart class needs to be added, and existing template HTML stays the same. Upload the updated foundation stylesheet to each theme used by these pages, including content pages loaded inside iframes.

{{PAGE_LAYOUT_DEMO}}

The example uses the actual theme and native zone wrappers. Its page columns use 24px; the form row within the first card retains its 40px default. At the native col-sm-* breakpoint, columns stack below 768px.

ScopeBehaviour
Page and native CCO layoutsRows directly under a layout div within ContentPanel, ContentWizardDisplay or EmptyMasterContentPanel, with columns that own ContentItemContainer > WebPartZone, use 24px. This includes empty zones and iParts with configured or empty class wrappers.
PopupsThe popup layout (EmptyMasterContentPanel) has no padding round its layout row to take the row's negative margins, which made popups scroll sideways. There, a layout row of one column has no gutter, so its content sits flush as before; a row of several columns keeps the gaps between them and stays inside the popup.
Theme labelsThe vendor sheet gives every label padding-left: 7px; margin-left: -7px, which hangs the label's box 7px to the left. Labels carrying a theme us-* class (switches, drop zones, checkbox rows, form labels) drop the offset in zUnionSuite.css; native labels are unchanged.
Empty iPartsAn iPart with nothing to show (the case page's Resolution on an open case) still renders an empty ContentItemContainer in its zone. Its native 0.9em bottom margin collapses through the empty box and the zone, pushing the whole column about 14px below its neighbours. zUnionSuite.css hides a WebPartZone > ContentItemContainer that is completely empty, so columns start level. The page editor (Easy Edit) keeps it, so the iPart can still be edited. A container with any content, even whitespace, is left alone.
Forms, filters and component gridsRows without that layout/zone structure keep their existing spacing. Each nested row defines its own gutter, so it does not inherit 24px merely because its parent page uses it. Card body padding and vertical spacing between iParts are unchanged.
Full-width banner pagesThe banner remains full-width and uses the containing row's half-gutter for its text inset: 12px with this layout. Text stays aligned with content below; sticky positioning still follows the banner's measured bounds.
Overrides and other layoutsCustom layouts without the native zone chain retain their existing gutter. An inline gutter on a row or a deliberate later rule with sufficient specificity can override it. us-report-no-styling on the layout row or an ancestor skips this adjustment. Parent-page CSS cannot style an iframe document.

This change retains the native negative-margin and column-padding balance. Unpadded iframe shells still need their own outer-row margin correction; reducing the gutter does not replace that correction.

Common token choices

UseToken(s)Notes
Banner gradient--brand-700 → --brand-800Lighter on the left beside the navigation, darkening toward the right.
Banner accent & left separator--accent; --brand-6002px top line and a subtle 1px inset left separator.
Ordinary & accent buttons--accent, --accent-hover, --text-on-accentExisting TextButton/btn controls update without adding classes. Recheck text contrast after a rebrand.
Surfaces & readable text--bg-surface, --text-strong, --text-base, --text-inverseUse the matching text colour for each surface.
Feedback--success, --warning, --danger, --info and their -bg tokensPair colours with a text label; don't communicate status by colour alone.
Type, shape, spacing--font-*, --fs-*, --space-*, --radius*, --shadow*These are defined tokens, not derived from the brand seeds.
Page gutterNative --bs-gutter-x on .rowStandard page-layout rows use 24px: 12px on each column side. Other rows retain the 40px foundation default or their existing component rules. Banner layout consumes its row's actual gutter; --page-gutter does not drive the banner breakout. See scope and example.

Definitions come from the shared theme; resolved values include active client root overrides and temporary preview seeds. Local fonts are used when the named web fonts are unavailable offline. Layout tokens describe defaults; the native header and sidebar geometry remain authoritative for sticky banners.

Banner component aliases Override on .us-banner
{{BANNER_TOKENS}}
AliasDefault

For a separately maintained component override, target .us-banner in UnionSuite-Client/Override.css, loaded after the shared theme and generated branding. The defaults use a low-specificity :where(.us-banner) selector. Recheck both gradient endpoints against text and keep --banner-accent and --banner-on-accent readable together. Remove obsolete inline banner CSS so it cannot compete with the theme.

IQA component aliases Override on an IQA iPart

Examples: --iqa-inset for card padding; --iqa-expanded-inset for expanded text insets. Component settings do not change native page gutters.

04 / QUICK REFERENCE

Which class goes where?

CSS selectors include a dot in code. iMIS class fields contain space-separated names without dots.

ClassApply toEffect / requirement
us-action-home-add-taskQuery Menu or Query Template Display iPart CSS class fieldClient-defined task action hook. Register home.add-task in Actions.js with the verified editor and assignee context. The guide preview supplies a sample definition.
us-query-searchQuery Template Display iPart CSS class fieldReusable search of the currently displayed results, with a header funnel. Works with notes, tasks or other templates; requires a Title and shared CSS/JS.
us-home-tasksTask Query Template Display iPart CSS class field, in place of the three classes it bundlesSection preset. Applies us-query-search, us-task-completed-filter and us-action-home-add-task from one class, for panels whose CSS class field would otherwise exceed the 100-character limit. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS. Also enables the Biscuit empty state.
us-contact-methodsContact methods Query Menu iPart CSS class field, in place of the two classes it bundlesSection preset. Applies ContactDetailsIQA and us-action-member-add-contact-method from one class: an Add contact method header button, and the marker a banner or menu Add contact method uses to find this report to refresh. Requires shared JS and ActionDefinitions.js.
us-case-tasksCase page Tasks Query Template Display iPart CSS class field, in place of the classes it bundlesSection preset. Applies us-query-template, us-query-search, us-task-completed-filter, us-task-progress, us-list-scroll and us-action-cases-add-task. Use the task rows. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS; Add task needs the site's cases.add-task action.
us-case-meetingsCase page Overview Meetings Query Template Display iPart CSS class fieldSection preset. Applies us-query-template, us-meetings, us-task-completed-filter, us-task-progress, us-list-scroll and us-action-cases-add-meeting: the meeting rows, past meetings behind Show past meetings. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS; Add meeting needs the site's cases.add-meeting action.
us-case-meetings-detailCase page Activity tab Meetings Query Template Display iPart CSS class fieldSection preset. Applies us-query-template, us-meetings, us-query-search, us-query-search-open, us-task-completed-filter, us-task-progress, us-action-cases-add-meeting, us-activity-detail, us-activity-detail-meetings, us-tab-panel, us-tabset-activity and us-tab-all: the detailed meeting rows as a Details view of All activity, with the search open. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS.
us-case-notesCase page Activity tab Notes Query Template Display iPart CSS class field, placed right under All activitySection preset. Applies us-query-template, us-notes, us-notes--ledger, us-query-search, us-query-search-open, us-action-cases-add-note, us-activity-detail, us-activity-detail-notes, us-tab-panel, us-tabset-activity and us-tab-all: the notes ledger as All activity's Notes Details view (its Notes source carries data-history="#notes"), with the search open; the ledger runs to its last note and the page scrolls. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS; Add note needs the site's cases.add-note action.
us-case-changesCase page Activity tab Changes Query Template Display iPart CSS class fieldSection preset. Applies us-tab-panel, us-tabset-activity, us-tab-changes, us-query-template, us-records, us-query-search and us-details-view-history: the change history in the Activity switcher, with the History Query Menu as its Details view. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS.
us-details-view-keyCSS class field of a list iPart (a Query Template Display), with a detailed iPart marked us-activity-detail us-activity-detail-key right under itA Details view toggle in the list's heading; pressed, the detailed iPart (usually a native IQA report, for its Export) takes the list's place in the same card. No detailed iPart, no toggle. Setup.
us-case-attachmentsCase page Attachments tab Query Template Display iPart CSS class fieldSection preset. Applies us-query-template, us-attachments, us-query-search, us-query-search-open, us-list-scroll and us-action-cases-add-attachment: the attachment rows with the search open. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS; Upload needs the site's cases.add-attachment action.
us-case-attachments-recentCase page Overview Attachments Query Template Display iPart CSS class fieldSection preset. Applies us-query-template, us-attachments and us-action-cases-add-attachment: the newest few attachment rows with Upload in the heading, no search. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS.
us-case-staffCase page Staff Query Template Display iPart CSS class fieldSection preset. Applies us-query-template, us-contacts-tiles, us-contacts-grouped, us-contacts-group-tone, us-contacts-lead-first and us-action-cases-add-staff: the case team as grouped contact tiles, one per row, the lead staff member first, with the lead staff crowns. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS; Add staff needs the site's cases.add-staff action.
us-query-search-openQuery Template Display iPart CSS class field, alongside us-query-searchStarts the list's filters open instead of closed. The panel then remembers whether they were left open or closed. Requires shared JS. Visual.
us-meeting--fullAlongside us-meeting in a meetings Query TemplateDetailed meeting row: who attends, the agenda or notes up to three lines, and the outcome. Blank parts collapse. Requires shared CSS. Visual.
us-placeholder--compactAlongside us-placeholder, in a list's No results fieldA short placeholder for an empty list, with the list's add button under it. Requires shared CSS. Visual.
data-us-attachment-accessA us-attachment row in an attachments Query Template"restricted" hides the row's actions behind Unlock, which runs the check the site defines with UnionSuiteRestrictedAttachments.defineCheck. Requires shared CSS and JS. Visual.
data-us-case-memberThe outer div of the case member card Query TemplateWhen the member has a job (from the jobs IQA), the Work label opens a menu to choose which of the member's jobs (current, not started or former) the case is about, or none (data-us-workplace-id: a job's workplace ID, or blank for not about a workplace; data-us-member-id; optional data-us-workplace-group). Requires shared CSS and JS; the save needs Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js. Visual.
us-task-completed-filterTask Query Template Display iPart CSS class field, alone or alongside us-query-searchIndependent Show completed toggle. Reads the explicit data-us-task-completed value in each task result; hides completed tasks initially. Requires a Title and shared CSS/JS.
home-welcomeAuthored header inside Content HTML or a Query Template resultShared welcome heading and subtitle typography with responsive alignment. Requires shared CSS only.
home-welcome-messageQuery Template Display iPart CSS class fieldRemoves the immediate panel surface, border, corners, shadow and spacing while retaining welcome typography. Leave Title blank and Display in cards unchecked.
home-welcome-dateOptional date span directly inside the authored home-welcome headerMuted right-aligned date; hidden at viewport widths of 900px or below. The template supplies the date text.
us-optional-fieldA field's column div in a read-only template (PanelEditorReadOnlyForm markup in a Query Template Display)Hides the whole field when its value comes back empty, for example no active adjustment. Templates have no conditionals; the value counts as empty when its only element has no content, so keep {#query.Field} directly inside it with no spaces.

05 / BANNERS

A shared identity for every page.

Agreement, Contact, Staff and dashboard banners use the same slots, theme and behaviour. Only their content and data source change.

ModeBanner iPart CSS classOn scroll
Normalus-bannerExpanded banner scrolls away with the page.
Stickyus-banner us-banner-stickyAll details remain visible.
Condensingus-banner us-banner-collapsibleSticky banner animates into a compact summary.
  1. Choose Content HTML for literal dashboard text, or Query Template Display for the current record. A record banner must return one result and one banner surface.
  2. Paste a banner HTML template into the content/template field. Replace placeholders using the fields configured in that IQA. Bracketed labels in our templates are author placeholders, not executable IQA syntax.
  3. Set the banner iPart's CSS classes from the table. Leave its native title and description blank and its native border disabled.
  4. For full width, add us-banner-page to the page CSS class and use a top-level full-width col-sm-12 layout column. Other sections retain their native padding automatically.
  5. Load updated zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js once through the site template. Apply banner classes to the visible content iPart. Retire old standalone banner includes after verifying the shared replacement.

This self-contained example uses the current banner CSS and JS. Its minimal sample layout does not run iMIS or Telerik. The example has its own scroll area so the guide's navigation stays usable.

Avatar, statuses, facts and a primary action

{{BANNER_PARTS}}

These are existing banner slots. Copy their classes from the class reference into the matching inner template elements. The complete banner templates below show their required structure; semantic badge modifiers go alongside us-banner__badge.

HTML for these banner parts Content HTML source or Query Template Display template

This is a complete inner header. Replace its example values and use the same banner iPart classes as above. Keep only the appropriate status badge; the preview displays all four to compare their styling.

{{BANNER_PARTS_TEMPLATE}}

What stays, and what folds

The record type/ID, title, status, optional Actions and navigation stay visible. Subtitle and details fold. Avatars and titles become smaller. After 60px of window scroll, the banner condenses over 260ms; within 16px of the top, it expands again. These separate thresholds prevent flickering.

The script pins the original surface below the visible native #hd header and follows the iPart's actual width and sidebar geometry. The original nodes stay in their form. A placeholder follows the animated height, so condensing does not leave a tall blank space.

Only the first visible eligible banner is enhanced. Details containing keyboard focus stay open until focus leaves; folded content becomes inert. Reduced motion removes animation. Easy Edit keeps the banner expanded in normal flow. Very short pages stay expanded to avoid scroll-height oscillation; pinning stops when less than 140px of usable viewport height remains.

Full width without wrapping every section

Banner primary actions and action-menu toggles reverse to --bg-surface with --text-strong text and an accent border on hover or focus. Pressed states use --accent-100 and inset shading; keyboard focus retains a contrasting outline.

us-banner-page resets .main padding, then adds a 5px inline-start gap showing --bg-page at viewport widths of 768px and above. This shared page inset separates every banner type from the sidebar and moves the content beneath it together, preserving alignment and native sidebar offsets. Below 768px the extra gap is removed. Contained banners without this page class retain their existing layout. It clears the outer layout rows' negative side margins and lets native columns provide normal content insets. The banner alone extends through its full-width column's half-gutter. Nested rows retain their native balance. No us-page-content wrappers or 100vw widths are needed.

The breakout is scoped to the wrapper chain verified in the captured pages. Nested or narrower-column banners remain contained. Sticky behaviour is independent of that layout adapter and can operate without us-banner-page.

Optional Actions

Status badges and Actions align on the same vertical centre when they share a summary row, in both expanded and compact modes. Narrow layouts can wrap without adding another wrapper or iPart class.

Keep div.us-banner__actions inside the summary to show the dropdown. Delete the entire div to omit it. The native details/summary disclosure supports ordinary Tab navigation. Shared JS closes it on outside click, Escape, focus leaving or action activation, and positions the dropdown within the viewport.

Sample commands are disabled. For navigation, use an a.us-banner__menu-item with a verified URL. For a command, keep button type="button", connect a verified handler in shared JavaScript, then remove disabled. Use shared event listeners rather than inline onclick attributes. The planned business-action registry is not required to author an ordinary working link.

Optional tabs

Keep div.us-banner__nav after details to display the tabs row. Delete that div to omit it. The row stays visible in compact mode and scrolls horizontally on narrow screens. Add, remove or reorder buttons and keep their keys stable.

Section switching is now available. Connect banner or standalone tabs to explicitly marked content using the page-sections adapter. The generic template keeps its placeholders disabled until you provide matching panels. Automatic native zone discovery and a CCO loading adapter remain separate future work.
06 / IQA REPORTS

Enhance the native report.

Apply classes to a Query Menu iPart. Continue using its native query, title, description, access, filtering, sorting and paging settings.

Dummy IQA with example dataHover or focus Member, Branch or Status to see the sort highlight and arrow; click to sort. Also try Find, paging, Export, Filters and Expand.
{{REPORT_DEMO}}

Native IQA — full report styling by default

Install zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js once through the shared site template. No author class is needed: supported native Query Menu iParts receive the full report card, panel header/description, responsive filters, styled multiselect chips, table headings, row spacing, sort affordances and pager, plus a Filters icon when visible native filter fields exist, and a download icon when an existing native Export menu exists. Filters start open. The adapter retains native Export nodes, handlers and form ownership; it does not create missing filters or export commands. Untitled reports receive a compact utility row.

Colours: the panel title bar matches the white card (--iqa-panel-header, which defaults to --iqa-surface) with an --iqa-border rule below it, so the card stands apart from the grey page. Column headings take the light --iqa-table-head tint (--bg-subtle). The title outranks the headings by type, not by fill. A panel tone (us-panel-tone-*) tints the title bar instead.

The former us-report filter/table layout is now the default. Supported FilterPanel fields use the responsive grid; the existing horizontal-filter adapter retains its supported native arrangement. Grid colours, uniform row surfaces, hover/selection, pager, empty-result appearance, ordinary filter borders/focus, accent Find and native loading feedback continue to use shared theme tokens. The query selector and report body use coordinated card insets. ReportCollection is not required. Detection requires the captured ContentPanel → ListerPanel → data-gridid structure and one owned RadGrid; unrelated grids and containing CCOs are not promoted.

Cell wrapping: result cells wrap between words (overflow-wrap: break-word). A column is never narrower than its longest word or button label, so a wide result scrolls inside the report instead of stacking a button such as Review one letter per line. A single over-long value, such as an email address, still breaks once its column is sized. Upload the updated zUnionSuite.css; no class or query change is needed.

{{NATIVE_REPORT_DEMO}}

Before the first Find: The native .ListSearchPrompt directly inside [data-gridid] uses --iqa-inset (18px by default) for horizontal padding, aligning its text with the query selector and filter fields. This foundation rule is in 99-Orion.css; upload the updated foundation stylesheet. No additional class or markup is needed. Native prompt visibility and vertical spacing are retained; us-report-no-styling opts out. native Query Menu searches also receive the shell and filter toggle while displaying their initial search prompt without a RadGrid. Detection requires the same ContentPanel → ListerPanel → data-gridid chain, a direct native DataSourcePanel with ParametersContainer, and a direct ListSearchPrompt ending in _HideResultsMessagePanel. The immediate panel owner is styled for both direct and CSS-class wrappers. Export remains conditional on a native export control; Expand still requires a supported results table. Update zUnionSuite.js for this enhancement; no author class or automatic search is needed. Existing no-styling exclusions and partial-refresh handling apply.

Native IQA with optional actions and Expand

This preview adds us-action-demo-add-note us-action-demo-view-history us-report-expandable to the Query Menu iPart CSS class field. Click Add note or View history for a local demonstration, or Expand to fill the preview frame. Demo definitions are local; production handlers belong in client Actions.js. Generated IDs are unique per instance.

{{NATIVE_ACTION_REPORT_DEMO}}

Where to paste: copy a complete recipe below into the Query Menu iPart → CSS class field. Configure the real query and its columns in iMIS. The fixture uses the captured wrapper/FilterPanel/RadGrid structure; its local data simulation is confined to this guide.

RecipeiPart CSS class field

The adapter provides the title/description treatment, native query-folder selector, responsive filters, multiselect chips, rows, sort affordances, pager and page-size control. All supported native reports receive this same styling automatically. Filters appears when native fields exist and Export when the native menu exists. us-report is now a compatibility class with no additional visual effect; Expand and custom actions remain opt-in.

Utility button states: press and hold Filters, Download or Expand to see the depressed inset shading. Open Filters/Export (aria-expanded=true) and expanded view (aria-pressed=true) retain a stronger selected background, a subtle tinted border and lighter inset shading. Selected colour uses --iqa-selected, with border and pressed tint derived from --iqa-link. Releasing returns to the current selected or normal state. Keyboard focus remains visible; disabled controls do not receive the pressed treatment. No extra class is needed; us-report-no-styling opts out.

Show all: the native pager's Show all N control returns an empty grid in iMIS itself, with or without the theme. UnionSuiteGridShowAll (version 1.0) takes that click on every RadGrid and sets the pager's table view to a page size of N instead, the same native command as the page-size dropdown, so every row loads in place. With one page, Telerik hides the pager and page-size control as a working Show all would; reload the page to return to paging. If the grid, its table view or the count cannot be found, the native click runs unchanged. No author class is needed.

Resizable columns and readable IDs

Expandable result rows

The native column is an expand/collapse control column. Its heading stays visually blank: the theme preserves Orion's hidden header label instead of applying data-heading typography to it. The header cell and its accessible name remain in the table, keeping all column positions aligned. The column adapter (version 1.2) skips this column's resize handle, minimum width and width overrides; row expand/collapse buttons retain their native IDs, names, state and handlers. Ordinary data columns remain resizable. No author class or iPart setting is needed.

{{IQA_EXPAND_DEMO}}

Row loading: when ASP.NET accepts an expand or collapse request from a native rgExpand/rgCollapse input or button in td.rgExpandCol, UnionSuiteIqaBusy (version 3.3) puts a compact shared spinner in place of that arrow, sized to the control within the expand column, for the length of the request. The row is not faded, so the spinner stays where the click was even on a wide report. The control keeps its focus, ID, name, handlers and disabled state; only its glyph is hidden, and the spinner catches a second click. Only that report's native _UpdateProgress1 indicator is suppressed, including while the row scrolls offscreen. Find and sorting retain their results-area loading feedback. A cancelled click does not start loading.

Sliding details: iMIS renders detail rows on the server; the grid leaves Telerik's hierarchy loading at its server default, so client-side expansion is not available and every expand or collapse is a partial update that replaces the whole grid. The theme keeps the report in place across that update. Before the replaced grid is first painted, the new detail row (the <tr> after the master row, holding table.rgDetailTable) is closed, detail rows are held out of column fitting so column widths do not shift, the page and grid scroll positions are restored, and focus returns to the replacement arrow if it was on the clicked one. The detail row then slides open with the shared fold (220ms). On collapse, an inert copy of the old detail row, without IDs or names, slides closed in its place. Other open detail rows are left as they are.

The spinner follows scrolling and clipping, and moves to the replacement arrow in a replacement row with the same native ID inside the same grid. It sets aria-busy on that row, announces “Loading row details”, and restores existing attributes and indicators on endRequest, including failure, or page navigation. Reduced-motion preferences remove the spinner's fade and rotation and open or close the detail row at once. Colours use the shared spinner's brand/accent tokens. Native postback handlers and disabled state are preserved.

This example uses the captured MetroTouch header/control structure and two hidden query columns. Its short request delay, grid replacement and detail content are simulated only in the guide; loading and sliding use production theme code. Live requests, permissions and content remain with iMIS. Row loading requires the supported _ContentPanel → data-gridid structure and a recognised ASP.NET request; unsupported controls or full-page requests retain native loading. The column-resizing adapter separately skips unsupported spanning data rows or split tables. Upload zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js together and reload the page. No new author class, query template or iPart setting is needed. us-report-no-styling retains native loading, updates the grid natively without the slide, and disables shared column enhancements. The slide has been checked against this simulation; a live iMIS grid still needs checking. Open the standalone example.

Native iMIS output — identification only; do not replace the generated controls
<th class="rgHeader rgExpandCol" scope="col" aria-label="ExpandColumn">Expand Collapse</th>
<!-- Corresponding result cell; preserve its generated ID/name/handlers. -->
<td class="rgExpandCol" aria-expanded="false">
  <input type="submit" class="rgExpand" title="Expand" aria-expanded="false" value=" ">
</td>
<!-- After an expand, the server adds a detail row below the master row. -->
<tr>
  <td class="rgExpandCol">&nbsp;</td>
  <td colspan="5"><table class="rgDetailTable">…</table></td>
</tr>

Data columns

These are automatic improvements with shared CSS/JS; no extra class is needed. Drag the right edge of a column heading to resize it. The handle uses a horizontal double-arrow cursor; hover, keyboard focus and dragging highlight a thin divider through the table instead of a box around the handle. Keyboard users can Tab to a resize separator and use Left/Right arrows (10px), Shift+arrow (40px), or Home to fit the column's visible contents. The separator is independent of the native sort link. Widths are remembered in memory for this page/report/query/header set across partial updates, and reset on a full page reload. When the table is wider than its container it scrolls horizontally.

Headings containing the standalone word ID or IDs, including Member ID, Legacy ID and Party ID, are detected case-insensitively; “Paid” and “Identity” are not ID columns. Those headers and their visible result cells stay on one line. The minimum column width fits the longest currently rendered value or heading, including padding. Each page of results is measured again; unseen server-side records cannot be measured in advance. ID columns cannot be resized below this readable minimum.

The example includes a Member ID column with differing identifier lengths. Try narrowing it, then resize Branch. The adapter preserves native sorting, IDs, links and postback handlers, and restores modified widths before native partial replacement. It supports visible single-header, single-table grids without spanning result cells; split/virtualized layouts, incompatible colgroups and recognised native resize handles are left alone. Hidden columns are supported: they keep their native indexes and visibility, receive no resize handles or width overrides, and do not block visible columns. Visible ID cells still map to the correct headers and colgroup entries. Tables in hidden panels initialize when they become measurable. The shared column adapter reports version 1.1 or later for this fix. us-report-no-styling disables these improvements. Native pager dropdowns and resizing alongside deployed Telerik configurations still require live verification.

Native query setup — automatic ID recognition
<!-- Configure the IQA column title as ID, Member ID, Legacy ID, Party ID, etc.
     iMIS generates the table; no custom class is needed on its cells. -->
<th class="rgHeader">Member ID</th>
<td><a href="/member/100002">100002</a></td>

Native date-range filters

Query-only reports without a rendered filter panel remove the selector divider. Empty result toolbars collapse even when there is no Export control, so the selector meets the column headings directly. Toolbars with text or controls stay visible.

Report cards reserve a small bottom inset (the inner --iqa-radius, 11px by default) below results or paging so native square surfaces cannot cover the rounded bottom border. Dropdown overflow stays visible. Full-screen reports have square corners and no bottom inset.

FinderAdder popup filters also receive contained gutters and a 12px/18px inset inside EmptyMasterContentPanel, even without a report wrapper or _ParametersContainer ID. Labels have 6px clearance for focus outlines. Upload shared CSS to the theme loaded by the popup iframe; parent-page CSS cannot style its contents. Native scrolling, selection and Cancel remain unchanged. us-report-no-styling opts out.

Directory IQAs using FilterPanelHorizontal receive the same panel inset as other IQA filters. Their native Bootstrap column counts and responsive stacking are retained; balanced 16px gutters keep controls inside the panel, including rows with native pr-3 and pr-0 utilities. This is automatic with shared CSS; us-report-no-styling opts out.

The sample Join Date Between field uses the supplied native GridFilterCalendar → CalendarInput → StylesDateText / image-button structure. Shared CSS gives each date an integrated calendar-button area and aligns the range label with ordinary filter labels. Below a 340px field width, dates are aligned with the label on two rows, with “and” immediately after the first date; at 340px and above, both dates and “and” share one row. This follows the field's available width, including horizontal filters. Without container-query support, the stacked layout remains usable.

No extra class or JavaScript is needed in iMIS. Keep native date format/conversion, input IDs, calendar image controls, screen-reader labels and validation spans. The theme overrides the inline 7em input width only inside this native date-range structure; validation can flow below its field. Colours use --iqa-surface, --iqa-header, --iqa-input-border, --iqa-border, --iqa-muted, --iqa-link and --iqa-selected. us-report-no-styling opts out. The calendar button uses the same outlined glyph and neutral surface as the approved form date input. Legacy ASP.NET CalendarBehavior popups (ajax__calendar classes) now share the themed surface, title/Today raised controls, navigation hover/press, muted other-month dates and filled selected day/month/year styling. Native geometry, visibility and event handlers remain in control. The offline calendar button opens an appearance-only sample using these shared classes; it does not implement native navigation or date filtering. Popups rendered outside the iPart retain global shared calendar styling even when the iPart opts out. Verify deployed calendar opening and date validation in iMIS.

Native structure reference — keep iMIS-generated IDs and handlers
<div class="PanelField">
  <span class="Label">Join Date Between</span>
  <div class="PanelFieldValue">
    <table class="GridFilterCalendar"><tbody><tr>
      <td><!-- Native CalendarInput, start date and its label/validators --></td>
      <td><span class="PanelFieldLabelFont AddPaddingLeft AddPaddingRight">and</span></td>
      <td><!-- Native CalendarInput, end date and its label/validators --></td>
    </tr></tbody></table>
  </div>
</div>

Filter behaviour

Filter disclosure animates over 180ms. Without us-filters-collapsed, whether the filters are open is remembered per page, report and query in browser storage shared by every tab, so a refresh, a CCO tab switch or a record opened in a new tab keeps it. Filter values are never stored. us-filters-collapsed overrides that choice and starts closed on initialization and each reconciliation, including partial refreshes. It does not overwrite the saved normal preference. The user can still open filters until the next reconciliation. Validation can reopen filters, and reduced motion skips the transition.

Expanded view

Expand creates a full-window report with pinned title/filters, sticky column headings, scrolling data and native pagination. The column adapter (version 1.3) distributes spare fullscreen width across visible data columns on the first opening and when the window grows. Hidden columns and native expand/collapse utility widths are preserved. Wider reports keep horizontal scrolling. Fullscreen resizing is kept separately from normal panel widths; Restore and Escape return to those widths and the captured page position. Width choices last for this page session, including native partial updates, and reset on a full reload. The 240ms transition respects reduced motion. Native IDs, controls, handlers and form ownership are retained, and moved nodes are restored around ASP.NET partial updates. Update zUnionSuite.js and reload the page to receive the sizing fix; no IQA or query template changes are needed.

This supports the captured single-table RadGrid structure. Split or virtualized grids receive a disabled Expand control. Report classes do not turn Query Template Displays, trackers, banners or groups of unrelated iParts into supported reports.

Unified actions: configure once, use in any placement

Implemented in the shared theme. UnionSuiteActions.define() connects one definition to repeated report-row controls, an iPart heading, a standalone button/link or a menu item. Every instance resolves its own context. The visual uses production controls and execution code; its records, native popup and ASP.NET responses are simulated. Open the standalone working example.

{{UNIFIED_ACTION_DEMO}}

Installation and ownership

Upload updated zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css together, plus Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js, and bump the loader's RELEASE. The theme loader runs them in order: zUnionSuite.js, then ActionDefinitions.js. Keep native dependencies and referenced business helpers loaded before registrations. The client Actions.js is not part of the loader yet, so its registrations wait for the loader. Do not put script includes in replaceable iPart content. General settings remain in Config.js.

Client registrations — wait for the theme loader
<!-- In the client Actions.js, around its registrations. -->
window.UnionSuiteLoader.ready.then(function () {
  // UnionSuiteActions.define(...) calls go here.
});
FileResponsibility
zUnionSuite.jsShared registry, validation, rendering, conflict diagnostics, popup execution and native refresh queue.
Scripts/ActionDefinitions.jsFifteen standard definitions, including Jobs Edit/Delete and Add Contact Method. Add Job now has one definition shared by header and menu placements. Also the agreement page set: 28 agreements.* definitions, the CloudToolz call and the agreement task saver. And the case page set: 26 cases.* definitions (six composers, each with its full form), the case task saver and the case access check: on /Cases_ManageCase or a page with the page CSS class us-case-page, CloudToolz POST /cases/case-access with {"CaseOrdinal"} (the CaseNum) answering {"Access": "Restricted", "MemberID"} replaces the page with /UTNewTheme/Cases_NoAccess?CaseID=&CaseNum=&ID= (noAccessPage), whose us-action-cases-back-to-member button opens /UTNewTheme/Party.aspx?ID=; a failed check stays on the page. While it runs, US-CASE-ACCESS in zUnionSuite.css hides the page's .main; granted or failed fades it in, and with no answer it shows after 15 seconds. Their popup pages and CloudToolz endpoints are proposed.
UnionSuite-Client/Actions.jsYour new definitions and explicit overrides. The supplied file is a ready-to-load empty client extension point.

Single-pass conversion: the old report registerAction/configureAction methods, menu register method, us-report-button/link/icon-* action classes and data-us-action/data-case-action command dispatch are retired. Convert their callers; no aliases are installed. Native filter/search/expand classes and structural menu classes still apply. Remove previous inline/delegated handlers from converted buttons. The conversion checklist identifies the exact replacements and remaining CMS work.

Where to put the action class

PlacementAuthoring instructions
iPart headingPut the action class in the iPart CSS class field. iMIS inserts an inner div around its output; the theme uses the component's own action slot. Keep a nonempty Title for Query Template Display. Native untitled IQAs can use their utility row.
Standalone button or linkPut the class on the actual button/anchor. Use type="button" on a button. Supply initial visible text; registration supplies presentation and accessible naming.
Menu itemPut the class on the item button/anchor alongside us-actions__item. The menu controller handles disclosure; the action registry handles the command. Add data-us-action-icon (plus, pencil, trash or one Tabler class such as ti-notes) to give this one control its own icon in place of the definition's; it works in any placement, and the action's other controls keep theirs.
Report rowPut the class and record attributes on each row's button. Reuse the class; give each row its real record key. No DOM ID or inline onclick is needed for dispatch.

Examples: , , , . Standard definitions come from ActionDefinitions.js. is a client integration hook: the live task editor and assignee contract have not been supplied, so it remains unconfigured until you define it.

Jobs row controls — literal sample values; adapt your existing SQL output
<button type="button" class="us-action-jobs-edit"
        data-id="103885" data-seqn="22" data-workplace="103842">Edit job</button>
<button type="button" class="us-action-jobs-delete"
        data-id="103885" data-seqn="22">Delete job</button>
AttributeValue / blank behaviour
data-idRequired member/party ID, e.g. 103885; mapped to context.partyId. Use the actual stored value and retain leading zeroes.
data-seqnRequired stored job ordinal, e.g. 22; mapped to context.ordinal. Do not use a display row number or changing ROW_NUMBER value.
data-workplaceRequired for Edit, e.g. 103842; mapped to context.workplaceId. Delete does not read it. Missing/blank disables Edit only.

These are literal HTML examples, not IQA substitution syntax. Emit and HTML-encode values from your existing SQL-generated Edit/Delete columns, retaining the existing rule deciding which rows offer Delete. If adapted to Query Template syntax, every referenced field must be selected in the IQA, including attributes and optional blank fields. The live Jobs SQL definition has not been supplied.

Create a registration: use the guide’s interactive action builder for popup, navigation and custom-function actions. It includes immediate button styling feedback, simulated operations, refresh settings and copyable JavaScript/author HTML. Custom helpers/providers still need to be supplied. The standalone builder uses the same form.

Complete registration shape

Keep identity metadata outside the three sections: presentation, context and action. The function below is a callable registration template: pass your real popup URL builder and optional refresh callback. It does not install a fictional editor. The default refresh targets the originating native report.

Client Actions.js — popup registration template
function registerRecordEditor(buildEditorUrl, refreshViews) {
  return UnionSuiteActions.define('records.edit', {
    className: 'us-action-records-edit',
    owner: 'client.records',
    source: 'Actions.js:records.edit',
    presentation: {
      label: 'Edit record', icon: 'pencil', tone: 'default', order: 0,
      default: 'button', header: 'button', row: 'icon', menu: 'menu-item'
    },
    context: {
      recordId: { from: 'trigger', attribute: 'data-id', required: true }
    },
    action: {
      type: 'popup', disabled: false, recordKey: ['recordId'],
      href: buildEditorUrl,
      popup: { title: 'Edit record', width: '80%', height: '90%' },
      refresh: refreshViews
        ? { when: 'close', run: refreshViews }
        : { when: 'close', targets: [{ type: 'origin-report' }] }
    }
  });
}
OptionSupported values and defaults
IdentityRequired namespaced key, one className using lowercase us-action- words, and nonempty owner/source. Class-to-key mapping is explicit.
presentationRequired label; optional icon plus, pencil, trash or one installed ti-* glyph; tone default/danger; numeric order (default 0). default/header/row/menu select button, icon, link or menu-item. Unspecified placement uses default (button if omitted), except in a menu, where it is menu-item. Icon mode without a glyph retains a text label.
context.FIELDChoose an attribute source (from:'trigger', 'owner', or 'closest' with selector, plus data-* attribute), a named URL query source (from:'query', parameter:'ID'), a literal value, or resolve(env). required defaults false. Optional validate(value,env) returns true, false or an error message. Resolvers/validators may return promises. Values must be scalar; strings are trimmed. Missing required or unresolved query values disable the instance.
action.typefunction uses run(env); popup uses href plus popup options; navigate uses href and optional target _self/_blank. href may be a string or resolver. Use one operation per definition.
AvailabilityOptional disabled boolean; requires array of named function dependencies; eligible(env) returning true, false or a reason; access.check(env), or access.permission plus a provider installed with setAccessResolver(fn). Checks may be async. Only true grants access/eligibility. access.denied is disable (default) or hide. Failed/pending checks remain unavailable.
InvocationOptional recordKey array of context names or synchronous function; otherwise all context values identify the operation, falling back to the owner for empty context. Optional confirm:{message} or async-capable confirm(env); only true proceeds. Function actions may provide successMessage, shown in the action notice at the bottom left. A plain message such as this fades after 4 seconds, paused while the pointer or focus is on it; an error, or a refresh failure with Retry, stays until Dismiss is pressed or another message replaces it. Native navigation does not support automatic confirmation/refresh; use a function workflow for those.
action.refreshOptional; when is close for popup or success for function. Choose targets or run, never both. Details below. No refresh is implied when omitted.

Callback environment includes key/actionId, trigger/element, owner/wrapper, placement, origin identity and the resolved immutable context. Invocation adds the original event and refresh facade. Refresh callbacks also receive result. Popup callbacks receive dialog/closeEvent. Use trigger rather than event.currentTarget after awaiting. Inline HTML never contains executable configuration strings.

Required values are checked during enhancement and again before execution, including after asynchronous confirmation and popup URL resolution. Missing context disables only that instance and exposes a reason in its accessible name and tooltip. Context/access state is re-evaluated after relevant attributes or native content changes; call UnionSuiteActions.refresh() when an external resolver's state changes or a dependency loads later. The registry preserves native disabled controls. The signed-in actor, viewed member, case and task assignee remain separate inputs; server permissions must still enforce access.

The execution route

  1. Discover: identify the authored control or generated heading slot and its definition. Unconfigured/conflicted controls remain unavailable.
  2. Resolve and validate: read this instance's declared context; check required values, dependencies, eligibility and access.
  3. Claim: prevent duplicate activation for this action/record, including another visible instance.
  4. Confirm: ask only if configured. Cancellation releases the claim without executing or refreshing.
  5. Execute: recheck context/configuration; invoke exactly one operation. A function must return the promise for its actual work.
  6. Observe: wait for the function result or the native popup's close. Closing includes Cancel/X; it is not a universal saved signal.
  7. Refresh: await the target plan or custom callback after the configured event. Keep a successful operation distinct from a failed refresh.
  8. Finish: clear busy/record guards, preserve native focus behaviour and announce errors or configured success. A refresh-only retry never repeats the operation.

While an action runs, its control has data-us-command-state="busy" and aria-busy, and is disabled with “Action in progress” in its label. An icon-only action (us-icon-button) also swaps its icon for the theme's button spinner in the link colour until the action finishes, so a download or a loading preview shows that it is working.

Navigation anchors receive a prepared, validated href and retain native new-tab/modifier behaviour. Prepared resolver values remain cached until refresh/context change; for a workflow requiring asynchronous checks at every activation, use a function or popup action. Full native popup lifecycle guarantees apply to action.type=popup. A legacy function that opens a popup and immediately returns does not expose its later close/save result to the registry.

Native origin refresh and your custom callbacks

origin-report refreshes the action's own Query Menu or Query Template Display. Query Menu uses its native IQA Refresh control and waits for the accepted ASP.NET request. Query Template Display uses the HTML refresh described below. Generated heading actions retain their renderer owner; row actions retain their report identity. The service re-resolves the target after replacement, excludes nested iParts and rejects missing/ambiguous targets. Both refresh paths share one queue. UnionSuiteIqaRefresh.refresh() remains the older overlay attachment method; it does not execute a report refresh.

Refresh plan — attach under action.refresh
// Functions use when:'success'; native popups use when:'close'.
{
  when: 'success',
  targets: [
    { type: 'origin-report' },
    { type: 'iqa', selector: '.RelatedIQA', scope: 'page', match: 'all' },
    { type: 'custom', run: refreshMySummary }
  ]
}
Custom refresh callback — pass your own awaited element updater
function makeRefreshCallback(reloadSummary) {
  return async ({ context, refresh }) => {
    await refresh.originReport();
    await refresh.iqa('.RelatedIQA', { scope: 'page', match: 'all' });
    await reloadSummary(context.recordId);
  };
}
// action.refresh = { when:'close', run:makeRefreshCallback(yourUpdater) };

Targets run sequentially. IQA selectors default to one match within the origin owner; declare scope:'page' for page-wide matching, or pass an Element scope. Zero/ambiguous matches fail; match:'all' deliberately selects multiple reports. A plan deduplicates native reports and records completed targets. A failed declarative plan retries remaining work. A custom refresh callback is rerun in full on retry, so it must contain repeatable view updates only.

The native methods accept timeout (default 30000ms), startTimeout (1000ms) and an optional AbortSignal. Cancellation/timeout stops waiting; it does not cancel or safely repeat a server mutation. Public entry points are UnionSuiteRefresh.capture(trigger,owner?), forOrigin(origin), native(origin,options) and iqa(selector,{scope:'page',match:'one'}). A callback can await its own functions for multiple elements; a selector alone cannot tell the theme how to reload arbitrary native panels.

On operation-success/refresh-failure, visible feedback offers Retry refresh without rerunning the original function. A banner/menu may have no origin report and needs an explicit target. Real editor saves and native requests require verification in deployed iMIS; local tests simulate those responses.

Query Template Display refresh

Updated zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css support refreshing a server-rendered Query Template Display in place. Add task and Open task use this automatically through their existing origin-report close action. No additional iPart class or template markup is required. Keep Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js loaded. The example below uses a fictional local response with a short delay; all replacement and theme initialisation use the shared helper.

{{QUERY_TEMPLATE_REFRESH_DEMO}}

Personal tasks. The example's first row shows Personal Task because its member link's data-id matches the sample signed-in user's __ClientContext.loggedInPartyId. The detail task template supplies this with the optional MemberId alias. Put on a.us-task__member-name, not on the iPart wrapper. A matching link becomes non-clickable text: its href is removed, aria-disabled is true and tabindex is -1. Its hover underline and icon highlight are suppressed. Other IDs, blank IDs and missing/invalid or anonymous client context retain the authored member name and link. Comparison is by string, preserving leading zeroes. The label and disabled state are applied on initial render and query replacement; the original name, href and accessibility attributes are restored when the ID no longer matches. No-styling opts out. If site code changes the hidden context's value property directly, call UnionSuiteTaskRows.refresh() afterward.

Member link with personal-task label support
<a class="us-task__member-name" href="{#query.MemberUrl}" data-id="{#query.MemberId}">{#query.MemberName}</a>

Select MemberId, MemberUrl and MemberName in the IQA; the detail template's field table defines each. MemberId is the party represented by the member link, not necessarily the task completion record's TaskPartyId. If this feature is unused, select '' as MemberId or remove the entire data-id attribute. Existing templates already supplying a correct data-id need only the updated shared JS.

To call it yourself, use the stable ID on the existing ContentItemContainer, or a selector for its author class div. The target must identify one iPart with a unique, stable container ID and one QueryTemplateSet. Do not put an author class on the native container: iMIS creates a separate inner div for the iPart CSS class field. For the task iPart discussed here:

Refresh the task Query Template Display
await UnionSuiteRefresh.queryTemplate('#ste_container_ciTasks');

The helper fetches the same page with credentials and current query-string filters (or the query portion of the SPA hash), without a browser reload or REST call. It matches the fresh iPart by its stable ID, keeps its existing container and immediate panel owner, and replaces the server-rendered contents. It then initialises the known native simplePaginate settings and explicitly rebuilds the theme header slots, action buttons, search and completion controls. Search text, filter disclosure and Show completed survive; completed rows stay hidden when Show completed is off. The outstanding count is recalculated from fresh results, and focus is restored when a corresponding control exists. Native pagination restarts at its default page.

Existing rows remain visible beneath us-query-refresh-overlay while loading. The helper adds this class itself; authors do not add it. It uses --bg-surface and the existing spinning-circles loader, centred in the section, including its reduced-motion treatment. aria-busy, inert content and temporary positioning are restored on completion or failure. Direct panels, wrapped panels, empty class wrappers and simple output without a panel are supported; no-styling iParts are refreshed without adding theme controls. Nested iParts, multiple result sets and output without a QueryTemplateSet require a separate integration.

HTTP errors, redirects, non-HTML/login responses, missing or duplicate identities and stale replacements reject the Promise. Previous content is retained until response validation and native pagination checks pass. The helper does not replay script tags, reload libraries or trigger a global ASP.NET load event. Keep the native simplePaginate plugin loaded if its startup block is present. An optional awaited initialize(container) callback handles additional site-specific code after replacement and before the theme controls are rebuilt. An initializer failure is reported after replacement; retry refresh to fetch and initialise again. The event us:query-template-refreshed bubbles from the container after successful initialisation.

Optional page filters, cancellation and custom initialisation
async function reloadTaskTemplate(initializeTaskContent, signal) {
  await UnionSuiteRefresh.queryTemplate('#ste_container_ciTasks', {
    timeout: 30000,
    signal,
    initialize: initializeTaskContent
  });
}

Options are url (an explicit same-origin page URL), timeout (30000ms by default), signal, initialize and match: 'all' for deliberately refreshing several selected iParts. Explicit selectors are page-scoped and each selected iPart is matched by its own ID; list position is not an identity. Calls are queued and deduplicated within each selection. Custom action callbacks can also use refresh.queryTemplate(selector, options). The CDN's legacy refreshQueryTemplate() is unchanged: replace its call sites with the shared method to get this lifecycle.

Adding without a reload: insert: true reads the page quietly, with no overlay, and adds only the rows the iPart does not have yet, each sliding in where the page has it. Rows the page no longer has fold away (a list of the newest few), and parts of staying rows the template marks (a count such as View all attachments (7)) take the page's new text. Nothing else in the iPart changes: search, filters, open rows and focus stay. Rows are compared by data-us-task-ordinal, data-us-contact-ordinal or data-ordinal, otherwise by their text. holding lists placeholder rows already in the set (the composer's holding rows): each new row takes one's place, matched by its data-us-holding-id, the rest in order. iParts selected together with match: 'all' share one page read. The Promise resolves with {status: 'inserted', inserted, removed} for each iPart, and us:query-template-refreshed carries detail.inserted. A paged list that grows past its page is refreshed in full.

Add the task just saved without reloading the list
await UnionSuiteRefresh.queryTemplate('#ste_container_ciTasks', { insert: true });

Activity feed: adding a record without a reload

After saving a record that an activity feed shows, ask only the feed's sources of that type for their newest rows. UnionSuiteActivityFeed.reload(feed, {quiet: true, types}) re-runs those sources' API IQAs from the start, adds the records the feed has not loaded, and folds their cards in where they belong. Nothing else in the feed changes: no "Loading activity…", no redraw, the chosen type, search and open cards stay as they are, and the type counts are re-queried. types lists source data-type values; leave it out to ask every source. The Promise resolves with the number of records added. A feed that has not loaded yet is left to load as usual, and plain reload(feed) still starts the feed again.

Fold a note just saved into the feed
const feed = document.querySelector('#ste_container_ciAllActivity .us-activity-feed');
await UnionSuiteActivityFeed.reload(feed, { quiet: true, types: ['note'] });

Activity feed: window, Load all and open filters

Optional attributes on the us-activity-feed element (the case page's Recent activity and All activity use them). The type chosen in a section is kept when a refresh rebuilds its feed, with no attribute needed.

AttributeEffect
A fixed window of that many days. The feed sends the start date to every source (the same IQAs as a full timeline, no copies filtered to the window), offers no date range, and the count reads "last 30 days". Use it in place of data-us-activity-days.
A Load all button beside Show more, shown while Show more is. It loads every record the chosen type and date range hold, fetching as Show more does; a very long history may need a second press.
A narrow feed, whose search and date range fold behind the heading's filter button, starts with them open. A wide feed shows them inline anyway.
data-us-activity-history-label, -history-section, -history-tabThe footer link (data-us-activity-history): its text (default View full history), and a section link to open instead of the address: the section (activity:all) and its CCO tab (Activity).
The case Overview's Recent activity · the feed element
<div class="us-activity-feed"
  data-us-activity-folder="$/_i4u_/Core/Cases/Layouts v2/Activity Feed - Base"
  data-us-activity-details-folder="$/_i4u_/Client/Cases - UnionSuite/Layouts/Activity Feed - Details"
  data-us-activity-filter="CaseNum" data-us-activity-value="{#query.Ordinal}"
  data-us-activity-window="30" data-us-activity-limit="10" data-us-activity-page="5"
