</div>

Activity feed: source options and Details views

Optional attributes on a source <li>. The types attachment (indigo, a paperclip) and change (brown, a history icon) join call, email, meeting, sms, note and interaction.

AttributeEffect
data-record-label, data-record-iconThe card's open button text and a Tabler icon class: data-record-label="View email" data-record-icon="ti-eye".
data-restrictedThe Summary the IQA returns for a restricted note (Restricted). That card shows the notes screen's restricted notice and Show note (US-NOTES-RESTRICTED), which checks access for the note whose ordinal is the ActivityKey's digits. The IQA must return no note text for it anywhere.
data-history="#key"With a #, a Details view in place of a View all link (below).
data-section-link, data-section-tab, data-section-labelWhile its type is chosen, a heading link through the theme's section links: case:attachments, Attachments, View all attachments.
data-comms-recipientsThe path of a recipients IQA (one row per recipient, filtered on CommunicationLogKey, as the agreement Communications panel's details IQA). A sent email's opened card shows the send's results bar and its recipients, exactly as the agreement panel does (US-COMMS-RECIPIENTS), from the row's CommunicationLogKey, Audience (Members shows counts only), Recipients, Delivered and NotSent columns. The case timeline's Emails Sent source uses it.
data-record-actionsTheme action keys, space-separated, whose buttons the opened card shows, each with data-ordinal set to the record's ActivityKey: agreements.view-attachment agreements.download-attachment opens a stored file (an attachment, or an email logged through Outlook) through CloudToolz. Every column the IQA returns named Data-name becomes a data-name attribute on the card for the actions to read: Data-us-file-id, Data-us-file-branch and Data-us-file-name for View and Download.

A change card's Detail, one Field: old → new per line, opens as a list with the field names leading.

Card details (data-details, in data-us-activity-details-folder). When a card first opens, the feed runs its source's details IQA for that record, filtered on the record filter (CaseNum) and ActivityKey, and shows everything it returns, laid out as a field group panel lays out its IQA (US-FIELD-GROUPS, the case's Case details and Resolution panels; 22 October 2026): Group-Label columns (FollowUp-Task status) under a heading per group, in column order; a number group (1-Outcome) starts a block with no heading; Tone-* badges and Alert-* columns work as in the panels. The record's keys, Ordinal, ActivityKey and the record filter's column, are left out; untick any other key column the IQA selects. An Additional-* column, the earlier convention, reads as its label alone, with no heading, so existing details IQAs look as before.

Blank values in card details: hidden unless (show blank) (owner, 22 October 2026). A details column that comes back blank (null, empty or spaces) is left out of the card, and a group whose columns are all left out goes with them, so a card shows only what was recorded. To show a field even when it is blank, end its column name with : FollowUp-Actioned notes (show blank) shows Actioned notes under Follow up with a dash when there is nothing in it, and the marker never shows in the label. It works on any details column, Additional-* included, and is not case-sensitive. This is the cards' rule only: field group panels (Case details, Resolution, the member card) are unchanged and show every column, blank ones as a dash.

Details views. Add to the feed iPart's CSS class (the case preset us-case-all-activity includes it). A source's data-history="#notes" names a detailed iPart on the same page whose CSS class carries us-activity-detail us-activity-detail-notes: a list or a native IQA grid, placed right under the feed. While Notes is chosen, a Details view toggle leads the heading's buttons; pressed, the detailed iPart takes the timeline's place under the type filters, closing up into the same card, and choosing another type brings the timeline back. Detailed iParts stay hidden otherwise. Each Details view is remembered as the viewer last left it (owner, 25 October 2026), per page and detailed iPart, in that browser (localStorage, UnionSuite.detailsView.v1:page:key): someone who works in the notes ledger gets it each time they choose Notes, and after a reload. Only the toggle changes it; choosing another type does not. Without storage it starts closed each time. The iPart also carries data-us-activity-type, the chosen type, so the case timeline shows only that type's add button (Add note, Add meeting, Upload). A details feed whose sources are all one type starts on that type and hides its type filters.

Adding in place: composers

Implemented in the shared theme (US-INLINE-COMPOSER in zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css, with two dark mode details in zzDarkMode.css). A section's add button opens a small form at the top of that section. After a save, the entry slides into the list where it belongs: no report reload, no loading overlay and nothing else in the section changes. The example's save, page read and popup are stand-ins; the composer, holding row and insert are the theme's own code.

{{INLINE_COMPOSER_DEMO}}

What the user sees

  1. The section's add button opens the composer at the top of that section only, with the cursor in the main field. The button shows pressed while it is open. Pressed again, it puts the composer away and keeps what was typed for next time; Cancel and Escape discard it.
  2. Save (Ctrl+Enter, or Enter in a one-line field) checks the required fields, then asks the spec's question if it has one; the button then reads Save anyway, and changing anything withdraws the question.
  3. The Save button spins for the save only. Once it has saved, the composer clears (keepOpen) or folds away, and a holding row opens where the entry will land: what was typed, a tick that draws itself and Saved, then a soft shimmer.
  4. The real row takes the holding row's place, growing from its height. If the report sorts it elsewhere, the holding row closes as the row opens there.
  5. In an empty list the same happens. With no results, iMIS renders the No results HTML (such as a us-placeholder) straight into the panel body, with no QueryTemplateSet. The holding row replaces that content, and the list then takes the page's result set and its first row, keeping an open composer. When a refresh finds a list's last row gone, the No results content returns. Query Template refreshes (UnionSuiteRefresh.queryTemplate, whole list, one row or a quiet insert) all handle an empty list, recognised by the pager script iMIS still renders.
  6. If the save fails, the composer stays as typed and shows the spec's failed message.
  7. A files composer with eachFile (case Upload) saves one file at a time, and Save counts them: Uploading 2 of 3…. Files that uploaded get their holding rows. If any fail, they stay chosen with a message naming them, and pressing Save again tries just those.

Setting one up

Three parts. Put the action class on the iPart that shows the list (its CSS class field): the add button appears in that panel's heading, and the class also tells the composer which list takes the new row. Register the composer with UnionSuiteComposers.define(key, spec) in the client registrations, inside UnionSuiteLoader.ready as for any action. Give it a save that returns the new record's {Ordinal}. The key's class is us-action- followed by the key with dots as hyphens; the full form is a second action, KEY-form, for a banner's Actions menu.

iPart CSS class — the list the meeting composer belongs to
us-action-cases-add-meeting
A composer: required and optional fields, a question before saving and the save call
// In the client registrations, after the theme loader (see Installation).
UnionSuiteComposers.define('cases.add-meeting', {
  label: 'Add meeting',                          // the section's add button
  context: {
    caseId: {from: 'query', parameter: 'CaseID', required: true},
    caseNum: {from: 'query', parameter: 'CaseNum', required: true}
  },
  recordKey: ['caseId'],
  form: {                                        // More options and the banner's Actions item
    href: ({context}) => '/Cases_CreateMeeting?CaseID=' + encodeURIComponent(context.caseId),
    title: 'Schedule meeting',
    fill: {Title: '#MeetingTitle', Date: '#MeetingDate'}   // optional: the popup's fields
  },
  lists: () => ['us-action-cases-add-meeting'], // the iPart classes the save changes
  arrive: {field: 'Date', attribute: 'data-us-meeting-date', order: 'ascending'},
  composer: {
    kind: 'meeting', label: 'New meeting', save: 'Save meeting',
    main: {type: 'text', name: 'Title', label: 'Meeting title',
           placeholder: 'Meeting title…', required: 'Give the meeting a title.'},
    fields: [
      {type: 'date', name: 'Date', label: 'Meeting date', required: 'Choose the meeting date.'},
      {type: 'time', name: 'Time', label: 'Start time'},
      {type: 'text', name: 'Location', label: 'Location', placeholder: 'Location', width: 'grow'}
    ],
    confirm: values => values.Date && values.Date < new Date().toISOString().slice(0, 10)
      ? 'That date has passed. Save the meeting anyway?' : null,
    submit: async (values, context) => {
      const response = await UnionSuiteAgreements.cloudToolz('/cases/quick-add/meeting', {
        method: 'POST',
        body: JSON.stringify({CaseID: context.caseId, CaseNum: context.caseNum, Title: values.Title, Date: values.Date,
          Time: values.Time, Location: values.Location})
      });
      return response.json();                    // {Ordinal: 1234}
    },
    saved: 'Meeting saved.',
    failed: 'The meeting could not be saved. Try again.'
  }
});
SpecPurpose
label, iconThe add button's text and icon (plus by default).
context, recordKey, owner, sourceAs for any action (unified actions). submit receives the resolved context.
formThe full form: href (a URL or a function of the action's context), title, label (the banner item's text), maxWidth (800 by default) and optional fill (below).
lists(values)The iPart classes the save changes. The composer's own list takes the holding rows; every list named takes the new rows quietly. A note with a follow-up can name the tasks list too.
arriveWhere the holding row opens: 'top' (default), 'bottom' of the rows showing, or {field, attribute, order} to sit among the rows by a value, such as a meeting by date. The report still decides where the real row goes.
feedFor an activity feed: the source data-type values the record shows as (default: the composer's kind).
pageUrlThe page the quiet insert reads. Leave it out: it reads the current page, as the Query Template refresh does.
composer.kind, label, saveThe modifier class (us-composer--KIND), the group's accessible name and the Save button's text.
composer.keepOpenClear and stay open after a save, for adding several in a row (tasks, staff).
composer.main, fieldsThe main field, then the fields on the bar below it (see the table of field types).
composer.confirm(values)Optional. Returns a question to ask before saving, or nothing.
composer.submit(values, context)Saves. Resolves once saved with {Ordinal} (or {Ordinals} for several); throws on failure.
composer.eachFileOptional, for a files main field. Calls submit once per file, with that file alone in the field, and counts them on Save. Files that fail stay chosen to try again.
composer.saved, failedAnnounced after a save (text, or a function of the values); shown when the save fails.

Field types

Every field has type, name (its key in values) and label (its accessible name); placeholder is optional. A field is required when it has a required message, the text shown when it is left empty. shownBy names a checkbox: the field shows, and is required, only while that box is ticked. width: 'grow' lets a bar field take spare width. showLabel: true shows the label beside a bar text, date, time or select field, for a value that would not say what it is on its own (the case task composer's Due Date); it shows and hides with its field.

TypeValue submit receivesNotes
text, textareaThe text, trimmed; '' if emptyA textarea main field is three lines and grows.
date, time'YYYY-MM-DD', 'HH:MM'; '' if emptyThe browser's own pickers.
selectThe chosen option's value (its text, for an option given as a string)options: an array of strings, an array of {value, label} pairs (a code saved, a label shown), or a function of the action's context that resolves to either, such as a value list read from an IQA. A function's list loads once the composer is built; until then the select shows Loading…, and if the read fails it says Could not load the list and saves a blank value.
checkboxtrue or falseCan reveal shownBy fields.
filesAn array of File objectsA drop zone that lists the files chosen. One holding row per file.
comboboxThe picked option's value, never typed textsearch({term, wrapper}) resolves [{value, name, meta, disabled}]; empty(term) is the no-matches text; avatars adds initials; preload(context) starts reading the list as the composer opens. A disabled option shows but cannot be picked (someone already on the case). The list opens when the field is clicked, typed in or arrowed down, not when the composer focuses it; a search that takes a moment shows a spinner and Loading… in the list.
multiselectAn array of the picked options' values; [] if noneoptions as for select (strings, {value, label} pairs, or a function of the context resolving to either), read once as the composer opens. Each pick shows as a chip with a remove button and leaves the list, which stays open for the next pick. Typing filters the list; Enter picks the highlighted or first option and never saves while something is typed; Backspace in the empty field removes the last chip. empty(term) is the no-matches text. The case add attachment composer's Tags.

Validation is the required check, the optional question and the server. There are no format, range or cross-field checks: the date and time inputs keep their own formats, and anything else is the save endpoint's to refuse. A field hidden by shownBy sends ''.

Where the new entry lands

A Query Template list is read quietly with UnionSuiteRefresh.queryTemplate(selector, {insert: true}) (above): only its new rows go in, matched to the holding rows by Ordinal. Rows are known by data-us-task-ordinal, data-us-contact-ordinal or data-ordinal in the template, otherwise by their text, so give each template one. An activity feed asks only its sources of the record's types for their newest rows (the quiet feed reload). All the lists one save changes share one read of the page. A list that cannot be read refreshes as usual instead.

The save endpoint must return the new record's {Ordinal}, or {Ordinals} for several. Without it the new rows still arrive, matched to the holding rows in order.

The full form: carrying the draft over and reporting a save

More options opens the full form with what was typed already filled in, and leaves the composer open underneath. The popup's URL gets a one-time usDraft key; the theme in the popup takes the draft from the page that opened it, once, so a postback or reload in the popup does not fill it again.

  • The theme fills the form when the spec has form.fill: each composer field name maps to the popup's field, a selector ('#MeetingTitle') or a function (value, document). It waits up to 15 seconds for each field to be drawn, so a form drawn after the page loads (BeyondFormz) works. Values go in through the field's own setter, followed by input and change; a select matches by value, then by text; files are handed over as files.
  • The form fills itself (HubForms): UnionSuiteComposers.received() in the popup returns {key, values, files}, keyed by the composer's field names, or null.

Neither iMIS nor the theme can tell a save from a cancel on its own, so the form says how it closed by passing a value as the popup closes:

The form passesWhat happens
{saved: true, Ordinal: 1234} (or Ordinals)The composer clears at once and the lists take the new row quietly.
{cancel: true} (iMIS's CloseAndCancelRadWindow())Nothing is read; the composer is still there as typed, with the cursor back in it.
Nothing (the popup's ×, a native editor)The lists are read quietly. A new row means it saved and the composer clears; otherwise it stays. Someone else adding to the same list meanwhile looks like a save.
BeyondFormz custom script on submit (success) — tells the page that opened it the form saved
/* UnionSuite popup result: tell the page that opened this form it saved. */
(function () {
  var result = { saved: true };
  // When the new record's ordinal is known, add it: result.Ordinal = 1234;

  var radWindow = typeof GetRadWindow === 'function' ? GetRadWindow() : null;
  if (radWindow) {
    radWindow.close(result);
    return;
  }
  try {
    if (window.parent !== window && window.parent.UnionSuiteDialog) {
      window.parent.UnionSuiteDialog.close(window, result);
    }
  } catch (error) {
    // Not opened by a UnionSuite page: leave the form as it is.
  }
})();

Run it only after the save has succeeded; a failed save keeps the popup open with the form's error. Add the new record's ordinal when the form can read it. It works in the native popup and the theme dialog, and does nothing when the form is opened as an ordinary page. The banner's Actions item opens the same form with no draft; its close is handled the same way.

Setting up a BeyondFormz form:

  1. Map the composer's fields to the form's in the spec's form.fill, using the form's own field selectors.
  2. Paste the script above into the form's custom script on submit, so it runs after a successful save only.
  3. If the submit step can read the new record's ordinal (from the Flowz or endpoint response), set result.Ordinal; otherwise leave it out.
  4. Give the form's Cancel button, if it has one, CloseAndCancelRadWindow().
  5. Check on the live site: the draft fills in, a save slides the row in and clears the composer, and Cancel leaves the composer as typed.

HubForms follows the same contract from its own code: on load in a popup, read UnionSuiteComposers.received() and prefill the fields whose names match (ignoring the rest); after a successful save, close with {saved: true, Ordinal} (Ordinals for several); on Cancel, close with {cancel: true}; on a failed save, stay open and show the error.

Limits

  • Lists are Query Template Displays and activity feeds. A native IQA grid is not a composer list.
  • A paged list (native simplePaginate) that grows past its page is refreshed in full instead.
  • The fill sets plain fields. Telerik date, time and combo boxes ignore a value set by script: map those to a function that uses the control's own client API.
  • Saves, popups and BeyondFormz forms need verifying on the live site; the example simulates them.

Presentation comes from the definition

The same action class can render a labelled button in a heading, an icon in a row and text with an icon in a menu. Configure the presentation section; there are no separate button/link/icon business-action class families. The following uses the actual renderer with example-only definitions.

{{REPORT_ICON_DEMO}}

Controls reuse shared TextButton, outline, icon and menu styling. Normal colours follow text-link, brand/surface and focus tokens; danger follows danger/danger-bg. Icon glyphs are decorative and the outer control supplies the accessible name, tooltip and focus state. Bundled plus/pencil/trash require no icon font; ti-* needs the installed Tabler assets. Existing reduced-motion/forced-colour treatments apply. Generated IDs are unique per instance and must not be used as business record keys.

Conflicting definitions

{{ACTION_CONFLICT_DEMO}}

Use define(key, fullDefinition) for normal registration. Repeating the same key/owner/source retains the original definition; it does not compare function bodies. A second owner/source for that key shows the warning icon on every instance and blocks both candidate operations. A class claimed by different keys is also a conflict. Repeated buttons using one definition are valid.

Explicit replacement and diagnostics
console.table(UnionSuiteActions.listActions());
console.log(UnionSuiteActions.getActionStatus('jobs.edit'));
// After reviewing the source, replace with the FULL winning definition:
// UnionSuiteActions.configure('jobs.edit', winningDefinition);
// Remove a definition and its claims:
// UnionSuiteActions.configure('jobs.edit', null);

Status contains key, namespace, status, className and owner/source registrations; it omits record data and executable functions. has(key) is true for configured and conflicted keys. configure replaces the whole definition and acknowledges prior claims; replaying a known include does not undo the override. Removing a definition leaves authored action classes unconfigured/disabled until removed or registered again.

Conflict warnings are keyboard-focusable and show an accessible explanation. Attempted activation announces an error. Duplicate registered definitions do not throw; invalid option schemas do throw before replacing an existing definition. A syntax error prevents that file executing and cannot be diagnosed as a duplicate registration. The shared runtime is loaded separately, so missing definitions remain unavailable. This is not a scanner for arbitrary overwritten globals or unrelated legacy handlers.

Events: us:action-conflict reports competing registrations; us:action-error reports a key/actionId, phase and Error without logging row context; us:action-complete reports completed or closed after successful follow-up. Runtime errors use visible accessible feedback. Context/availability reasons appear on the control. The guard cannot undo earlier capture handlers or operations already running.

Native popup options

{{POPUP_ACTION_DEMO}}

Set action.type='popup'; the adapter uses Asi.js ShowDialog_NoReturnValue and all 13 forwarded native arguments. href may be async. The native function must exist before activation. URLs must resolve to HTTP(S), cannot contain credentials or a fragment, and use URLSearchParams for record values. Asi.js supplies its native popup/cache handling; do not reuse captured ParentPageInstanceKey or DialogCacheParam values.

action.popup optionDefault / behaviour
presenter'radwindow'. 'dialog' opens the page in the theme dialog instead of ShowDialog_NoReturnValue; use it only for read-only or theme-owned pages.
titlepresentation.label; optional native dialog header string.
width / height90% each. Positive pixel number, px string, or whole 1%–100%. With presenter 'dialog', height defaults to 'auto' (fit the page).
maxWidth / maxHeightOff. Positive pixel number or px string. When width (height) would open larger than this on the current window, the popup opens at this many pixels instead; smaller windows keep the percentage. For example width '90%' with maxWidth 960 is 960px on a 1920px screen and 90% on a 1000px one.
fullscreenBelowOff; positive pixel breakpoint adds Mode=Maximized below it. Zero disables.
dismissibleDialog presenter only. true: a click on the backdrop closes the dialog. Set false for a page where an accidental close would lose work.
args / iconUrl / templateTypenull / null / E. Forwarded native arguments; visible native iconUrl support depends on Asi.js. RadWindow presenter only, as are closeWindowOnCommit, preserveStatefulBusinessContainer and sourceObject; setting one with presenter 'dialog' throws.
windowNameGenerated unique name. A configured name cannot be reused while that editor is still open.
closeWindowOnCommit / preserveStatefulBusinessContainerfalse / false; native boolean flags.
sourceObjectActivated control; native argument 13.
onBeforeCloseOptional synchronous callback. Return false or call closeEvent.set_cancel(true) to cancel. Promises are rejected and closing is cancelled. The dialog presenter passes a closeEvent with the same set_cancel, plus reason (close, escape, backdrop, dismiss or script).
onCloseOptional async-capable callback, once after native close. Prefer action.refresh for view updates and refresh-only recovery.
onErrorOptional error callback. Runtime errors also have visible feedback and us:action-error.

The action/record guard lasts until the popup closes and follow-up completes. Before opening, stale context, removed controls, conflicts or changed definitions cancel pending resolution. After opening, the validated context and original report identity are retained for close handling even if the report is replaced. Native close is not a saved signal; refresh.when='close' also runs after Cancel/X. A save-only integration needs the particular editor's verified result contract.

Current definitions and legacy dependencies · Helper cleanup: replaced, retained and still to migrate · Execution contract and implementation limits.

Theme dialog (UnionSuiteDialog)

Implemented in shared CSS and JavaScript. A native <dialog> around a same-site page, with the same title, close control, frame and backdrop as the restyled RadWindow (see Dialog title bars). Use it for read-only and theme-owned pages: speaker or record details, previews, lists. The browser keeps keyboard focus inside it and blocks the page behind it; focus returns to the opening control on close.

Keep iMIS editors on the RadWindow. Editors that close themselves through the RadWindow (Save & Close, CloseWindowOnCommit) cannot close the theme dialog, and a RadWindow an editor opens would sit beneath it. Address, job, contact method and agreement editors stay on the default presenter.

From a definition, set popup: {presenter: 'dialog'}; locks, refresh on close and onClose work as for the RadWindow. From your own script, call UnionSuiteDialog.open(href, options). It returns a handle with closed (a promise of {reason}), close(), reload(), dialog and frame.

Open a page in the theme dialog
UnionSuiteDialog.open('/_i4u_/Core/Your-Page.aspx?ID=' + encodeURIComponent(id), {
  title: 'Speaker bios',
  width: '90%',
  maxWidth: 760
}).closed.then(({reason}) => {
  // reason: close, escape, backdrop, dismiss or script
});
UnionSuiteDialog.open optionDefault / behaviour
titleEmpty. Shown in the header and used as the frame's accessible name.
width / maxWidth900px / off. Pixels, px string, or a whole percentage of the window. The dialog never exceeds the window less 16px each side.
height / maxHeight'auto' / off. 'auto' fits the page and follows it as it grows or shrinks; past the window height the page scrolls inside the dialog. A pixel or percentage height fixes it.
imisPopuptrue: adds IsPopup=true (and TemplateType=E unless the URL sets one) so iMIS uses its popup layout and the theme marks the page with data-us-dialog-page. Set false for a page that is not an iMIS content page.
dismissibletrue: a click on the backdrop closes the dialog.
fullscreenBelowOff. Opens full-window when the window is narrower than this many pixels.
onBeforeClose(handle, closeEvent)Optional, synchronous. Return false or call closeEvent.set_cancel(true) to keep the dialog open.
returnFocus / nameThe focused element / none. Where focus goes on close; the frame's window name.

Escape closes the dialog from its header or from inside the page, unless an open control in the page (an expanded combo box, the page's own popup) takes the key. A page can close its dialog with a attribute on a button or link, or by calling parent.UnionSuiteDialog.close(window). The page loads with the theme's popup spinner. Opening and closing fade and lift over 220ms and 160ms; reduced motion makes both instant. Pages on other sites are refused, because the dialog reads the page to fit and to close on Escape. Tokens: --us-dialog-radius, --us-dialog-shadow, --us-dialog-scrim and --us-dialog-scrim-opacity, shared with the RadWindow and redefined in zzDarkMode.css.

Opt out: native iMIS presentation

Put on the Query Menu iPart. The shared stylesheet excludes the wrapper and its descendants, leaving the loaded native iMIS/Orion rules in control. Header utilities, generated custom actions, expansion and theme IQA Find/Refresh feedback are disabled. Existing native filtering, Export, sorting and paging remain. This class wins over other report classes. It does not remove site-wide inherited tokens, separately loaded client CSS or styles on popup elements rendered outside the iPart. After toggling it programmatically, call refresh() to restore moved controls; native partial updates reconcile automatically.

The unified registry populates these slots from registered us-action-* classes. It supplies shared button/link/icon styling, unique IDs and the execution route. Keep business definitions in the dedicated standard/client files. Unconfigured classes produce unavailable controls rather than guessing a destination.

Feedback messages

Implemented in zUnionSuite.css using native iMIS message classes. Existing pages update automatically; no new page/iPart class or JavaScript is needed. The theme preserves native icons, message content, roles and lifecycle.

{{MESSAGE_DEMO}}

Placement: iMIS normally generates these classes. For authored HTML, apply them to the message element itself. Click a class to copy:

Success, information, warning and error use their semantic tokens; these are independent of brand seeds. Inline spans remain on the surrounding text baseline. Existing PNG message icons are retained at 24px in page messages. Security indicators and standalone icon spans retain their native treatment. Use warnings calmly: iMIS also uses AsiWarning for empty sections.

Authored status message
<div class="AsiSuccess" role="status">Successfully updated</div>
<p>Status: <span class="AsiSuccess">Updated</span></p>

System alerts

The native System Notification panel (#SystemAlertPanel, above the page content) is styled automatically: each alert is its own block, in the information colours with a leading edge. The alerts are inset 12px from the content above and below and 16px from each side, and stack 8px apart. A hidden alert, such as the inactive iMIS upgrade notice, leaves no gap. Each alert has a dismiss button.

Each alert iMIS supplies shows the iMIS logo before its icon; screen readers hear "iMIS". The logo is embedded in zUnionSuite.css from THeme/UnionSuite/images/imis-logo.svg, so no image upload is needed. In dark mode it is drawn in the strong text colour. Our own alerts can show the hub logo in the same place, and screen readers hear "Union Innovation Hub": an alert whose content includes an element with both shows the hub logo; uhubAlert alone shows the message with no logo. The theme draws it 20px high from the site's /images/Hub/UHUB_Logo.png (521 × 245, the HUB badge on a transparent background, so it suits dark mode too); that file must exist on each site where these alerts show, and the offline preview below cannot load it. Author no logo in the alert itself. The two kinds arrive differently: ASI's messages bring their own icon row (.media), while iMIS puts our message straight into the alert's list item, so ours have no icon.

Sitewide alerts from System Alerts

Sitewide alerts are rows in the i4u_UT_SystemAlerts table with SitewideAlert checked; the same row can also be a Staff Bulletin post. The System Notification panel shows the alert tasks in its Process Automation alert set; on the staff site that is i4u_StaffSystemNotificationMessage. Give the task a data source: an IQA on the i4u_UT_SystemAlerts business object selecting Alert, Link and LinkText, filtered to SitewideAlert equals true, StartDate less than or equal to @Date and EndDate greater than or equal to @Date. Give every alert both dates; a blank date fails its filter. Paste the template below into the task's alert message using the editor's HTML view, and replace SystemAlerts with the data source's name.

{{SYSTEM_ALERT_TEMPLATE}}
FieldValueDescription / blank behaviourExample
{#SystemAlerts.Alert}RequiredThe alert text, one short sentence. HTML is kept, so bold and inline links work.A hotfix has been deployed.
{#SystemAlerts.Link}OptionalTrimmed destination for the link after the text. Blank hides the link and its tab stop. Use an http(s) URL, a site path starting with one slash, or a nonempty #anchor; other formats are treated as blank./staff/forums/hotfixes
{#SystemAlerts.LinkText}OptionalThe link's label. Blank reads Read more. Not shown when Link is blank.Hotfix notes

goes on the element around the alert's content, with beside it for the hub logo (the template includes it; delete it for an alert without the logo), and on the optional link. The hidden link and the Read more label are CSS only; no JavaScript is involved. Not yet confirmed live: whether a task shows one alert for each row its data source returns or only the first, and whether the merge field inside href carries a real Link (the editor keeps the class names, and a blank Link arrives as href=""). Until the first is confirmed, keep one sitewide alert active at a time.

Dismissing an alert

zUnionSuite.js adds a Dismiss alert button to the right of every alert, ASI's as well as ours, so no message needs editing; there is no class to add. The alert slides closed. Dismissing the last one closes the panel and its spacing, and keyboard focus moves to the next alert's button, or else to the start of the page content. Alerts iMIS hides itself, such as the inactive upgrade notice, get no button. From 950px wide and below the button is 44px.

A dismissed alert stays hidden in that browser, for the signed-in user, until its text or a link in it changes: an edited or new alert shows again. Dismissals are kept in the browser's localStorage (UnionSuiteDismissedAlerts, the latest 50 per user). They do not follow the user to another browser, and iMIS knows nothing of them. us-report-no-styling on an ancestor opts out.

APIUse
UnionSuiteSystemAlerts.restore()Shows the current user's dismissed alerts again and forgets those dismissals. For support, from the browser console.
UnionSuiteSystemAlerts.refresh()Re-checks the panel. Alerts iMIS adds after the page loads are picked up without it.

Not yet confirmed live: iMIS appears to fill the panel after the page loads, and the theme hides a dismissed alert as it arrives, before it is drawn. If a page arrives with its alerts already in place, a dismissed alert can show for a moment before it is hidden.

For authored dynamic errors use an appropriate alert region; preserve roles already supplied by iMIS. Styling alone does not announce an update or dismiss a message. Compare native and shared-theme examples, including validation, inline, system and legacy messages.

Form fields

Implemented in zUnionSuite.css, with native file-drop enhancement in zUnionSuite.js. Add to the iPart's CSS class setting or a container around ordinary native fields. Do not nest an HTML form inside the iMIS page form. This opt-in avoids changing every legacy form layout at once.

{{FORM_DEMO}}

Existing wraps each field. Put on the label and on the control wrapper. iMIS usually generates these: preserve IDs, names and handlers. The two-column demo layout is not required by the theme.

Ordinary controls use 36px height, strong token borders and a soft focus glow. Pointer and keyboard focus use a thin --field-focus-colour border and --field-focus-glow shadow. The colour defaults to --brand-600, derived from --seed-primary. This applies to ordinary PanelFieldValue controls, including us-form containers, and panel-editor CalendarInput2 text fields. Invalid ordinary fields retain their danger border. A transparent outline preserves a system focus indicator in forced-colour mode; browsers without color-mix retain the focus border. Read-only and disabled fields use darker neutral surfaces. Native date inputs have a full-height calendar icon in Edge/Chrome; other browsers retain their native picker. Firefox draws its own icon inside the field, so there the room kept for the Edge/Chrome icon is dropped and its icon sits at the field's right edge. Telerik and Chosen widgets keep their native adapters. IQA and Query Menu filter fields take the same border and glow, for pointer and keyboard focus, in place of their former navy outline.

BeyondForms fields

Applied automatically. BeyondForms is CloudToolz's form builder. Each form renders [data-beyondformid] > .rendered-form > .form-group > .form-control with its own Bootstrap stylesheet. In zUnionSuite.css (US-BEYOND-FORMS), its text, date, number, select and textarea fields take the ordinary field look above: 36px height, strong token border, 6px corners, 13px UI font, brand border on hover and the field focus glow. Disabled, read-only and aria-invalid="true" fields match too. Lists CloudToolz enhances with Select2 get the same box; the Select2 dropdown, tags and search keep their own styling. Date fields keep the browser's own picker and date format, with the same full-height calendar box as ordinary date fields in Edge/Chrome (in place of the shorter one in 99-Orion.css); Firefox shows its own icon at the field's right edge. Each field's label takes the us-form label: 12px semibold UI font in --text-muted, 5px above the field, without the vendor sheet's 7px hanging offset; the required asterisk uses --danger. Checkbox and radio option labels, buttons and the form's layout are unchanged, and nothing needs to be added to the form. us-report-no-styling on the iPart or an ancestor opts a form out.

File drop works on direct native file inputs inside these field wrappers, respects disabled, multiple and accept attributes, and dispatches native input/change events. The theme makes no upload request; existing application handlers still own processing and server validation. Keyboard users retain the native Choose file control. No extra drop-zone class is required.

Copy the field markup into Content HTML or your authored template; replace the outer form with a div on an iMIS page. Replace sample IDs and values; associate help/errors with aria-describedby and mark invalid controls with aria-invalid. Sample data, grid layout and submit prevention below are demonstration-only.

For red branding (which can resemble an error) or pale branding, set --field-focus-colour to a clearly visible alternative in THeme/UnionSuite-Client/Override.css, loaded after the shared theme. No extra author class or JavaScript is required. This field-only override also reaches IQA filter fields; it leaves button, checkbox and radio focus styles unchanged. The glow keeps its fixed size and opacity; there is no automatic contrast adjustment.

Optional client field focus colour
:root {
  --field-focus-colour: #006f94;
}
{{FORM_TEMPLATE}}

Popup forms

Implemented in the theme (Forms/), 20 October 2026: the task form. Live use waits on its IQAs, its CloudToolz endpoint and its three iMIS pages, which are blank placeholders until built (setting up). Not yet verified on the dev tenant.

A popup form is one kind of record form (a task, later a note, milestone or attachment) that works the same on every module. Each module has its own iMIS page, and that page holds only a placeholder; the theme's forms runtime draws the form into it, saves it through CloudToolz and closes the popup with the popup form contract. The forms bring their own styles, so they work on a page without the UnionSuite theme (a client may take a module without the rest of the styling); with the theme they take its colours, font and dark mode. All forms are planned to move to HubForms once it has the features they use; until then these are built to the same contract, so HubForms can replace them one form at a time without changing the pages or the endpoints.

{{POPUP_FORMS_ADD_DEMO}} {{POPUP_FORMS_EDIT_DEMO}}

Using the task form

Written for the staff who use it, so it can become client-facing help.

  • Where the task goes: the line at the top names the record ("Agreement A107", "Case C217") or says Personal task · Assigned to you.
  • What needs doing? is the only required field: one line, as the task will read in the task list. The flag beside it marks the task High priority; the High Priority badge shows while it is on. Add details opens a box for anything the person doing it needs to know.
  • Due date (optional): pick a date, or press Today, Tomorrow or In a week. Once there is a date, Add time adds a time beside it (in 15-minute steps; empty the time to remove it) and Clear date removes both.
  • Assign to (optional, on cases and agreements): starts as you. Choose someone else from the list (type to search), or remove the name to leave the task Unassigned, as something to be actioned as part of the case or agreement itself. On a case the list is the case's staff; on an agreement it is every staff member or the agreement's own staff, as the page is set up. Personal tasks are always yours, so they have no Assign to.
  • Tags (optional) appear only when task tags have been set up for the module. Subtags appear once a chosen tag has some, shown as "Bargaining › Offer response".
  • Save task saves and closes the popup; the task list refreshes. Cancel closes without saving. Ctrl + Enter saves from anywhere in the form.

Save and add another

For adding several tasks at once, after a meeting say. New behaviour, kept until there is feedback on it (owner, 20 October 2026). Adding only.

  1. It saves the task exactly as Save task does.
  2. The popup stays open. What needs doing?, the details and the High priority flag clear; the due date and time, Assign to, Tags and Subtags stay for the next task, since tasks added together usually share them.
  3. The footer says Saved "the task". Add the next one. and the cursor goes back to What needs doing?.
  4. Closing afterwards, with Save task or Cancel, keeps every task already saved: Cancel does not undo them, and the task list refreshes to show them all.

Editing a task

The pencil on a task row opens the same form with the task filled in, its details open when it has any. Completed ticks the task off (the task line strikes through, as on the task rows) or, unticked, reopens it. Save changes saves and closes; there is no Save and add another. The line at the end says who added the task and when. Deleting a task is not on the form: it is planned as an administrator-only action.

Setting up a task form page

One iMIS page per module. On each, add a Content HTML iPart holding only its placeholder (no other markup, and no <form>: the iMIS page is already one form). Make each page available to the staff who add that module's tasks.

Content HTML — the three task form pages
<!-- Personal tasks (home page) -->
<div data-us-form="task" data-module="personal"></div>

<!-- Case tasks -->
<div data-us-form="task" data-module="case"></div>

<!-- Agreement tasks: data-assign="all" (every staff member) or "record" (the agreement's staff) -->
<div data-us-form="task" data-module="agreement" data-assign="all"></div>

Typealwaystask.

Pointing the task buttons at the pages

In Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js, set each page's path in UnionSuiteTaskForms (the block at the top of the file). While a path is blank, that module's task buttons keep opening the form they open today; once set, home.add-task, agreements.add-task and the agreement task row pencils (agreements.edit-task, agreements.view-task, agreements.preview-task) open the task form, the pencils with the task's Ordinal. The home page's pencil (home.edit-task) opens the row's TaskUrl: have the home tasks IQA build it as the task's own module page with its ModuleID, ModuleOrdinal and Ordinal. Case task actions are still in the case page work (prototypes/wip/case-page/) and follow when it is promoted.

Loading

With the theme, nothing else is needed: the loader (index.js) loads Forms/UnionSuiteForms.js on any page with a data-us-form placeholder, with or without the theme's CSS. A page without the theme includes the runtime itself, from the uploaded theme folder:

Content HTML — the forms runtime without the theme loader
<script src="/App_Themes/UnionSuite2/Forms/UnionSuiteForms.js?v=1.0.10"></script>

Change the folder to the tenant's theme folder and the version to the loader's RELEASE: the runtime loads its form files and stylesheet beside itself with the same ?v=, which is what makes browsers fetch a new release (the theme folder is cached for a week).

Still to build (owner)

The task form's IQAs and endpoint are blank in the CONFIG block at the top of Forms/TaskForm.js. Until they are set the form still draws: its lists are empty (Tags is not drawn at all), edit says it is not set up, and Save says saving is not set up and logs what it would send to the browser console. Build each IQA to these filters and columns (output aliases), then put its whole path in CONFIG:

Task form IQAs
CONFIG keyFiltersColumns
taskModule, OrdinalOne row: Title, Details, AssignToID, AssignToName, DueDate, DueTime (14:30, or a date-time), HighPriority, Completed (true/false or 1/0), TagOrdinals and SubtagOrdinals (comma-separated, e.g. 5,6), AddedBy, AddedOn. Blank values are fine.
parentTagsModule (optional)Ordinal, TagName. Task tags only, separate from note tags; tags with no module show on every module.
childTagsParentTag, given as "5","6"Ordinal, TagName, ParentTagOrdinal.
caseStaff, agreementStaffModuleOrdinalID, Name: the staff assigned to that case or agreement.
allStaffnoneID, Name: every staff member.

Make each IQA available to /api/query for the staff who use the form. endpoint is the CloudToolz path that saves a task, below; CloudToolz does all of the saving, including the insert into iMIS.

What a form sends, and how it closes

Save posts one JSON object to the form's CloudToolz endpoint, through the same ZenToken call as the theme's other CloudToolz actions (RequestVerificationToken, SelectedPartyId, LoggedInPartyId and WebsiteKey headers). Every form starts with the same record fields; the rest are the form's own, by field name.

The task form's payload
FieldValueBlank
Type"task"—
IDthe signed-in user's party ID—
Module"personal", "case" or "agreement"—
ModuleID, ModuleOrdinalthe parent record, as in the addressnull on personal tasks
Ordinalthe task being editednull when adding: add a new task
TitleWhat needs doing?, trimmednever (required)
HighPrioritytrue / false—
Detailstext""
DueDate"2026-10-24"null
DueTime"14:30", only with a datenull
AssignTo{"ID": "104133", "Name": "Morgan Lee"}. Personal tasks always send the signed-in user. Name can be blank (the "starts as you" default before the staff list names it); treat ID as the truth.null: unassigned
Tags[{"TagOrdinal": "5", "TagName": "Bargaining"}][]
Subtags[{"TagOrdinal": "5", "SubtagOrdinal": "52", "SubtagName": "Offer response"}][]
Completedtrue / false; editing onlynot sent when adding

The endpoint answers with the saved record's ordinal, {"Ordinal": 1234} (new or edited). Any HTTP error shows The task could not be saved. Try again. and keeps the form as it was. The popup then closes with one of the contract's results:

How the forms are built (the format every form follows)

For theme maintainers adding or changing a form, and for whoever moves a form to HubForms.

Files
FileWhat it holds
Forms/UnionSuiteForms.jsThe runtime: finds placeholders, reads the context, loads the form's file and the stylesheet, loads the record and option lists, draws the form in a shadow root, validates, saves through CloudToolz and closes the popup. Its shared field types serve every form. FORM_FILES maps each form name to its file.
Forms/UnionSuiteForms.cssThe styles of every form. In a subfolder, so iMIS never links it on ordinary pages (it links only the theme root's .css); the runtime fetches it once and adopts it into each form before drawing, so a form never shows unstyled.
Forms/TaskForm.jsOne form: a CONFIG block (endpoint, IQAs) and its definition, registered with UnionSuiteForms.define('task', spec). Mostly a list of fields.

Styling rules. A form draws in a shadow root, so nothing outside reaches in (not the iMIS vendor sheets, Orion or the theme's class rules) except inherited values and CSS custom properties. It uses no theme classes (us-switch, us-badge, the theme's checkbox) and draws its own, with inline SVG icons instead of the icon font. The stylesheet reads each theme token once, on :host, into a --usf-… name with a fallback (--usf-accent: var(--accent, #006f94)), and every rule uses only the --usf-… names: with the theme the form takes its colours, font and dark mode; without it, a plain light form. Tokens read: --font-ui, --text-base, --text-strong, --text-muted, --text-link, --bg-surface, --bg-subtle, --bg-sunken, --border, --border-strong, --accent, --accent-hover, --text-on-accent, --field-focus-colour, --field-focus-glow, --focus-ring, --danger, --danger-bg, --success, --radius, --radius-sm. Touch sizes follow max-width: 950px; rows go to one column when the form itself is under 520px wide (a container query).

A form definition (spec)
KeyMeaning
typeSent as Type.
modules, recordModulesThe modules the form supports, and those that need ModuleID + ModuleOrdinal.
record(context)The top line: {icon, label, detail}. Icons: user, briefcase, file.
loadEdit: {query, params(context), toValues(row, context, lookup)}. lookup(name, ids, labels) turns ids into a select's options.
fieldsThe fields, in order (below). Consecutive fields with the same row share a two-column row.
actionsButton labels: save, saveEdit, again (Save and add another; omit to leave it out), cancel.
again.keepThe fields Save and add another keeps; the rest clear.
submit{path, failed}: the CloudToolz path (blank: not set up) and the failure message.
payload(payload, context, values)Optional last adjustment to what is sent.
Field types
TypeKeysValue sent
recordnone: draws spec.record—
textsize: 'large', placeholder; flag: {name, label, badge} adds the toggle button beside it (and its badge); details: {name, label, link, placeholder} adds a textarea behind a linktrimmed text; the flag true/false; details text
textareaplaceholder, shorttrimmed text
datequick: [{label, days}]; time: {name, label, add, step} adds the optional time"yyyy-mm-dd" or null; the time "hh:mm" or null
selectsource (or source(context)): {query, params(context, values), value, label, parent}; single, search (default on), emptyText, unavailable, dependsOn (reloads when that select changes), hideWhenEmpty (drawn only while it has options){ID, Name} (single) or a list; toPayload for another shape
checkstrikes: a field whose text strikes through while tickedtrue/false
metatext(context, record): a quiet line—

Every field also takes name (the payload key), label, required (true or the message), optional (shows "(optional)"), row, modes (['add'], ['edit']), modules, when(context), default (a value or default(context)), draft (the composer draft's field name), payload: false and toPayload(value, context, values). The context a form receives: module, mode (add/edit), userId, moduleId, moduleOrdinal, ordinal and options (the placeholder's other data-* attributes, camel-cased).

Adding a form

  1. Copy Forms/TaskForm.js's pattern: a CONFIG block (endpoint and IQAs, blank until built), the definition, and the closing define and module.exports. Write every IQA path as one whole string.
  2. Add the file to FORM_FILES in UnionSuiteForms.js and to ON_DEMAND in index.js, so Show Usage scans it (node tools/check-usage-sources.cjs fails if it is missing).
  3. Need a field the runtime does not have (a file upload, an access choice)? Add the type to the runtime: its drawing, its value and payload in the pure core, its styles in UnionSuiteForms.css, a test, and a row in the table above. Keep the types generic, so every form can use them and HubForms can match them.
  4. Add tests to tools/test-popup-forms.cjs, the pages' placeholders, the actions that open it, and a guide entry like this one, including its payload and IQA tables.
  5. Bump the loader's RELEASE.

Moving a form to HubForms: keep the page, the address, the payload and the close results; replace only what draws the form. A HubForms form needs the field types above (cascading selects, the flag, quick dates with an optional time, hide-when-empty lists) and a blocking CloudToolz save that returns {Ordinal}; the gaps are recorded in HubForms' docs/UNIONSUITE-POPUP-FORMS.md.

Previews and tests. prototypes/wip/task-form/task-form-preview.html runs the real files on sample data with iMIS stubbed, with switches for module, add or edit, the agreement assign list, task tags, the endpoint, the theme and dark mode. Offline pages (that preview, the examples above) set window.UnionSuiteFormsConfig before the runtime: stylesheet (CSS text), search (an address) and forms.task (sample IQAs and endpoint); production pages set none of these. Tests: node --test tools/test-popup-forms.cjs. Script API: UnionSuiteForms.define(name, spec), .mount(placeholder), .mountAll(), .core (the pure helpers), .query(path, filters), .cloudToolz(path, init).

Accessibility, motion and limits

Every control has a label; required fields are marked and say what is missing beside the field, and the first one takes focus. The flag is a pressed button; selects are a button with a listbox (Down opens it, Up and Down move, Escape closes and returns to the control); the time and details are revealed by labelled buttons. Revealed sections slide over 220 ms, colours and the flag transition over 150 ms, and reduced motion makes them instant. The form is drawn once, after its record and lists have loaded, behind one Loading… line. Enter in a one-line field does nothing; Ctrl + Enter saves. Lists load up to 2,000 rows each. The form reads the address once; open a new popup for another record.

Badges for reports and panels

Implemented in shared CSS. Small, non-interactive labels distinguish records without colouring whole rows. Upload updated zUnionSuite.css after the native theme. No JavaScript or iPart class is required.

{{BADGE_DEMO}}

Apply us-badge to a span inside the IQA's HTML output or panel template, not to the table, row or iPart wrapper. Add one colour modifier from the table. Keep a meaningful visible label; colour alone must not carry the meaning.

Classes — click to copyUse
us-badge us-badge--primaryPreferred / Primary: pale brand colour with dark brand text.
us-badgeSecondary or neutral classifications.
us-badge us-badge--successVerified / Complete: positive outcome, not merely preferred.
us-badge us-badge--warningNeeds attention.
us-badge us-badge--dangerBlocked / Error.
HTML inside a result cell or display template
<span class="us-badge us-badge--primary">Preferred</span>
<span class="us-badge us-badge--primary">Primary</span>
<span class="us-badge">Secondary</span>
<span class="us-badge us-badge--success">Verified</span>
<span class="us-badge us-badge--warning">Needs attention</span>
<span class="us-badge us-badge--danger">Blocked</span>

For a conditional badge, have the existing query/template emit the span only when the record meets the condition; otherwise emit no badge. The theme does not infer status or alter sorting/filtering. Use the IQA's supported HTML-rendering configuration; escaped output displays tags as text. Keep sort/filter values in the underlying query and encode dynamic label text. Remove old inline foreground/background styles from the span so they do not override the theme.

Keep the Preference column if it supports scanning, sorting or filtering; otherwise place the badge beside the value with normal spacing. Use plain text for unremarkable rows. Reserve full-row tinting for selection or records requiring action. Badges have no hover, click, focus or animation behaviour; do not add tabindex, button roles or live announcements to static labels. Optional decorative icons must retain the text label and use aria-hidden="true".

Badges use 12px semibold type, 2px/8px padding and 6px corners. Long labels wrap within their container. Default colours use --bg-sunken/--text-base; primary uses --brand-50/--brand-900; semantic variants pair --success, --warning or --danger with their matching -bg tokens. Forced-colour mode adds a visible border. Check contrast after client token overrides. Existing native badges and banner-specific us-banner__badge remain unchanged; us-report-no-styling excludes this enhancement. Authored spans work after partial refresh without initialization. A badge left empty by a blank query field is not drawn; to hide its whole field too, see us-optional-field in the class reference.

06d / LISTS & CARDS

A shared shell for different kinds of content.

Native Query Template Display cards are styled in 99-Orion.css only when iMIS renders .card.QueryTemplateItem (Display in cards checked). With that option unchecked, ordinary QueryTemplateItem content stays unboxed. Shared aliases, custom HTML shells and optional arrangements are in zUnionSuite.css. Separate cards suit bulletins and notes; connected rows suit tasks; the timeline variant suits dated history. Content slots are optional, so every list need not contain the same fields.

Install updated 99-Orion.css and zUnionSuite.css in their existing order, before client branding/Override.css. No additional stylesheet, icon font or JavaScript is required for these list shells. Page columns, sidebar widths and homepage layout remain outside the component.

Open the standalone list and card reference — native iPart previews, settings and a single-result Query Template for every example. iMIS creates all outer panel, list and card wrappers.

{{LIST_DEMO}}

Search a Query Template Display

Add to the Query Template Display iPart's CSS class field, alongside its existing list and Add action classes. Set a nonempty Title. Load updated zUnionSuite.css after 99-Orion.css, keep client branding/Override.css after the shared CSS, and load zUnionSuite.js once through the site template. No additional stylesheet, search HTML or icon font is needed. This reusable search works with notes, tasks and unrelated query templates. The separate us-task-completed-filter class adds the task-only Show completed control; task completion actions and homepage geometry remain separate.

The class creates a funnel button beside existing header actions and a search field below the heading/description. It starts collapsed. Closing the funnel clears the input and its saved search text, restores results and updates the count immediately; reopening starts with an empty field. The independent Show completed choice and native paging visibility still apply. The “Searchable task results” example above uses the actual shared controller and the unchanged Tasks Query Template; open its funnel to try it. For compact tasks, the combined class value is . Retain your existing Add action class in that same field.

By default, search matches displayed result text without case sensitivity and ignores extra spaces. An optional data-us-search attribute overrides this text for its own result, as described below. The visible-text fallback excludes hidden content, template markup, control attributes and nested iParts. Native hidden/paged results remain hidden. Matching counts and “No matching results on this page” feedback appear below the results. With us-task-progress the results line (“4 of 4 results on this page.”) sits in a card footer instead: the panel's last child, outside the body, so it stays under a scrolling list, at the bottom edge of a us-panel-fill panel, and hidden when there is nothing to report. No Footer HTML is needed. This searches the currently displayed page only; it does not query iMIS, fetch other pages, change native pager totals, determine completion or change access permissions. Use query parameters for filtering the full data set. Clearing search restores the original results and their existing click handlers.

Each iPart has its own generated IDs, query and open state. Whether the filters are open is remembered per page and panel in browser storage shared by every tab, as the IQA report filters are, so a refresh, a CCO tab switch or a record opened in a new tab keeps it. The panel is identified by its iMIS content ID, which a tab switch keeps. Nothing else is kept: every load starts with no search text, no quick-filter chip pressed, and Show completed (or show past meetings) off. Closing the filters also resets them: the search text is cleared and any pressed quick-filter chip released, so the whole list shows again. The funnel exposes Show filters/Hide filters, aria-expanded and aria-controls; search is labelled from the panel title, describes its page scope, and announces counts politely. Opening focuses the search if the trigger still has focus. Enter in search does not submit the iMIS form. The existing IQA 180ms slide handles rapid reversals; reduced motion skips it, and windows 950px or narrower receive 44px controls. Every heading icon toggle shares one on state: a .us-iqa-icon-button with aria-expanded="true" (the open funnel) or aria-pressed="true" (the expand toggle and Show completed tasks) takes the --iqa-selected fill, a --iqa-link icon, a faint link-coloured border and a slight inset shadow. Dark mode follows through --iqa-selected; forced colours add a 2px system-highlight border. Every heading icon button, including the relocated native Export, also shares one keyboard focus ring: a 2px --border-focus outline, 2px offset. A new toggle needs only the class and the ARIA state; do not give it its own pressed colours or focus rule. Colours use --iqa-* aliases to --bg-surface, --bg-sunken, --brand-100, --text-* and --border; the input uses --radius and --font-ui. Pointer and keyboard focus match the taskbar search: a --field-focus-colour border and search icon, with a 4px shadow at 20% opacity and a 0.4s transition. Client focus-colour overrides and the dark palette apply to both searches. Reduced motion removes the transition; forced colours retain a 2px system-highlight outline. The updated shared CSS is sufficient; no markup or JS change and no new tokens are required.

iMIS still generates the CSS-class div, panel, body, QueryTemplateSet and result wrappers. Both cards and plain results are supported; apply the class to the immediate owner generated by the iPart setting, never a surrounding zone or each result. A missing title, missing QueryTemplateSet, banner or us-report-no-styling owner receives no controls. Partial body/panel replacements rebuild one search, retain its query/open state while the class wrapper survives, and preserve existing header actions. Replacing the entire iPart or reloading starts fresh. Changes to rendered rows are observed. For custom insertion or runtime class changes, call UnionSuiteIqaFilters.refresh() after changing the DOM; native ASP.NET load/endRequest hooks do this automatically. Removing the class or opting out releases the search's visibility markers.

Repeating Query Template HTML: keep the existing result markup. For example, this minimal task result becomes searchable simply by adding the class above; the query aliases are suggested mappings, not verified database fields.

{{QUERY_SEARCH_FIELDS}}
Query Template field — one searchable result
<div class="us-list__header">
  <div class="us-list__heading">
    <h3 class="us-list__title">{#query.TaskTitle}</h3>
    <p class="us-list__meta">{#query.MemberName}</p>
  </div>
</div>

Choose which values search uses

Optional goes on one authored wrapper inside each repeating Query Template result. When present, its value replaces visible-text matching for that result. Without it, existing templates continue to search their displayed text. Use this to include an ID or alternate name, or exclude action labels. The contact examples in the gallery demonstrate this: searching 101000 matches Adam Phillips although the ID is not printed.

{{EXPLICIT_SEARCH_FIELDS}}
Query Template field — explicit searchable values
<div data-us-search="{#query.ContactName} {#query.ContactId}">
  <strong>{#query.ContactName}</strong>
</div>

These are suggested query aliases. Every field referenced in data-us-search must be included in the IQA Select/display column list, even if it is not printed in the result. For unused optional fields, select a custom SQL column returning '' with the matching alias, or remove every reference to that field from the template, including its search token. Keep attribute substitutions HTML-encoded, never noencode. Extra spaces and case are ignored; punctuation, phone formatting and word order still matter. This is substring matching, not fuzzy search or phone-number normalisation. An explicitly empty/whitespace attribute matches no nonempty search; remove the attribute to restore visible-text fallback. Use one attribute per result: if multiple owned attributes exist, the first in DOM order wins. Attributes in nested iParts/results and no-styling content are ignored. The supplied value is searchable even when its element is hidden, but records hidden by native paging remain excluded. Other data-* attributes are never indexed. This is not a way to hide confidential information: values remain readable in the browser HTML. Updates, addition and removal of data-us-search automatically reapply the current search.

No outstanding tasks: Biscuit’s hammock

Implemented. The preset displays Biscuit asleep in his hammock when the loaded task results have no outstanding tasks. The live example starts with one completed task: use Show completed to reveal it, or add a sample task and tick its checkbox to see Biscuit return.

{{HOME_TASK_EMPTY_DEMO}}

Put us-home-tasks in the Query Template Display iPart CSS class field; iMIS inserts the extra div around the native panel. Set a nonempty Title, leave Display in cards and Hide when there are no results unchecked, and use the task template and field definitions below. Keep Header blank and the optional task footer in Footer. Load updated zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js; load zzDarkMode.css last after client CSS when using dark mode. No separate illustration asset or script is needed.

Paste this static HTML into the separate No results field. The marker belongs on this paragraph, not on the iPart or a repeating result. It supplies a readable fallback without JavaScript and lets the theme distinguish confirmed empty output from a missing/loading/error result container. It uses no query fields.

{{HOME_TASK_EMPTY_TEMPLATE}}

The query must be scoped to the logged-in user and return all outstanding tasks, with completed tasks optionally included. This helper does not query iMIS or establish the current user itself. It counts every loaded result before text filtering and native visibility filtering, so a search with no matches cannot claim the user is caught up. It cannot inspect records on unloaded server pages: configure the home query to include all outstanding tasks. Each result must retain its existing data-us-task-completed marker; missing or unknown states count as outstanding.

Biscuit appears once the last completion/save and row-exit sequence settles. Reopening or adding an outstanding task removes him. Entering a search hides the illustration and retains normal no-match feedback; showing completed tasks replaces the illustration with matching completed rows. An empty native response uses the No results snippet above, while unmarked empty/loading/error output stays untouched. Native partial replacement and UnionSuiteIqaFilters.refresh() reconcile the state; removing the preset or applying us-report-no-styling removes generated content and restores the fallback paragraph. The three older individual helper classes still work but do not enable Biscuit.

The decorative SVG is hidden from assistive technology; the heading, description and existing polite result status convey the state without moving focus. The hammock settles once over 4.8 seconds; reduced motion disables movement. It fits narrow panels without a fixed page width. Surface, shadow and copy use --bg-subtle, --border, --text-strong, --text-muted, shared type and spacing tokens; Biscuit retains his approved fur and hammock colours. Dark mode inherits the existing semantic palette.

The theme now also recognises declared query iParts, keeping the card border, rounded corners, shaded heading, description and body spacing for both states. Your existing us-list-scroll, us-query-search or us-task-completed-filter class is sufficient. Populated queries without those classes retain automatic detection.

Contact query field definitions

Include all nine output aliases in the IQA Select/display column list when using the unchanged template. Only ContactName needs a nonempty value; “optional” below means the value may be blank, not that a referenced column may be omitted. For each unused optional field, add a custom SQL column with expression '' and the matching output alias, or remove every reference to that field from the template HTML. See the query column requirement. The paths below are illustrative: substitute your actual iMIS destinations.

{{CONTACT_ROW_TEMPLATE}}

To reproduce the previous badge backgrounds with the current client branding, return #F0F3F7 for Delegate or Delegate coordinator, #D1DCE6 for Region, or #F3F3F4 for General. These are example query values; the CSS contains no role-name rules. Explicit hex colours stay fixed when branding changes; a blank colour continues to follow the neutral theme tokens.

Map the query aliases shown above to your actual IQA outputs. ContactName supplies the name; ContactUrl its optional destination; ContactId supplies the optional searchable ID. RoleLabel supplies the optional badge text. RoleColour supplies its exact background as an opaque hex colour, for example #DBEAFE or #5B3F86. Values can vary freely by record; there is no predefined role/category mapping. Three-digit hex such as #ABC is also supported. Case and outer whitespace are ignored; include the leading #. Named colours, CSS functions, alpha hex and invalid/unresolved values use the neutral theme badge.

The shared helper chooses black or white badge text, whichever has the higher contrast against the supplied background. The examples include a light blue delegate badge, a peach region badge and a dark purple coordinator badge with white text. The hex colour controls only the badge; it does not change fonts, spacing, row styling or search. Blank RoleLabel still hides the badge. Keep labels in sentence case so role information is also conveyed in text. Colour attribute changes update automatically, and replacement/opt-out restores helper-owned inline properties without removing unrelated styles.

Updating the previous contact template: replace the RoleKey output alias with RoleColour and paste the updated template, whose article uses . Supply a hex colour or blank for every row. The iPart classes and RoleLabel stay the same. The old named-key palette is no longer used; unchanged old templates receive neutral badges. Keep the query substitution encoded, rather than inserting arbitrary CSS in a style attribute.

WorkplaceName/WorkplaceUrl, Email and Phone provide the optional detail rows. Keep unused aliases in the IQA Select/display column list as custom SQL columns returning '', or remove all their template references, including the relevant optional blocks, link attributes and data-us-search tokens. For example, removing Email requires removing both its detail block (including the mailto link) and its search token. The helper hides rendered blank optional fields including their icons, and hides the contact-details container when both fields are empty; it cannot satisfy a missing IQA column. Invalid/blank URLs become plain text; verify destinations in the source query. Supported destinations are http(s), root-relative paths, ./, ../ and # fragments; contact detail links also support mailto and tel. Query substitutions must stay encoded. The helper does not fetch contact data or save edits, and no new IQA API endpoint is involved.

Long text wraps, names remain prominent, badges use 12px medium-weight shared styling, and details stack when the contact content width falls below 400px. Hover/focus highlights the full row. Reduced motion removes the hover transition; forced colours retain system-colour badge text and boundaries/focus. Row and neutral badge colours come from --text-strong, --text-base, --text-muted, --text-link, --bg-subtle, --bg-sunken, --border, --border-focus and --font-ui. Valid RoleColour values populate --us-contact-badge-bg and --us-contact-badge-text on that badge only; these runtime properties are not author classes. Direct, wrapped, empty-wrapper and nested native iPart structures are supported; apply us-report-no-styling to opt out. DOM replacement and in-place contact changes are reconciled automatically; UnionSuiteContacts.refresh() requests a manual refresh if needed.

is independent of contact styling and search. Add it to a Query Template Display iPart CSS class field to limit its native body to 414px. It also works on notes or other result templates. A bottom fade appears only while more content remains below and updates after scrolling, resizing, search, pagination and native replacement. It disappears at the end or when everything fits.

The class also identifies the query iPart when iMIS omits its list: the card shell remains and the empty message uses natural height, with no scroll fade. See empty-state placement and HTML.

The panel heading/description and generated filter controls stay outside the scroll area. Any optional authored Header/Footer and native pagination inside the panel body scroll with its results. The iPart CSS class field holds 100 characters. iMIS truncates anything longer without warning, and a class cut short simply never matches, so the feature silently does not appear. A panel that also carries tab placement reaches the limit quickly: us-tab-panel us-tabset-home us-tab-tasks us-query-search us-task-completed-filter us-action-home-add-task is 105 characters and loses the end of the action class. Use the us-home-tasks preset above, which applies the same three classes from one 13-character token (or us-contact-methods for the contact methods panel), or move the action out of the field by authoring its button in the Query Template instead. Tab classes cannot move: they place this iPart within a group of sibling iParts, so only the wrapper can carry them.

Keep existing list-layout and Add action classes in the same field. A nonempty Title and updated shared CSS/JS are required for either option. No filter HTML is needed in the iPart Header field; put explanatory text in the iPart description. iMIS creates the extra class div and all native panel/list/card wrappers.

Task template: add to one authored wrapper around each repeating task result. Missing, blank or unrecognised values remain visible; status wording is never used to infer completion. IsCompleted below is a suggested output alias, not a verified database field. Task rows: compact (default) and full

Use these for task lists on the homepage, the agreement page and the case page (owner, 19 October 2026). They are one row in two versions, on the detail row's grid: , the default, and , for a list where every note should show. Both carry us-task--detail as well.

  • First line: the title as plain text, then the High Priority badge (PriorityBadge), the details button (compact only) and the pencil, which is the only way to edit. The badge and buttons stay level with the title's first line, however long the title, and this line never moves.
  • Second line: the date (long form, from the query: Overdue 10 September 2026, Due today), the member (Personal Task when it is the signed-in user), and the task's tags: the first, then a button that shows the rest in place. Blank tags show nothing.
  • The note: compact rows keep it out of sight; a task with a note gets the details button (us-task__details), which opens the note under the row, across the full width, and closes it again. Full rows show it under the title, up to two lines, with a More link when it is longer. The note slides open and shut (instant with reduced motion), and stays in the markup either way, so the header search matches all of it. An opened note shows every tag too.
  • A task with no title (legacy notes, owner 20 October 2026): when TaskTitle is blank, the note takes the title's place and look, up to two lines, in both versions. A compact row keeps the details button only when the note is longer than two lines, and opening it shows the whole note in place; a full row keeps its More link. The tick and details button are named from the start of the note, so the template's aria-label="Complete task: {#query.TaskTitle}" needs no change. A blank title must be truly empty: a title that returns only spaces still counts as a title.

The pencil. On the homepage it is with data-us-task-url="{#query.TaskUrl}", which opens the task in the native popup, as the detail row's title link did. On the agreement page use us-action-agreements-edit-task with data-ordinal instead; on the case page, the case's own edit action. Load the updated zUnionSuite.css, zUnionSuite.js and Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js.

Query columns. The detail row's columns (below), plus PriorityBadge (High Priority or blank) and TaskTags (the task's tag names from the task tag list, comma-separated, blank for none). The query composes TaskDateLabel in long form, with the words Overdue, Due and Actioned. Completion saving, search, Show completed and the completion effect are the same as the detail row's.
