Offline examples use fictional destinations and cannot verify the live editor or report refresh.

The row is one grid at every list width: title, then note, then the member with the date right-aligned beside it. It reshapes on the width of the list rather than the window, because a homepage panel and a tasks page can be very different widths on the same screen. The title keeps an 18ch minimum and a 62ch maximum measure, the note is clipped to one line by CSS so the whole note still matches the header search, and the date is capped at 190px so it can never take the title's width. Blank values collapse rather than leaving empty lines. The query composes TaskDateLabel in full, including the words Overdue, Due and Actioned: overdue rows are red and say "Overdue", so the state never depends on colour alone. DueState only drives that emphasis and is not updated when a staff member ticks a row, so completed rows always show a plain green Actioned date.

{{TASK_DETAIL_ROW_TEMPLATE}}

Completing a task is saved. Supply TaskPartyId and TaskOrdinal — the ID and Ordinal of the task's i4u_UT_Interactions row — and ticking the checkbox sends PUT /api/i4u_UT_Interactions/{TaskPartyId}/{TaskOrdinal}, setting FollowUpActioned to true; reopening a completed task writes false. It is a partial update carrying only ID, Ordinal and FollowUpActioned, so no other field on the interaction is touched. Ordinal and FollowUpActioned use their typed System.Int32 and System.Boolean wrappers, and the call sends the page's RequestVerificationToken with same-origin credentials. It is a PUT, not a POST: a POST would create a second interaction rather than update the one shown.

The tick, the celebration and the strike-through happen immediately, but the row only leaves the list once iMIS accepts the change. If the write is rejected the row returns exactly as it was — original checkbox state, original date label, no strike-through — and shows reading “Not saved. Try again.” for six seconds, announced politely through role="status". Leave TaskPartyId and TaskOrdinal blank and nothing is written: the checkbox behaves locally, as it did before, which is also what the older two-line template does.

Scrolling, resizing, changing search or completion filters, hiding the page or enabling reduced motion stops the celebration immediately. Any pending save continues: the checkbox cannot start another write until that request settles, and the saved completion marker changes only after success. A rejected request still restores the original state and date and shows the save error. Removing a row or opting out of styling also clears its effects; a late response affects only that original row, never replacement query results.

{{TASK_FOOTER_TEMPLATE}}

The two-line template below is local only: its checkbox contains a separate control using role=checkbox and aria-checked. Space, Enter or clicking toggles it immediately. Completing checks the box, draws the tick and strikes the title, then plays the seasonal celebration described below. Unless Show completed is on, a 100ms pause follows the particles, the row slides left over 450ms and its space collapses over 300ms. The shared completion marker updates after the sequence, preventing filtering from cutting off the animation. Show completed brings the row back with an Actioned date based on the browser's current date. Reopening has no exit animation and restores the due text remembered in this instance. For a row initially returned completed, optionally supply data-us-task-due-label on its us-task root with the original due label; otherwise reopening leaves that date blank. No task identifier or save API is required for this local behaviour, and no request is sent. Reloading or native result replacement restores query-supplied state.

Exiting rows are inert; focus moves to another visible checkbox, the open search or the header funnel. Search/completion-filter changes finish active exits and re-filter; removed result rows cancel their animation. Reduced motion skips slide/collapse and finishes active motion. Checkbox targets are 36px, or 44px at 950px or narrower; forced colours retain the check. Applying us-report-no-styling excludes these interactions and styles. Use one completion controller per row: remove an older inline toggle handler before adding data-us-task-toggle.

TaskTitle must return the task subject. If it displays N or IP, correct the source query's alias mapping; those status codes are not task titles. TaskDateLabel supplies the initial visible date. The task note and a literal Status badge are not included in this homepage-style row. Row colours and spacing use the shared surface, text, border, success, radius and font tokens; the footer uses --iqa-inset and the same border/surface/text/link tokens. Page height and scroll windows remain page-layout choices.

Completed tasks start hidden. Show completed includes them while preserving the current search; outstanding tasks remain included. The supplied template's completed titles are muted and struck through; keep its visible status or Actioned date so completion has a text indication too. The query supplies dates, sorting and the last-30-days selection. Return those completed records in the query: the toggle cannot retrieve records omitted by query filters or native paging. Both controls operate only on the currently displayed page and preserve native hidden states, pager totals, row controls and permissions.

Turning Show completed on expands newly revealed completed rows into place over 300ms, moving following rows downward. Turning it off reverses exactly that: the rows stay in the list and collapse over 300ms, and filtering waits for them rather than blinking them out. Current search and native paging still apply. Rapid toggles, search changes, removal and reduced motion cancel the reveal cleanly; keyboard focus remains on the toggle. The completion button exposes aria-pressed and controls the result list. Enter/Space toggle it. The completion toggle is an always-visible header icon beside the funnel, using <i class="ti ti-checkbox" aria-hidden="true"></i> with the accessible name “Show completed tasks”, a tooltip and aria-pressed. Load the theme’s Tabler icon stylesheet. The funnel opens a full-width search field and focuses it; when only completion filtering is enabled, no funnel is shown. Upload updated shared CSS and JS; existing iPart classes need no change. Counts/no-match feedback are announced politely. Each iPart retains its search, completion and disclosure state across body/panel replacements while its class wrapper survives; reload or full iPart replacement starts fresh. Removing an option releases that option and retains the other; removing both or applying us-report-no-styling cleans up generated controls and state. Completion-marker changes are observed, and custom class changes use UnionSuiteIqaFilters.refresh(). When the iPart’s .us-task-completed-toggle button has aria-pressed="true" (Show completed tasks is on), completion skips the row slide/collapse and keeps focus on the checkbox. When the button is off or absent, the normal completion slide/collapse runs. The presence of us-task-completed-filter alone does not suppress animation. No new class or template HTML is needed; upload updated zUnionSuite.js. Completion celebrations are implemented site-wide for the shared .us-task template with its direct [data-us-task-toggle] control, inside native Query Template results. No new author classes or IQA fields are needed. The normal effect is confetti; data-us-color-scheme="dark" on the document root selects moons and stars. On 31 October (the browser’s local date, checked at each completion), bats override either mode. The tick draws in 280ms; confetti lasts 800ms, night particles 1100ms and bats 1050ms. When Show completed is off or absent, a 100ms pause follows, then a 450ms slide left and a 300ms gap collapse. Show completed on retains the row and focus after the celebration. Reopening has no celebration. Reduced motion skips particles and movement. Decorative particles are hidden from assistive technology and cannot intercept clicks. Search/filter changes, removed rows, styling opt-out, scrolling, resizing, hidden tabs and reduced-motion changes clear active effects and settle the local completion state. Upload zUnionSuite.css, zUnionSuite.js and last-loaded zzDarkMode.css together. Confetti colours map through --task-confetti-1/2/3 to accent, brand-500 and success; Halloween uses --task-bats-1/2/3. The night palette --task-night-1/2/3 lives in zzDarkMode.css. The live task example above uses shared behaviour. Persistence remains local-only; this styling change does not save completion to iMIS. The filter itself does not save task state; the separate us-task row template supplies the local-only checkbox and slide-off behaviour described above.

Search uses the existing IQA tokens. The completion button uses --border, --radius, --bg-sunken, --text-muted, --accent, --accent-100, --text-strong and --font-ui; keyboard focus uses --border-focus. Controls wrap on narrow layouts and use 44px touch targets. Reduced motion skips the shared disclosure animation; forced colours retain an explicit pressed border. Native wrapper ownership, nested iParts and no-styling exclusions follow the same search contract.

Keep the three layers separate

LayerRendered markupResponsibility
Outer iPart panelContentItemContainer → optional iPart-class div → panel, with panel-heading and panel-bodyTitle/actions and the overall panel surface. The body has 12px vertical and --iqa-inset (18px) horizontal padding. This inset also contains Header and Footer.
Optional item shellcard.QueryTemplateItem > card-bodyCreated by Display in cards. Card appearance and inner padding; omitted when unchecked.
Repeated contentBulletinCard, or the optional us-list__* content slotsRecord title, author/date and rich text. Works with the checkbox on or off, without adding another shell.

The iPart's configured Header and Footer render once as span.template-header before QueryTemplateSet and span.template-footer after it, both inside panel-body. These get block layout and a 12px separation from the list. Enter their content in the corresponding iPart fields; do not repeat it inside BulletinCard or paste it into each result. These native whole-list fields are separate from us-list__header/us-list__footer, which belong to one item.

Rich-text body: the supplied DocumentBody field already emits paragraphs. Use <div class="us-list__body">{#query.DocumentBody noencode}</div>, not a p wrapper. A p cannot contain other paragraphs; the browser repairs that nesting into empty paragraphs and unpredictable spacing. Narrow layouts: the bulletin's page-layout column keeps the usual two-to-one split beside its neighbour while the row has about 760px, and moves below it otherwise, at full width. The item shell gets a 3px --accent leading edge. Hover/focus uses a 5% accent tint over --bg-surface and a subtle shadow; linked cards lift 2px on hover. Pressing their optional destination link (including its stretched card area) returns the lift to zero, scales the card to 99%, adds a 12% accent tint and an inset shadow tinted with --brand-900. The press transition takes 60ms; release returns to the normal/hover state without delaying native navigation. Unlinked cards and separate body links do not trigger the card press effect. The linked footer uses --border, --text-link, --accent-100 and --text-strong. Existing panel titles, header/footer inset, card padding, typography and native paging stay in control. Member notes, tasks and other iParts retain their styling.

Optional per-announcement destination: add a query output alias named AlertUrl and use the linked template below. This is a proposed alias for your actual URL field or expression, not a built-in iMIS field. Return a trimmed URL for linked records and an empty string for all others. Supported forms are https://…, http://…, a site path starting with one slash, or a nonempty #anchor. Validate URLs in the source query and keep the substitution encoded. Do not add noencode to AlertUrl. Blank, unresolved and unsupported forms produce no visible footer, arrow, extra tab stop or stretched click area.

Place one anchor directly inside BulletinCard, after us-list__body. Its label, accessible name and destination come from the template; edit the visible Read more text and aria-label together if desired. When the label comes from the query, wrap it in before the arrow: a blank value reads Read more, so the query needs no expression for it. The Staff bulletin from System Alerts recipe does this, with posts from the i4u_UT_SystemAlerts table, which also feeds sitewide alerts. An anchor pseudo-element covers its native card, and interactive content inside the rich body stays above it. Normal link behaviour supplies keyboard activation, copying and opening in a new tab. Linked cards receive a visible focus outline; unlinked cards have no pointer action or added tabindex. Avoid wrapping the entire rich body in an anchor because it can already contain links.

No initialization is needed after paging or partial refresh. Reduced motion removes transitions, lift and press scaling while retaining press tint/shadow; forced colours preserve borders and focus. us-report-no-styling excludes this enhancement. us-list--no-shell or legacy template-list suppresses the accent shell, fill and lift; choose ordinary cards for the approved appearance. Display in cards unchecked keeps the optional footer link as an ordinary link without the stretched click area. This recipe targets the supplied Query Template Display/BulletinCard markup; verify the actual query's encoded output and destinations after deployment.

Counts for one record

Add to the section to count for the record the page is showing, such as the agreement on the agreement page. The value is the name of a parameter in the page address (?AgreementID=A107&AgreementNum=123); the loader reads it and runs each query in the folder through GET /api/query by its folder path and name, with a named filter of the same name set to that value. Give every IQA in the folder a filter with that search label (for the agreement trackers, AgreementOrdinal Equal, search label AgreementNum). Without the parameter in the address the status reads "Counts need AgreementNum in the page address." and nothing is queried. Sections without the attribute run their queries unfiltered, as before. Example, the agreement page's coverage counts (folder $/_i4u_/Core/CA/v2/Coverage Trackers, IQAs 01 Covered Workplaces and 02 Covered Members):

Needs Attention for one record · Content HTML
<section class="us-attention"
  data-us-iqa-folder="$/_i4u_/Core/CA/v2/Coverage Trackers"
  data-us-iqa-filter="AgreementNum"
  aria-label="Coverage">
  <header class="us-attention__heading">
    <h2>Coverage</h2>
    <span>Covered by this agreement</span>
  </header>
  <ul class="us-attention__items" aria-label="Coverage counts"></ul>
  <p class="us-attention__status" role="status" aria-live="polite">Loading counts…</p>
  <button type="button" class="TextButton us-attention__retry" hidden>Retry</button>
</section>

Needs Attention query field definitions

For a custom asynchronous click handler that opens an in-page report, prevent the link's default navigation and call UnionSuiteAttention.run(link, asyncAction). Pass the actual card anchor and a function returning the loading Promise. Feedback lasts until that Promise settles; rejection is returned to your handler for its normal error display, and repeated calls for the same pending card reuse the Promise. Ordinary report links need no handler. The generated data-us-attention-opening marker and us-attention__spinner are runtime details, not author classes.

These are the result fields read from each tracker IQA by the shared API loader. The supplied Content HTML has no repeating query substitutions. Select the three required outputs in each IQA; select Link when providing a destination, or return a blank custom SQL value for an unlinked card.

IQA output aliasValueDescription / blank behaviourExample
CountRequiredNonnegative integer, including zero. Displayed as the large number; never calculated from the number of returned rows.12
HeaderRequiredNonempty plain-text card heading.Applications
LabelRequiredNonempty plain-text explanation of the count.Awaiting review
LinkOptionalCard destination. Missing, null, blank or unsafe values produce a static card. Valid relative, ~/ application-root, http(s) and fragment links create a real anchor in the same tab. Use your actual destination./reports

Each IQA must return exactly one summary row, even when Count is zero. Set its filters and security in IQA; without data-us-iqa-filter this loader supplies no prompted filter values or contact context. Enable Available via the REST API for each tracker IQA, and give the signed-in staff users access to the folder and queries. The component uses their existing same-origin session and the page's RequestVerificationToken; no password or API key belongs in Content HTML. See ASI's IQA REST availability guidance.

The loader resolves the folder with DocumentSummary FindByPath, lists its published IQD children with FindDocumentsInFolder, sorts by query Name and executes the first four by DocumentVersionId (with data-us-iqa-filter, by folder path and name through GET /api/query). Subfolders are not traversed. Prefix names with 01, 02, 03 and 04 to control order; the visible Header still comes from each IQA. Extra queries are ignored. It makes two folder requests plus at most four IQA requests, requesting up to two rows from each to catch accidental multirow results. See the DocumentSummary API and IQA execution by document version key.

Zero stays visible. An empty folder shows a distinct message. A failed or malformed query retains its position with an em dash, its query name and Count unavailable; successful sibling cards remain visible. Retry reloads the folder and counts. API failures are never displayed as zero. Requests time out after 20 seconds each. Results are rendered as text, without interpreting Header/Label as HTML. The list exposes aria-busy while loading and the status announces errors; only linked cards enter the tab order, with visible keyboard focus. Reloading an instance after its retry button is focused returns focus to the list once the results are ready.

Content replacement or changing the folder attribute aborts stale requests and initializes the new instance. Repeated theme inclusion is idempotent. us-report-no-styling on the component or an ancestor disables the styling and stops its automatic loading. Counts load once per instance; there is no polling or persistent cache. After a relevant action, call UnionSuiteAttention.reload(sectionElement) to fetch fresh counts; UnionSuiteAttention.refresh() discovers new/reconfigured instances without reloading established ones. This implementation is tested with simulated API contracts; verify folder access and actual IQA responses in your iMIS environment.

The component uses --bg-surface, --bg-subtle/--bg-sunken, --border, --radius-lg, --font-ui, --text-strong, --text-base, --text-muted, --accent and --border-focus. It uses one to four equal columns, wrapping three/four cards to two columns at 700px of component width and stacking at 420px. Page column ratios stay in page layout CSS. Reduced motion removes the hover transition; forced colours retain the count underline and keyboard focus.

{{MEMBERSHIP_STATS_DEMO}}

Matching card heights: cards placed side by side in a Bootstrap row stretch to the tallest card in that row. The shared theme carries that height through each card's own iMIS wrappers and places its footer at the bottom. Bootstrap widths, gutters and wrapping stay in control; stacked mobile cards use natural heights. This applies automatically with one card per column, including the two 8+4 rows shown here. Update both shared CSS and JS; the existing five card HTML blocks do not need changes. Rows with extra visible content or several cards in a column are left alone. The theme manages data-us-membership-row and data-us-membership-stretch; do not add these attributes yourself. Native, empty and classed wrappers, opt-outs, nested row boundaries and partial replacement are supported. The example above uses the captured native Bootstrap/zone/container structure.

Exact IQA filenameRequired result fields
Total Member CountMemberCount, one summary row
Members JoinedGroupCode, JoinedCount
Members ResignedGroupCode, ResignedCount
Member Counts by Financial StatusFinancialStatusCode, MemberCount
Member Counts by GroupGroupCode, MemberCount
Member Counts by CategoryCategoryCode, MemberCount

All queries use the literal folder $/_i4u_/SandBox/CRM Layouts/Home_Page/Stats. Static breakdowns must each count every current member exactly once and reconcile to the total. Event queries must use date >= StartDate AND date < EndDate, with distinct Search Labels StartDate and EndDate. No AsAtDate column is required. Current month-to-date joins/resignations compare with the same elapsed days of the previous month (capped at its month end); 13 September compares 1–13 September with 1–13 August. A prior zero shows “previously 0”. Total members has no historical comparison until snapshot history is connected.

Membership Overview query field definitions

Select the fields listed for each IQA above. The shared loader reads these aggregate results through the API; the Content HTML contains no repeating query tokens. Code values may be blank for an unassigned group. Counts must be nonnegative integers, including zero. These examples illustrate the field formats.

IQA output aliasValueDescription / blank behaviourExample
MemberCountRequiredCurrent member count for the total or one financial-status/group/category row. Each static breakdown must reconcile to the total.1200
JoinedCountRequiredNumber of joins in the requested date interval for one GroupCode. Returned by Members Joined.24
ResignedCountRequiredNumber of resignations in the requested date interval for one GroupCode. Returned by Members Resigned.7
GroupCodeOptional valueMatching group key/display label across current members, joins and resignations. Keep codes consistent across those queries; blank values form the (empty) group.Full member
FinancialStatusCodeOptional valueFinancial status key/display label. Match configured data-us-financial-codes exactly for financial members. Blank values are unassigned.Financial
CategoryCodeOptional valueMembership category key/display label for the category bars. Blank values form the (empty) category.Standard
Inner section attributeConfiguration
data-us-membershipsummary, financial, groups or categories, already set in each template.
data-us-stats-folderRequired folder path above. Preserve underscores; change on all cards when moving the queries.
data-us-time-zoneBusiness date, default Australia/Sydney.
data-us-financial-codesExact returned code(s), default Financial; pipe-delimit multiple codes. Keep consistent on tracker and financial card. Unassigned status is separate.
data-us-group-labelTable's first column. Template uses Member category; edit this and the visible heading for Region, Industry, etc.
data-us-start-parameter / data-us-end-parameterOptional IQA Search Label overrides, default StartDate / EndDate.
data-us-query-total, data-us-query-financial, data-us-query-joined, data-us-query-resigned, data-us-query-groups, data-us-query-categoriesOptional query filename overrides; defaults above.

Unassigned values: null, omitted, empty and whitespace-only codes display as (empty), with their counts retained. The supplied Name columns contain dimension headings, so row labels come from Code. Blank rows coalesce; duplicate nonblank codes, invalid counts and mismatching static totals show an error. Group codes must be consistent across member, join and resignation queries. Event-only groups remain in the table with zero current members. Net change is joined minus resigned, not a historical total-member calculation.

Loading and accessibility: each visible card uses the theme's spinning-circles loader, a minimum loading height, aria-busy, live errors and keyboard-accessible Retry. Cards share results and pending requests within the document; normally eight query requests cover the six IQAs because each event query needs two periods. Paging is checked and capped at 20 pages of 100 aggregate rows, with a 30-second timeout per page. Errors never silently become zero. Retry refreshes all cards with that folder; hidden cards use the new cache when revealed. Tab revisits retain results, and there is no polling.

Same-origin iframe visibility is checked through its parent containers. Offscreen lower cards wait until scrolled into view. To defer the embedded page's own server-side work, also defer its iframe URL until Stats opens; this loader controls browser API queries only. Cross-origin authenticated embedding is unsupported. Late insertion and partial replacement are supported without changing parent iPart panels. us-report-no-styling on a card or ancestor opts out of styling and loading. Optional API: UnionSuiteMembership.refresh() rechecks visibility; UnionSuiteMembership.reload(cardElement) explicitly refreshes the folder's cards. Data is cached for the document's lifetime until an explicit reload/retry.

Theme mapping: --accent provides short tracker markers and the leading category bar; --brand-700/--teal-300 colour the financial chart; --neutral-300 marks unassigned status. Changes use --success/--danger; surfaces use --border, --bg-surface, --bg-subtle, --radius-lg and --font-ui. Counts and labels accompany all charts. The shared spinner respects reduced motion. No new client colours are hard-coded.

Copy or download each inner template below. Install all six in the row arrangement above, or use a data card independently. The heading and history placeholder are optional, with no API requests. Do not install the preview fixture home-stats.js or its captured JSON on the live site.

iPart settings and Query Templates

Each recipe below includes the exact iPart classes and settings; a field definition table with required/optional values, descriptions, blank behaviour and examples; copyable HTML for one result; Header/Footer instructions; and separate copyable No results HTML. The tables are generated from one shared definition source and checked against the actual template tokens during the guide build. Sources are the *-Query-Template.html files in THeme/UnionSuite-Guides/usage/templates/List-Templates/. The bulletin preserves your supplied query substitutions. Notes, tasks and history use clearly listed suggested output aliases: match those aliases in your source query or replace the tokens with its actual substitutions before use.

Every template field needs a selected query column

Every {#query.FieldName} referenced in Query Template HTML must have a matching output alias in the IQA Select/display column list. This applies to visible text, links and data-* attributes, including search-only values. A field used elsewhere in the query, such as a filter, still needs to be selected if the template references it.

For an unused optional field, either keep it selected as a custom SQL column returning a blank string, with the matching output alias, or remove every reference to it from the template HTML. Remove the associated optional block where appropriate, plus any occurrences in href, mailto:, tel:, data-us-search and other attributes. Hiding content with CSS or JavaScript does not remove the query column requirement.

For example, to keep {#query.Email} in the template without supplying email data, add a custom SQL column to the IQA Select/display list, set its expression to '' (two single quotes), and set its output alias to Email. The contact helper can then hide the rendered blank email row. “Optional” describes the value or removable HTML block, not an unselected field that remains referenced.

Ordinary text stays encoded. Only the existing bulletin DocumentBody uses noencode for its rich HTML; do not add noencode to plain-text fields. Optional blocks can be deleted as described in each recipe. Only the optional linked bulletin recipe adds a destination substitution, AlertUrl; configure that selected query output before use. The previews insert fictional data into these same templates at build time; no query execution, filtering, sorting or paging runs in the reference.

{{LIST_TEMPLATES}}

Tokens and behaviour

With Display in cards checked, the card uses --bg-surface, a 1px --border outline, --radius-lg corners (12px) and --shadow-sm. Its card-body uses --card-pad-y/--card-pad-x padding (16px/18px). The item shell itself has no padding, and BulletinCard adds no surface or padding. The gap is --space-3 (12px). Compact items use --space-3/--space-4 padding (12px/16px) and --space-2 gaps (8px); rows have no gap or individual shadow. Titles use --text-strong and 16px semibold --font-ui; body text uses --text-base at 14px; metadata uses --text-muted at 12px. Links and focus use --text-link, --text-link-hover and --border-focus. Timeline dots use --brand-600.

Public component aliases are --list-surface → --bg-surface, --list-border → --border, --list-radius → --radius-lg, --list-padding → --card-pad-y/--card-pad-x, --list-gap → --space-3, --list-title → --text-strong, --list-text → --text-base and --list-muted → --text-muted. Component aliases are scoped on authored us-list wrappers and recognised native ContentItemContainer wrappers. For a deliberate client difference, set the alias on the relevant native iPart or .us-list wrapper in Override.css, loaded last; internal frame/rail/item aliases are implementation details. Avoid introducing separate ad hoc card colours per page.

This is presentation only: native queries, filtering, paging, sort order, record visibility and task completion remain with their existing owners. Native inline display:none and authored hidden states remain hidden; no initialization or refresh call is needed after a partial update. A timeline does not assemble the planned combined activity feed. Paste the separate plain paragraph into the iPart's No results field, and leave Hide when there are no results unchecked to show it. The repeat template does not render zero-result messages, and CSS does not infer missing records.

Static cards have no lift, pointer cursor, tabindex or whole-card click handler. Opt-in Staff Bulletin cards have hover feedback; a valid optional card link also enables the small lift. Use actual links/buttons for actions with clear record-specific names; keyboard focus remains visible. Status badges keep text labels and timeline markers are decorative. Text and metadata wrap; content is not clipped to a fixed height. Forced colours retain visible outlines and rail markers. us-report-no-styling excludes the new native card shell and shared list enhancements on that wrapper and its descendants; other foundation/client styles still apply. Banner Query Template Display wrappers are excluded from the automatic card shell. The theme does not force QueryTemplateSet into a one-column grid; native Display in columns layout remains in control. Custom grids, nested lists and record actions need their own integration review; use this template for a single list, and control page columns outside it.

A value from its own IQA

Implemented in the shared theme (US-QUERY-VALUE in zUnionSuite.js). Fills a few words of authored HTML, such as a count in an iPart's Footer, from a separate one-row IQA through /api/query. Use it where the value is not one of the list's own rows: the case Overview shows its newest three attachments, and its footer counts all of them with a count IQA, instead of a count column repeated on every row.

{{QUERY_VALUE_DEMO}}

The example uses the theme's own code; its count IQA is a stand-in. Add a file refreshes the list, as an upload does, and the count reloads.

  • on any element: the IQA's whole path. data-us-value-filter names the IQA's filter; its value is data-us-value-filter-value, or else the page URL parameter of the same name.
  • Inside it, each takes that column of the first row as text. An empty name takes the first column.
  • The element is hidden until every value has arrived. Author it with hidden too, so it never shows empty before the script runs. It stays hidden for a failed or empty query or a blank value, and for 0 with data-us-value-hide-zero.
  • It reloads when the Query Template list in the same iPart refreshes, for example after an upload. UnionSuiteQueryValue.refresh(element) reloads one on demand.

Paste into: the Query Template Display iPart's Footer field. Change the data-us-value-query path to your count IQA. For the count IQA, use the same source and case filter as the list, with a prompt named CaseNum, and one display column, AttachmentCount: the Ordinal column with the Count aggregate. It needs no SQL expression, alias or GROUP BY.

{{QUERY_VALUE_TEMPLATE}}

Lead first

Implemented in the shared theme (US-LEAD-FIRST in zUnionSuite.js). Add to a Query Template Display's iPart CSS class field, and the result whose row has data-us-contact-lead set to true (any case) or 1 moves to the top of the list. The other rows keep the IQA's order. Use it when the lead is worked out in the IQA from a value on the parent record, which the IQA cannot sort on: a case's lead staff member or primary party, for example. It happens before the list is painted, after every load, refresh, quiet insert and change of lead, and works with grouped contact tiles (the lead is first in the whole list, whatever their group). With no lead, nothing moves.

The lead flag in the IQA — a custom SQL expression aliased ContactIsLead
CASE WHEN [vBoi4u_UT_Cases].[LeadStaffID] = [vBoi4u_UT_Cases_Staff].[ContactId] THEN 'true' ELSE 'false' END

Put it on the row element the template already marks for the contact, as data-us-contact-lead="{#query.ContactIsLead}", and sort the IQA on real columns (group, then name).

The people a case is for: usually one member, sometimes several members, sometimes an employer. Each party is a selectable card in the left third; the chosen party's card fills the right two-thirds, with their details from a second IQA. The primary party is crowned, listed first and chosen first. Over the list sit an always-showing search, a Select toggle and Email parties, with a line saying who the email goes to. Below 800px of panel the preview stacks under the list, and choosing a party scrolls their card into view.

The primary party flag — a custom SQL expression aliased ContactIsLead
CASE WHEN [vBoi4u_UT_Cases].[PrimaryContactID] = [vBoi4u_UT_Cases_Contacts].[ContactId] THEN 'true' ELSE 'false' END

The party details IQA

Each party's details come from a separate IQA (the template names the client's $/_i4u_/Client/Cases - UnionSuite/Layouts/API - Manage Case - Party Details, so each site chooses its own details), run by the theme (US-FIELD-GROUPS, through GET /api/query) when the party is first shown, and kept while the page is open. It needs a prompted filter named ID (the party's iMIS ID, from ContactId) and returns one row. "No details recorded." means the call worked but returned no row: check the filter is a prompt named ID, not a @url value. "These details could not be loaded." means the call failed.

The column names lay out the panel, so the details are changed by editing the IQA's columns alone:

  • Group-Label puts a field under a sub-heading: Work-Employer shows Employer under Work. The sub-heading has a short line before it and a line to the panel edge after it, so it reads apart from the field labels. The name splits at the first hyphen only. A group named with a number (1-ID) starts a new line with no heading. Fields show in column order, groups in the order of their first column.
  • Alert-Title, Alert-Message and Alert-Tone (success, warning, danger, info) show a status alert above the fields: the membership warning. A blank Alert-Title leaves it out.
  • Tone-Label shows the field with that label as a badge.
  • Blank values show a dash; ISO dates read "30 Jun 2026"; true/false read Yes/No; email and web addresses become links. Fields sit side by side where they fit (Email beside Mobile); only a value longer than 60 characters takes the full row.
Suggested columnShowsExample
Alert-Title, Alert-Message, Alert-ToneThe warning: Unfinancial (danger), No longer a member (danger), New member (warning); blank for a financial memberUnfinancial · Paid to 30 Jun 2026. Check eligibility before providing assistance. · danger
Contact-Mobile, Contact-EmailContact details, as text and a link0412 555 019
Membership-Paid to, Membership-JoinedMembership dates2026-06-30
Work-Employer, Work-Workplace, Work-Classification, Work-DelegateWorkStoreperson Grade 2

The same IQA serves employers, so it returns the same columns for them: the member-only groups show dashes. The same standing rules feed the list's MemberStanding and MemberStandingTone; write them once. The case Overview's member card uses the same columns from an IQA filtered on CaseNum, so its Work fields are the case's job.

Case actions

The panel uses three of the case actions in Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js. Their popup pages and CloudToolz endpoints are proposed, so until the site has them, Add party's full form opens an error and the saves fail and say so. A client can replace any of them in UnionSuite-Client/Actions.js.

ActionWhereWhat it does
cases.add-partyThe panel heading (from the preset)Adds a contact to the case: a composer, a popup or both. The composer's list is the active members at the case's workplace, from $/_i4u_/Core/Cases/Layouts v2/API - Manage Case - Contact List - Primary Workplace (CaseNum required, Search optional; at most 20 rows of ID, Full Name, Member Type and Member Category). People already on the case show but cannot be picked. It saves through /cases/quick-add/contact.
cases.set-primary-partyThe crown button in the preview (from the template, with data-ordinal)Saves the case's primary contact, then moves the crown: set data-us-contact-lead on the rows and the preview, and data-us-lead-arrived on the preview for 440ms so the crown pops in. Lead first moves the row. The crown shows for the primary party; the button for anyone else except an employer.
cases.email-partiesThe list's toolbar (added by the script)Emails the parties: while Select is on (us-parties--selecting on the panel body), the ticked; otherwise those the search leaves shown, or all. Read each row's data-us-contact-ordinal.

Behaviour and limits

The chosen card has aria-pressed="true", and the preview is named after the person and announced politely. A choice the search hides stays until another is made; an in-place refresh picks again. Select puts a checkbox on each card (Select all and Clear in the line under the toolbar); turning it off clears the ticks. Transitions follow the theme's timing and honour reduced motion. us-report-no-styling leaves the panel as plain cards. Custom scripts that replace the rows are picked up automatically; UnionSuiteCaseParties.refresh() is available. Without the script every row stays a plain card and the details stay hidden.

Case member card and the case's job

Implemented in the shared theme (US-CASE-MEMBER in zUnionSuite.css; the job choice is US-CASE-WORKPLACE in zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js, 24 October 2026, with current and former jobs from 25 October 2026, and the live jobs IQA, not started jobs and a blank for not about a workplace from 28 October 2026). The case's primary party, first in the case Overview's side column: name, member line and View profile, then their details from a second IQA, laid out by column name as in the party details. A member can hold more than one job, current or former, and a case is about one of them, or about none (i4u_UT_Cases.PrimaryWorkplaceID, a varchar: a job's workplace ID, or blank).

  • The control: when the member has a job, the Work label becomes a button (Work and a chevron) that opens a menu, and the count sits at the end of the heading's line: "2 jobs", or "2 current · 1 not started · 1 former" when they differ.
  • The case's job opens the Work group with its status: a green Current badge and "Since Mar 2020", a grey Not started badge and "Starts Mar 2027", or a grey Former badge and "Worked there Mar 2016 – Jan 2019".
  • The menu lists the jobs, under Current jobs, Not started and Former jobs when they differ (a not started or former job with its dates), then Not about a workplace on its own. The case's choice is tinted with a bar on its leading edge.
  • Not about a workplace (a blank PrimaryWorkplaceID): the Work fields give way to "This case isn't about a workplace." The case create form sets the primary contact's primary workplace, so a blank is a choice; an older case that was never set reads the same way until a job is picked.
  • No jobs: the card is as it was, with no control.
{{CASE_MEMBER_DEMO}}

The example uses the theme's own code with sample rows. Its two IQAs and the save are stand-ins: nothing is saved.

iPart settings

Use a Query Template Display iPart in the case Overview's side column, with the Case - Primary Member IQA: the case's party rows joined to the contact, filtered on CaseNum and sorted primary party first, so its one row is the primary party. In the iPart CSS class field, enter (View profile in the panel heading). Title [[user]] Member. Leave Display in cards and Display in columns unchecked and Header/Footer blank. Rows per page 1. The primary party is changed on the Parties tab (Make primary party), not here.

No results field — Member
<p>No member on this case.</p>

Query Template and field definitions

Paste the HTML into the Query Template field. Every alias below must be selected in the IQA, including the ones that may be blank. Neither of the card's IQAs joins the member's jobs: a member with two jobs would repeat their row. The jobs, and so the count, come from their own IQA.

Change the data-us-fields-query path to your member details IQA (the template names the client's $/_i4u_/Client/Cases - UnionSuite/Layouts/API - Manage Case - Primary Party Details). It is the card's own, apart from the Parties tab's party details IQA, which stays filtered by ID. It takes a prompted filter named CaseNum (not the member's ID), reads the member from the case's PrimaryContactID, and joins the case's job only on PrimaryWorkplaceID (a left join), so its Work-* columns are the case's job, and blank when the case is not about a workplace. Its other columns are the party details IQA's (Alert-*, Contact-*, Membership-*).

{{CASE_MEMBER_TEMPLATE}}

Choosing the case's job

The choice needs PrimaryWorkplaceID on the card (above), the jobs IQA and one CloudToolz endpoint. Until the site has the jobs IQA, the card shows its details with no control.

The jobs IQA: $/_i4u_/Core/Cases/Layouts v2/API - Manage Case - Primary Contacts Workplaces, available through GET /api/query, with a required prompted filter named CaseNum. It returns the primary party's jobs, current, not started and finished, one row each. It is read once per card, as soon as the card arrives, alongside the details. Its rows give the count and the menu. Column names are matched whatever their case, spaces or underscores (Workplace Name, WorkplaceName).

ColumnShowsExample
Workplace ID (or WorkplaceID, ID)The value saved; matches PrimaryWorkplaceID. A row without it is left out, since it cannot be saved.4101
Workplace NameThe menu item's titleDandenong DC – night shift
Workplace TypeIts second line; blank leaves it outDistribution centre
Member Job StatusActive, Not Started or Finished (any case); blank counts as Active. Decides the badge, the count and the menu group.Finished
Job Start Date, Job End DateThe job's dates, shown as month and year (ISO or d/MM/yyyy; other text shows as it is). An active job: "Since Mar 2020". One not started: "Starts Mar 2027". A finished one: "Worked there Mar 2016 – Jan 2019", "Left Jan 2019" with no start, "Worked there from Mar 2016" with no end. Blank dates leave the badge alone.2016-03-07, 2019-01-25
Work-Employer, Work-Workplace, Work-Classification, Work-DelegateOptional: the same Work columns as the details IQA, which fill the card the moment the job is picked. Without them the card reads its details again after the save, and the previous job's fields are dimmed until they arrive.Storeperson Grade 2
  • The save: CloudToolz POST /cases/quick-update/set-primary-workplace with {CaseID, CaseNum, ContactID, WorkplaceID} (CaseID and CaseNum from the page URL, ContactID from MemberId), writing i4u_UT_Cases.PrimaryWorkplaceID: a job's workplace ID, or blank for Not about a workplace. The flow refuses a workplace that is not one of the primary party's jobs. It goes through UnionSuiteAgreements.cloudToolz in Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js. When the primary party changes, set-primary-party should set the new primary's only job, or clear it.
  • After a pick: the card changes at once: the status takes the job's values, and the fields too when the jobs IQA carries Work-* columns (otherwise they are dimmed until the card's details are read again); for Not about a workplace the fields fold away. A failed save puts the previous choice back and says "Not saved. Try again." A saved one reloads the page's other field panels quietly, so Details › Workplace follows, and the card fires us:case-workplace-changed with detail.workplaceId and detail.job (the jobs IQA's row; null for Not about a workplace).
  • Another group: the choice edits the field group named Work. Add data-us-workplace-group="Job" to the card for a group with another name; the jobs IQA's columns then start Job-.

Behaviour and limits

The label has aria-haspopup="menu" and aria-expanded; the menu's items are menuitemradios, the case's choice checked, in groups named Current jobs, Not started and Former jobs. Arrow keys, Home and End move through the menu; Enter or Space picks; Escape closes it and returns focus to the label; Tab, a click elsewhere, a scroll or a resize closes it. A screen reader hears "Workplace for this case: …" (or "This case isn't about a workplace.") after a save. The menu is fixed to the window, so the side column cannot clip it, and opens above the label when there is no room below. In a narrow column the status's dates wrap beside the badge. At 950px or narrower the label and the menu items are 44px touch targets, and the heading keeps its height. Transitions follow the theme's timing and honour reduced motion; a card redrawn after its jobs have arrived takes them at once, with nothing moving. A failed jobs read is not repeated until Work is pressed. Custom scripts that redraw the card are picked up automatically; UnionSuiteCaseWorkplace.refresh() is available.

Case page lists: team, meetings and files

Implemented in the shared theme (20 October 2026: US-CONTACT-TILES, US-MEETINGS, US-ATTACHMENTS and US-PLACEHOLDER in zUnionSuite.css; US-RESTRICTED-ATTACHMENTS and US-QUERY-SEARCH in zUnionSuite.js). The rest of the case page's lists, with their iPart classes from the class reference (us-case-staff, us-case-meetings, us-case-meetings-detail, us-case-attachments, us-case-attachments-recent).

{{CASE_LISTS_DEMO}}

The example uses the theme's own code with sample rows. Its access check and staff actions are stand-ins.

  • Case team: with us-case-staff, the contact tiles sit one per row (the team usually lives in a narrow side column), and every card carries a crown. Put both in the card's us-contact-tile__actions: <span class="us-case-staff__crown" role="img" aria-label="Lead staff member"><i class="ti ti-crown" aria-hidden="true"></i></span> and <button type="button" class="us-case-staff__make-lead us-action-cases-set-lead-staff" data-ordinal="{#query.Ordinal}">Make lead staff</button>. The card with data-us-contact-lead="true" shows the solid amber crown; every other card shows the quiet Make lead staff button, which brightens on hover. A new lead's crown pops in while data-us-lead-arrived is on the card, and the initials circle changes colour gradually.
  • Detailed meetings: beside us-meeting adds who attends (us-meeting__people, an icon then the names), the agenda or notes up to three lines (us-meeting__notes) and the outcome once held (us-meeting__outcome, with a bold Outcome label). Blank parts collapse.
  • Filters that start open: beside us-query-search starts the list's filters open instead of closed. The funnel still closes them, and the panel then remembers that, as it does for every list.
  • Restricted files: a us-attachment row with data-us-attachment-access="restricted" (from the IQA's AccessClass) keeps its actions hidden behind an Unlock button. Pressing it runs the site's check; granted opens the padlock and brings the actions in, refused shakes the button and says so for a few seconds. The check is a hook the site defines once, as the case actions do in ActionDefinitions.js: UnionSuiteRestrictedAttachments.defineCheck(async ({ordinal, row}) => {}) resolves when access is granted, or throws an error with denied: true. Without a check, Unlock reports that the file could not be checked.
  • Compact rows and empty states: in us-case-attachments-recent the attachment rows are compact (a smaller file tile, centred). makes the placeholder short enough for a list's No results field, with the list's add button under it; the list's "no matching results" line steps aside while it shows.

Query Template Display iPart CSS class field
home-welcome-message

This class affects only its immediate panel and that panel's body wrappers. Nested iPart panels keep their styling; a wrapper without a panel is unchanged. Names, branch names, greeting and date are authored or supplied by your existing data integration; this CSS does not identify the current user, calculate dates or change “Good morning” by time of day. Keep one main h1 on the page. No interactive controls, animation or runtime detection are involved; the styling applies immediately to replaced content and works through direct, empty or configured native wrappers. us-report-no-styling on the component or an ancestor opts out.
