06g / LOGIN CREDENTIALS

The native User credentials control, restyled.

Implemented in shared zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js (US-CREDENTIALS), with dark icon variants in zzDarkMode.css. The control itself is unchanged: every field, postback, validator and picker is native iMIS.

{{CREDENTIALS_DEMO}}

Placement: on the contact CCO tab's content page (tab title Login Credentials), add one Content Web User Control iPart pointing at the User credentials control, and put in its CSS class field. Where a contact banner is on the page (the contact profile CCO), add to hide Contact information, which repeats the banner's ID and name; leave it off where the page is opened on its own. iMIS wraps the whole control in a div with that class; no other setting is needed. Leave Display a border around this content unchecked. There is no HTML to paste.

Content Web User Control iPart CSS class field (profile CCO)
us-credentials us-credentials--hide-contact

What changes: Contact information, User credentials, User information and Staff access become cards with white headings, matching the report panels. The credentials fields share one label/value grid, with username and email shown read-only. Locked out, Last sign in, Last active and User class move into their own block: on a wide panel (1300px and up) the first row reads credentials, details, Password Reset; narrower, the block sits under the credentials behind a rule, its values aligned with theirs; at 760px and below everything stacks. Only the rows’ place changes; their controls and postbacks stay native, and a re-render has them moved again. A copy button between the username and its remove button copies the username, confirmed by its green tick alone. Password reset becomes a card beside the fields. Account, Roles and Security groups are three equal-height boxes with no frame around them; each list's header bar holds its title and Add. Add opens a checklist in that box instead of the native popup: search, already-assigned entries ticked and greyed, then Add 2 roles. The choices are read from the native Select Role / Select Group page in a hidden frame, so the same permissions apply (a user who cannot assign SysAdmin does not see it), and the selection is handed to the native add, so iMIS adds it exactly as the popup would; Save is unchanged. If that page cannot be read, Add opens the native popup. Each assigned role has an info button beside its delete button that opens the role’s description under its name (read from GET /api/Role on first use). While the list loads, the checklist shows the theme spinner beside "Loading roles…"; Add 2 roles shows the button spinner until iMIS has added them. After a role or group is added or removed, a warning at the top of User information says the changes take effect when you save; Save or Cancel clears it. Account uses the same box, its title in the header bar. When Disabled is ticked or the expiration date has passed, the Account box turns to the danger tone with a badge for each reason (Disabled, Expired 8/10/2020), updating as the fields are edited. Any login that cannot sign in (locked out, disabled or expired) is also named first on the tab: an error message above the cards, This user can’t sign in., lists each reason, and a lockout adds Unlock account, which runs the native unlock immediately. The Locked out status then reads in red and the native pencil is hidden. Password and Confirm password are marked as a new password (autocomplete="new-password"), so browsers stop filling in the signed-in user's own saved password; a browser may still offer to generate one. The legacy delete images become trash icon buttons: the native image input stays the control, its image is moved out of view and a theme glyph is drawn instead. Staff access levels form a grid of labelled dropdowns.

Staff access appears only for staff users. The control always renders it collapsed and has no setting for that, so the shared script opens it once per render; a manual collapse holds until the next render. The script also adds Set all to to its heading: one dropdown, with no form name so it is never posted, that sets every access level at once and dispatches each native dropdown's change event. When Full staff user is selected and every area is at 0 - No Access, the Staff access heading turns amber with a No access badge, and a warning at the top of the tab (after any can’t-sign-in error) reads This staff user can’t use any modules; it updates as the user class and levels are edited, and Save makes the change real. It shows the shared level, or Mixed levels when the areas differ, and is hidden while the panel is collapsed. Saving is unchanged.

Layout: the component sizes against its own width (a us-credentials container). Below 760px the password reset card moves under the fields; below 520px labels stack above their fields. Tokens: --bg-surface, --bg-subtle, --bg-sunken, --border, --border-strong, --text-strong, --text-base, --text-muted, --danger, --danger-bg and the panel heading tokens. us-report-no-styling on the wrapper or an ancestor opts out of the styles and the script.

Known limits: the no-login state (a contact without credentials, where the control offers to create a user) and the locked-out state have not been captured, so they keep native styling where they differ. The native password meter keeps its own colours. Titles such as "Password Reset" and "Send Email" come from the control and stay title case.

06h / EMAIL SUBJECT TAG

Every agreement email names its agreement.

Implemented in shared zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js (US-SUBJECT-TAG). The Create Communication page itself is native iMIS: recipients, preview, validation and sending are unchanged.

{{SUBJECT_TAG_DEMO}}

Placement: the agreement send page, /_i4u_/Core/Collective_Agreements/v2/Popups/Send-Email-Contacts.aspx, holds the Communication Creator iPart (a copy of iMIS's Create Communication page); put in the iPart's CSS class field. iMIS wraps the iPart in a div with that class; no other setting is needed. There is no HTML to paste. The agreement Contacts panel's Email button (us-action-agreements-email-contacts) opens the page with AgreementID, the recipients query, its filters and ReturnUrl in the address (emailContacts.page in Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js). The page's own body class, us-agreement-comms-page (set on both send pages), widens the Header tab's fields (US-AGREEMENT-COMMS-PAGE, below).

Communication Creator iPart CSS class field
us-agreement-comms

What changes: the field shows Agreement A107 –, from the page address, inside it before the cursor, read-only, so staff know every email is sent with it; they type the rest after it. Every email sent from the page goes out as "Agreement A107 – their subject", or "Agreement A107" alone when they leave the subject blank. Open loads a template as usual: its subject appears beside the tag, and any agreement tag already at its start (another agreement's, or the earlier "(Agreement ID: A107)" and "[Agreement ID: A107]" styles) is dropped. "Agreement" followed by a code with a digit counts as a tag, so a template subject such as "Agreement update – details" keeps its words. Save and Save As are hidden, because either would store this agreement's tag in a template; the bottom Schedule Email button is unchanged.

How it works: the native subject field stays in the form, hidden, and is the one posted, so its validators still check it and show their messages under the visible field. Staff type in a proxy field with no form name, so it is never posted, and each change writes the tag and their text into the native field. The Subject label moves to the proxy. Anything else that fills the native field is taken in: a postback (changing Type, Preview, Send) that brings it back tagged, or a template loaded with Open. The proxy shows that subject without any agreement tag at its start, and the native field gets this agreement's tag again, so the tag is never doubled or stale. The check runs on every page load and partial postback, when the proxy takes focus, and before any click on the page reaches its button, so Send and Preview always post a tagged subject. Save and Save As are hidden by their native handlers (SaveCommunication, SaveAsCommunication), not their labels, which can be translated. Without AgreementID in the address, or with a value that is not an ID (letters, numbers, hyphens and underscores), the page stays native.

Layout: an input cannot carry ::before, so the script wraps the proxy in a span that draws the field (the theme's height, border, corners, hover and focus ring) and shows the prefix as its ::before, from data-us-subject-prefix. The prefix cannot be selected or edited; a click on it puts the cursor in the field. Screen readers get the prefix in words through the proxy's description. The wrapper keeps the native field's classes, so it takes the same width as the fields around it.

Header field widths: on pages whose body carries , From, To, Cc, Bcc and Subject share one width, 720px and never past their column (owner, 6 October 2026), so the subject and its prefix have room and the fields line up. Type keeps its own, shorter width. Without the body class the fields keep iMIS's InputXLarge width. Tokens: --bg-surface, --border-strong, --brand-600, --field-focus-colour, --field-focus-glow, --text-base, --text-muted and --font-ui. us-report-no-styling on the wrapper or an ancestor leaves the subject native (Save and Save As stay hidden).

Tag format: "Agreement A107 – " (owner, 5 October 2026): readable for staff and outside recipients, with no brackets. Square and angle brackets are out: with Advanced Email tracking on, iMIS will not send a subject containing [, ], < or > (Advanced Email, Troubleshooting; the page carries a matching validator). Curly braces were ruled out because they read like iMIS merge fields ({#party.Email}). The tag is text, not a merge field. The planned send hook (TODO.md) can find agreement sends by "Agreement <ID>" at the start of the subject, after any "RE:".

Open: probed on the live send page (5 October 2026). Loading a template reloads the page with the same address plus CommunicationId=<template>, so AgreementID is still there and the script tags the template's subject on the reload.

Mail merge: the recipients query from the address sends one copy per row, and each column is a {#recipient.<column>} field. A template opened here may only use the query's columns: a field the query lacks makes the send fail. The tag itself is text from the address, not a merge field, so it cannot fail a send.

Logging the send: iMIS sends on the server and never gives the page the new communication log key, but the page reloads with "Emails have been queued for processing". On that reload US-COMMS-LOG runs the $/_i4u_/Core/CA/v2/API - Agreement Email Logs IQA for the exact subject, takes the newest row sent by the signed-in user (retrying for a few seconds while the row is written), and inserts AgreementOrdinal, CommunicationLogKey, Audience and CommunicationType into i4u_UT_CA_Communications, unless that key is already there. The address supplies AgreementNum (or @url:AgreementNum in queryparams), Audience=Staff|Members and CommunicationType=Email|SMS; without them, us-agreement-comms--members means Members, otherwise Staff, and the type is Email. The Contacts Email button passes AgreementNum, Audience=Staff and CommunicationType=Email. A status line at the top of the iPart, directly under iMIS's message, follows the work: "Adding this email to agreement A107…" with the theme's spinner (shown for at least 0.7 seconds, so it can be read), then "Added to agreement A107's communications." as a success message, or a warning: "The email was sent, but it could not be added to agreement A107." with the reason. It slides in and its colours ease between the states (instant with reduced motion). While it works, the ways back to the agreement are held, so the email cannot be left unlinked (owner, 6 October 2026): the iPart's « Back (a.back-link-control) and the link in iMIS's message that goes to the same page (Return to previous page, matched on its address; the page's ReturnUrl counts too) are dimmed, skipped by Tab and ignore clicks, then come back when it ends, whether it succeeded or not. The communication log link stays usable. A scheduled send shows a different message and is not logged here.

Case emails

The case send page works the same way (owner, 21 October 2026). Put in its Communication Creator iPart's CSS class field (and us-case-comms-page on the page body for the wider header fields). The case page's Email parties action (us-action-cases-email-parties) opens /_i4u_/Core/Cases/Layouts-v2/Popups/Send-Email-Parties.aspx with CaseID, CaseNum, Audience=Parties, CommunicationType=Email, the recipients query ($/_i4u_/Core/Cases/Layouts v2/API - Communication Recipient List - Parties), ReturnUrl and CommunicationId, the default case template ("Generic Case Email", by its DocumentVersionId; owner, 24 October 2026). The template sets From, To, Type, the subject and the body; the address keeps the recipients. The subject reads "Case C217 – their subject", and after the send the email is linked to the case: CaseOrdinal, CommunicationLogKey, Audience (Parties or Staff) and CommunicationType in i4u_UT_Cases_Communications, which has the agreement table's columns with CaseOrdinal for AgreementOrdinal. The log row is found with the case's own $/_i4u_/Core/Cases/Layouts v2/API - Case Email Logs IQA: the agreement's returns only subjects with the agreement prefix. It needs the agreement IQA's columns (CommunicationLogKey, SentDate, SentByID) and an exact Subject filter, for subjects starting "Case". The case timeline's Emails Sent source reads the link table.

Open lists the case templates (US-COMMS-TEMPLATES; owner, 24 October 2026). On a page whose Communication Creator carries us-case-comms, the heading's Open opens a theme dialog listing the communications published in $/Common/Communications/Templates/i4u/Cases, with a search. Choosing one reloads the page with &CommunicationId= that template, as iMIS's own Open does, so the recipients and the case stay; it replaces the subject and message, and the dialog says so. Browse all folders opens iMIS's Document Browser. A template must be published in that folder to be listed. If the folder cannot be read, the dialog says so and Browse all folders still works. The agreement send pages keep the native Open. Nothing to configure on the page: the folder and the default template are fixed in the theme for now.

Basic and Advanced (US-COMMS-BASIC; owner, 24 October 2026). The case send page opens in basic mode: the tabs go, and the Header and Body show as one section. From, To and Type become read-only lines filled from the open template, with its name and Change in Advanced. Template name, Cc, Bcc, the unsubscribe page, the Recipients and Data sources tabs and "Pass query values to matching report parameters" are hidden. The editor keeps undo, redo, bold, italic, underline, the two lists and the link tools. Its Insert field offers First name ({#party.FirstName}), Full name, Role on the case, Case number ({#recipient.CaseID}), Email and Phone, each shown only when iMIS's own Insert field lists it for the page, so a field the recipients query lacks cannot be added. The Advanced switch, first in the heading, brings back iMIS's own page with every field editable; the choice is remembered in each person's browser, basic by default. Nothing to configure on the page beyond us-case-comms.

The template's header and footer are locked in basic mode (owner, 24 October 2026). Only the rows between the template's <!-- MESSAGE START --> and <!-- MESSAGE END --> comments take edits; typing, Delete, Enter, paste, cut, drop, the toolbar and Insert field are stopped anywhere else, with a short note under the editor. The editor's table resize handles are hidden, and so is its Design / HTML / Preview bar: basic mode stays in Design view, since the HTML view would edit the header and footer as code. Nothing is added to the email's HTML. A template without the two comments is not locked; put them around the message rows of its table (as prototypes/wip/email-composer/templates/Case-Email-Template.html does). Advanced edits everything.

Review and send replaces Send Now in basic mode. It shows the email as each recipient will get it: From, To and Subject, and the body with {#recipient.X} filled from the recipients query and {#party.X} (first, last, informal and full name, email, company, ID) from the recipient's party record, stepping through recipients with the arrows or the list. It warns when a field is blank for that recipient, and blocks the send when the message uses a {#recipient.X} column the query lacks (iMIS will not send it). iMIS's own Preview cannot be used: on a send page with a recipients query it reports no previews. Send to 7 recipients presses iMIS's Send Now; after its postback the theme reads iMIS's confirmation ("… to N recipient(s)") and presses Yes, Send Now only when N matches the review (all recipients, or those with an email). Otherwise it leaves the confirmation open with a warning, on the page and in the popup, for staff to decide. To check a page without sending, set sessionStorage['us-comms-send-dry'] = '1' in the browser: the theme reports the counts and presses nothing.

Who the email goes to

Implemented in shared zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js (US-SEND-RECIPIENTS; option 4 of prototypes/wip/agreement-page/recipients-compare.html, owner, 5 October 2026).

{{SEND_RECIPIENTS_DEMO}}

Placement: a Content HTML iPart at the top of the send page, above the Communication Creator, holding only the div below (paste it in the HTML/source view). Leave Title blank and Display a border unchecked. The theme fills it; nothing else goes on the page.

Content HTML iPart (send page)
<div class="us-send-recipients"></div>

What it shows: one information line: the number of recipients, the count per group, and in red how many will not receive it, with Show recipients. That slides open a table of Name, Role, Group, Email and Status on the page surface, capped at 260px with its own scroll and the header kept in view. A recipient with no email, or with no iMIS ID (the To field is {#party.Email}, which needs one), gets a red badge; everyone else reads "Will receive" in muted text. No match, or a list that will not load, turns the line into a warning that points to the Recipients tab. With no query in the address, or after the send (iMIS's "Emails have been queued" message), the div stays empty and hidden.

Where the list comes from: the page address, as the Email button or a Query Menu report's Email button sends it: query (the IQA path) and queryparams. The theme runs that IQA through /api/query with the same filters: blank values are skipped, as the Communication Creator does, several values pass as "177","183", and @url:AgreementNum becomes AgreementNum. The send itself is unaffected: iMIS applies its own filters. Name each filter by the IQA's own prompt (AgreementOrdinal, CaseOrdinal): iMIS's send ignored @url:AgreementNum and @url:CaseNum and counted every record's recipients (151 and 224 against 1 and 7; iMIS's confirmation, 24 October 2026), while this box, which reads the same filters through /api/query, showed the right number.

Column (alias)RequiredUse
NameYesThe recipient's name; blank shows "(no name)".
EmailOptionalShown in the table. When the query has the column, a blank value is a "no email" problem.
IDOptionalNot shown. When the query has the column, a blank value is a "no iMIS ID" problem.
RoleOptionalA table column when the query has it.
GroupOptionalA table column and the per-group counts when the query has it; blank reads "No group".

Tokens: --bg-surface, --border, --border-focus, --danger, --text-link, --text-muted, --text-strong and the message tokens of .AsiInformation / .AsiWarning; us-badge--danger for problems; the slide is UnionSuiteRecordCards.fold. us-report-no-styling on the div or an ancestor leaves it empty. Still to confirm live: that /api/query applies the URL-sourced AgreementNum filter.

Every row is counted: the theme reads the list 500 rows at a time, up to 10,000, so a list of thousands of members gets its full count. Past 10,000 the count is the query's own total, and the "will not receive it" figure says how many rows it checked.

Emailing an agreement's members

Implemented (owner, 6 October 2026): Email members (us-action-agreements-email-members) opens the member send page. It is an Email Members button, with the send icon, in the Covered Members report's heading (add the class to that Query Menu iPart's CSS class field, after its section classes: us-coverage-totals us-tab-panel us-tabset-coverage us-tab-members us-action-agreements-email-members, exactly the field's 100 characters) and a labelled item in the agreement banner's Actions menu. It emails every covered member, whatever the report's own filters show, so it is drawn in red (the action's danger tone: a red outline button with a pale red hover in the heading, a red item in the menu) to say it matters and needs care. The page is /_i4u_/Core/Collective_Agreements/v2/Popups/SendEmail-Members.aspx. The recipients are every member the agreement covers, from $/_i4u_/Core/CA/v2/API - Communication Recipient List - Members, with the agreement's ordinal passed directly as its only filter, [{"Item1":"AgreementOrdinal","Item2":"123"}] (emailMembers in Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js; owner, 6 October 2026: a value passed through @url:AgreementNum did not filter reliably). The IQA needs a filter named AgreementOrdinal. The address also carries AgreementID, AgreementNum, Audience=Members, CommunicationType=Email and ReturnUrl, as the Contacts send does. With thousands of members a list of names is no use, so the page shows the count only, with a link back to the agreement's Coverage tab, where the members are listed.

{{SEND_RECIPIENTS_SUMMARY_DEMO}}

Setting up the page (the same two iParts as the Contacts send page):

  1. Communication Creator iPart, CSS class field: . The first adds the "Agreement A107 –" subject tag and logs the send to the agreement; the second records the send as Members if the address has no Audience (the button always passes one).
  2. Content HTML iPart above it, Title blank, no border, holding only the div below.
Content HTML iPart (member send page)
<div class="us-send-recipients us-send-recipients--summary"
  data-us-recipients-link="/Agreements_ManageAgreement?AgreementID={AgreementID}&amp;AgreementNum={AgreementNum}&amp;AgreementTab=Coverage"
  data-us-recipients-link-label="View covered members"></div>

Options on the div: us-send-recipients--summary shows the line (count, groups, how many will not receive it) with no Show recipients and no table. data-us-recipients-link adds a link at the end of the line; {Name} in it is the page address's Name parameter, so {AgreementID} becomes A107. A parameter the page lacks, or an address on another site, leaves the link out. data-us-recipients-link-label is its text (default "Open the record"). Both options work on the Contacts send page too. The link opens the Coverage tab; opening its Members section from the address is not built yet (TODO.md).

Sent emails on the agreement

Implemented in shared zUnionSuite.css (US-RECORD-RECIPIENTS) and zUnionSuite.js (US-COMMS-RECIPIENTS), on the theme's record cards (US-RECORD-CARDS). README decision 13: one card per send on the agreement's Activity tab.

{{COMMS_RECIPIENTS_DEMO}}

Audience chips (US-COMMS-FACETS; owner, 6 October 2026): a Members and a Staff chip, each with its count, sit above the search in the panel's filter area; pressing one shows only that audience's sends, and pressing it again shows all. The count beside the panel title follows the chips and the search ("1 of 5 communications · Members"), with a clear that resets both, and the funnel shows a dot while a chip is pressed. There is no class to add: the panel's CSS class field is full, so the chips switch on for any us-records list whose cards carry data-us-comms-audience, as the Communications template's recipients block already does. With one audience in the list there are no chips. They use the agreement panels' chip styles, as the terms and increases chips do.

Placement: a Query Template Display on $/_i4u_/Core/CA/v2/Query Template - Manage Agreement - Communications - Summary, with us-records added to its CSS class field (alongside the Activity tab's own classes: us-query-template us-tab-panel us-tabset-activity us-tab-communications us-records us-query-search). Display in cards and Display in columns off. The template is a record card per send whose details end with the div below; the field list and expressions are in prototypes/wip/agreement-page/Communications-IQA-Build.md (the template is still a prototype: templates/Agreement-Communications-Query-Template.html).

Inside the card's us-record__detail
<div class="us-comms-recipients"
  data-us-comms-query="$/_i4u_/Core/CA/v2/API - Manage Agreement - Communications - Details"
  data-us-comms-key="{#query.CommunicationLogKey}" data-us-comms-audience="{#query.Audience}"
  data-us-comms-recipients="{#query.Recipients}" data-us-comms-delivered="{#query.Delivered}"
  data-us-comms-not-sent="{#query.NotSent}"></div>

What it shows: the first time a card opens, "Loading recipients…" and then a label with the count, a thin delivery bar and a count per state: Opened (Open, Click), Delivered (Delivered, Unsubscribe), Pending (Queued, Deferred, Generated, Resent…), Not sent and Failed (Dropped, Bounce, Spam Report). A staff send then lists every recipient with their address and status, problems first, only a failed status in colour; a row tints on hover, so a name can be followed across to its status in a long list; past 15 the rest slide in under "Show all". Recipients iMIS did not send to (no email, or no contact record) have no row: on a staff send they are the summary's total less the rows returned, so a dropped recipient (who has a row) counts once, as Failed, and a line gives the number. A member send shows the bar and counts from the summary row's own numbers, with no request and no list. A load that fails offers Retry. When the list replaces "Loading", the block grows to its new height (instant with reduced motion).

AttributeRequiredUse
data-us-comms-queryYesThe details IQA path.
data-us-comms-keyYesThe send's CommunicationLogKey; passed to the details IQA as the CommunicationLogKey filter.
data-us-comms-audienceOptionalMembers shows the counts only; anything else (Staff) loads recipients.
data-us-comms-recipients, -delivered, -not-sentOptionalThe summary's counts: the label's total, a member send's Delivered, and Not sent on both.
data-us-comms-shownOptionalHow many recipients show before "Show all" (default 15).

Details IQA columns (by alias, ignoring case): Recipient (required; falls back to Address), optional Address (its own column when the IQA returns it; hidden on a narrow card), Status (the latest event's name, or its code from CommunicationLogEventTypeRef, shown as its name; blank reads as Queued), optional RecipientGroup (a group column appears when recipients span groups). The same markup and styles serve the activity feed's recipients (US-ACTIVITY-RECIPIENTS, a candidate).

Verified live (5 October 2026): the prefix shows in the field, the send succeeds, and the communication log stores the subject as "Agreement A107 – subject".

Known limits: only the Header tab was captured; the Body, Recipients and Data sources tabs are untouched.

Change history

Implemented in shared zUnionSuite.js (US-CHANGE-FIELDS), on the theme's record cards (US-RECORD-CARDS). The standard change history for cases and agreements (owner, 25 October 2026): one card per save, headed by what happened ("Meeting added") with who and when, which opens to list the fields that save changed. It stands on its own: changes are kept out of the activity timeline, where "Meeting added" would repeat the meeting's own card. The list is an ordinary Query Template Display rendered with the page; each card loads its fields through GET /api/query the first time it opens.

{{CHANGE_HISTORY_DEMO}}

Where the changes come from: the change log, i4u_UT_Logging, which CloudToolz flows write through the shared Logging: Log Changes to IMIS flow (LogChangesToIMIS). It holds one row per field: ID (the record: a case's CaseID), Table, Reason (Add, Edit or Delete), Field, Old, New, UpdatedById, ChangeDate, SourceRef, the key every row of one save shares, and ChangeRank, each row's place in its save (1, 2, 3…, as SQL's ROW_NUMBER would number them). Rows written before the log recorded a ChangeRank are left out.

The logging flow writes ChangeRank (owner, 25 October 2026: an IQA cannot filter on a SQL expression, so the list's one row per save is the row ranked 1). Add ChangeRank to the i4u_UT_Logging panel source as a whole number, then add one line to the object each compile script in Logging: Log Changes to IMIS builds:

Compile Log History Objects - Add & Edit — the row's place in its save
    ChangeRank:    getVariable('allChanges').changes.findIndex(change => change.Type === changes.Type) + 1,
Compile Log History Objects - Delete — a delete writes one row
    ChangeRank:    1,

Placement: a Query Template Display on the record's change history IQA (below). In its CSS class field: , after any tab classes. Title [[history]] Changes. Display in cards and Display in columns unchecked, Header and Footer blank, Rows per page 20. No results: No changes recorded yet.

Paste one result's HTML into the Query Template field, and change data-us-change-query to your fields IQA. Every alias below must be selected in the change history IQA.

{{SNIPPET_CHANGE_HISTORY}}

The change history IQA: one row per save

Source i4u_UT_Logging. The expressions use the IQA's alias for it, [vBoi4u_UT_Logging], and read the panel's table, i4u_UT_Logging, for the save's other rows. Filters: ID Equal @url:CaseID (an agreement: @url:AgreementID, as its flows log it), and ChangeRank Equal 1, which keeps each save's first row. Sort on the raw ChangeDate descending, then Ordinal descending; if the Sorting tab offers only displayed columns, add ChangeDate as an extra column (the template ignores it).

Columns: ChangeKey is SourceRef and Action is Reason, both aliased. ChangeDay and ChangeTime format ChangeDate as the Communications cards do: FORMAT([vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[ChangeDate], 'd MMM yyyy', 'en-AU') and LOWER(FORMAT([vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[ChangeDate], 'h:mm tt', 'en-AU')). The rest are SQL expressions:

Event — what happened, from Table and Reason (add a line per table the record's flows log; notes and tasks share a table, and a task logs Category Task)
CASE [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[Table]
  WHEN 'i4u_UT_Cases' THEN 'Case'
  WHEN 'i4u_UT_Cases_Meetings' THEN 'Meeting'
  WHEN 'i4u_UT_Cases_Contacts' THEN 'Party'
  WHEN 'i4u_UT_Cases_Staff' THEN 'Staff'
  WHEN 'Zen_CloudFiles_Storage' THEN 'Attachment'
  WHEN 'Zen_CloudFiles_Notes' THEN
    CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM i4u_UT_Logging l
                      WHERE l.ID = [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[ID]
                        AND l.SourceRef = [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[SourceRef]
                        AND l.Field = 'Category' AND l.New = 'Task')
         THEN 'Task' ELSE 'Note' END
  ELSE REPLACE(REPLACE([vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[Table], 'i4u_UT_Cases_', ''), 'i4u_UT_', '')
END + ' ' +
CASE [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[Reason]
  WHEN 'Add' THEN CASE WHEN [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[Table] = 'i4u_UT_Cases' THEN 'created' ELSE 'added' END
  WHEN 'Edit' THEN 'updated'
  WHEN 'Delete' THEN 'removed'
  ELSE LOWER([vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[Reason])
END
Summary — the record's name (a meeting's title, a note's or task's text, an attachment's new file name), otherwise how many fields changed
COALESCE(
  (SELECT TOP 1 NULLIF(l.New, '') FROM i4u_UT_Logging l
   WHERE l.ID = [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[ID]
     AND l.SourceRef = [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[SourceRef]
     AND l.Field IN ('Title', 'Name', 'Subject', 'Note', 'ActionedNotes', 'FileName')
   ORDER BY l.Ordinal),
  (SELECT CASE COUNT(*) WHEN 0 THEN NULL WHEN 1 THEN '1 field'
          ELSE CAST(COUNT(*) AS varchar(10)) + ' fields' END
   FROM i4u_UT_Logging l
   WHERE l.ID = [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[ID]
     AND l.SourceRef = [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[SourceRef]
     AND l.Field > ''),
  [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[Note])
ChangedBy — who saved it
COALESCE(
  (SELECT TOP 1 n.FULL_NAME FROM Name n WHERE n.ID = [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[UpdatedById]),
  [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[UpdatedById])

The fields IQA: one row per field

One IQA serves every record type, as it reads a save by its key alone. Suggested path: $/_i4u_/Core/Logging/API - Change Fields. Source i4u_UT_Logging; a prompted filter on SourceRef, Equal, with its prompt named ChangeKey (not @url: the theme passes it), plus a Field Not equal filter for each key a flow logs that people don't need (CaseOrdinal). Sort on Ordinal ascending. Available through the REST API.

ColumnRequiredUse
FieldYesThe logged field name. Shown as words: MeetingDate reads Meeting date, PrimaryJob[] Primary job.
OldValue, NewValueYesOld and New, aliased. A date, as the log's US text (10/28/2026 12:00:00 AM) or ISO, reads 28 Oct 2026, with its time unless midnight; anything else shows as logged.
LabelOptionalA name to show instead of the field's own: a SQL expression such as CASE [vBoi4u_UT_Logging].[Field] WHEN 'MeetingType' THEN 'Type' END. Blank falls back to Field.

What a card shows (from the template's data-us-change-action, the save's Reason): an Add lists the values given, leaving out blank ones; an Edit shows each field as old → new, with Empty for a blank side (the logging flow only logs fields that changed); a Delete logs no fields, so its card has no open button. The first opening shows "Loading changes…", then the list; a failed load says so with Retry. The fields stay loaded while the page is open.

AttributeRequiredUse
data-us-change-queryYesThe fields IQA path.
data-us-change-keyYesThe save's key, passed to the fields IQA as the ChangeKey filter.
data-us-change-actionYesAdd, Edit or Delete (also Added, Created, Updated, Removed, Deleted).

Tokens and styles: the card is the record card's change type (--activity-change, the history icon); the field lines are the activity feed's change list (us-record__changes); the slide is UnionSuiteRecordCards.fold. us-report-no-styling on the div or an ancestor leaves it alone. Search (us-query-search) covers each card's headline, summary and who, not the fields, which load later.

Details view: the whole log in a Query Menu

Implemented in shared zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css (US-DETAILS-VIEW; owner, 25 October 2026). The change history can swap its cards for the log itself, one row per field, in a native Query Menu with its Export, as the activity feed's Details views swap the timeline for the notes ledger. The example above has one: press Details view in its heading. Pressed, the cards, their search and footer step aside and the Query Menu shows in the same card, without its own heading; pressed again, the cards return. The choice is remembered per page in that browser, as the activity feed's Details views are. If the page has no detailed iPart for it, there is no toggle.

  1. Add to the change history's CSS class field. On the case page's Changes the classes then run past the field's 100 characters, so use the preset instead, which includes it.
  2. Right under it on the same page, add a Query Menu (IQA report) iPart on the change log, one row per field: source i4u_UT_Logging, filtered on ID Equal @url:CaseID, with columns such as ChangeDate, the Event expression, Field, Old, New and the ChangedBy expression, sorted on ChangeDate descending then Ordinal ascending. Its CSS class: , plus the same tab classes as the change history (on the case page: us-tab-panel us-tabset-activity us-tab-changes). Give it a title ([[history]] History): it is hidden while the Query Menu shows in the change history's card, but keeps the iPart identifiable in the editor.

The key after us-details-view- pairs the two (history here); the toggle wears the theme's heading-toggle "on" state and is announced as pressed or not, controlling the Query Menu's container. Any list iPart can have a Details view the same way, with its own key.

Known limits: not yet run against the live log. FORMAT needs SQL Server 2012 or later. A field label set in the Label column applies wherever the fields IQA is used.

White outlined panel actions, including Add Job and Add New Address, retain their 1px theme border in panel headers. The shared action layout honours --button-border from us-outline-button; hover, pressed and keyboard-focus feedback remain available. Update zUnionSuite.css; existing action classes and templates need no changes.

07 / NATIVE CONTROLS

Use the buttons iMIS already renders.

Implemented in the shared CSS. Existing pages need no new button class, markup migration or JavaScript.

Where to paste: button classes belong in the actual button/link's HTML class attribute. In Content HTML, use its HTML/source editor; in a Query Template Display, use the authored row template. Existing native buttons keep their controls and handlers. Wrapper recipes explicitly identify their container placement.

Button examples, classes and templatesClick a class chip to copy · expand HTML template for a complete snippet

Dedicated button types, visual examples and recommendations · Approved button spinner and comparison examples

Choose by purpose

TypeClasses / contextWhen to use
PrimaryTextButton PrimaryButtonOrange. Main task: Find, Save or Create.
SecondaryTextButtonNavy. Default supporting action.
Supporting outlineTextButton us-outline-buttonAdd note or Assign beside a dominant action.
Low emphasisTextButton LinkButtonView history or secondary navigation.
DestructiveTextButton DangerButtonDelete/remove data. The handler owns confirmation or undo.
Positive confirmationTextButton SuccessButtonApprove or complete; not every Save needs green.
WarningTextButton us-warning-buttonConsequential, non-destructive actions.
Compact record actionTextButton us-icon-buttonRepeated row actions; give each an accessible record-specific name.
Panel utilityExisting report filter/export/expand controlsOperate on the report; keep compact and subordinate.
Banner actionus-banner__action inside the banner surfaceRetains inverse banner styling. See banner examples.

Classes belong on the actual button or link, except documented native container recipes. The examples below include class chips and copyable templates. One dominant action per local task area; sizes, groups and availability are separate from purpose.

{{BUTTON_DEMO}}

Native message containers combining AsiWarning and AsiWarningIcon (including ContentRecordPublishPanel), or equivalent information/success/error classes, reserve space for their background icon. No extra class is needed. Standalone icon spans and inline messages retain their native layout. See the publish-notification example in the message reference.

Checkboxes and radio buttons

Native checkbox and radio inputs automatically use an 18px control and the primary brand colour (--brand-600), including ordinary forms and IQA filters. No extra class is needed. Add Contact account-type radios and Create an account labels use centred paired layouts. Browser-native checked, indeterminate and disabled behaviour is retained, with a visible keyboard focus ring. Telerik replacement widgets and recognised custom switches retain their own sizing.

Place in your form; keep native generated markup on iMIS pages
<label><input type="checkbox" name="updates"> Membership updates</label>
<label><input type="radio" name="contactMethod" value="email"> Email</label>

Native field validation

Native ValidationError text uses the danger token; iMIS still controls when messages appear. Shared UnionSuiteValidation marks the related enabled field only while a non-empty error is visible, adds aria-invalid and links the error through aria-describedby. It uses native controltovalidate when present, otherwise a single unambiguous input in PanelFieldValue. Existing accessibility attributes are restored when the error clears. No new author class is needed and no validation or submit handler is replaced.

Required

Native form Save and Cancel

The global mapping follows native iMIS roles: TextButton is navy; add PrimaryButton for orange primary emphasis. UsePrimaryButton applies orange to descendant TextButton controls. Native Save carries PrimaryButton and Cancel remains a plain TextButton; no form-specific override is required.

Shared loading presentation

UnionSuiteBusy.show(button, options) is the shared presentation helper used by native Find, Save, Sign In and UnionSuiteButtons.run. It supplies the same simple ring, disabled-opacity matching, positioning and restoration. The ring is placed again on every frame while it shows, so it stays with its button when the button moves without resizing (the sign-in card re-centring as its message closes) or its label changes. It does not submit, validate or change disabled state. Default mode shows a leading ring and existing text when they fit, otherwise a centred ring; mode: 'center' forces the ring alone and mode: 'label' retains the label. Use UnionSuiteButtons.run for custom Promise-based operations; only use show/clear when your integration already owns a verified request lifecycle.

Presentation only — inside an existing request lifecycle
const indicator = UnionSuiteBusy.show(button, { mode: 'center' });
// On completion, error or cancellation:
indicator.clear();
// Alternatively: UnionSuiteBusy.clear(button);

Native adapters remain separate: UnionSuiteIqaBusy follows ASP.NET begin/end requests; UnionSuiteSaveBusy and UnionSuiteSignInBusy observe their specific native disabled transitions. A disabled button elsewhere is not automatically assumed to be loading.

Taskbar search comparison (trial) shows current and proposed styling with fictional records and simulated states. No production taskbar changes are included; legacy styling must remain the fallback.

Choices and dropdowns reference: Approved orange checkbox/radio states and themed Telerik autocomplete lists are now in shared CSS. The legacy column remains a screenshot reconstruction. Native select menus retain browser rendering.

Calendar reference shows the approved calendar and month/year styling, now included automatically in shared CSS for RadCalendar and RadCalendarMonthView. The month heading and native SelectTodayLink shortcut have subtle raised borders, no hover underline, and inset pressed states; the heading uses 4px padding and a chevron. Navigation arrows highlight on hover and press. Native rcToday is outlined, rcSelected is filled primary, and rcFocus remains keyboard focus. Month/year OK uses white text on accent, reversing to accent text on a white surface on hover. Today/OK/Cancel have inset pressed shading. Tokens follow client overrides. Query Template Display shell

Consistent header heights: themed panel title/action rows and the Needs Attention heading share --panel-header-min-height: 44px, --panel-header-padding-block: 6px and --panel-header-action-height: 32px. Contents are vertically centred; labelled header actions use the shared minimum action height. Panel titles and Needs Attention also share --panel-header-font-size: 14px, --panel-header-font-weight: 700 and --panel-header-line-height: 1.4, using the theme UI font. These settings apply to the same theme-managed shells, not every native iMIS heading. No additional author class or HTML is required. Existing panel/iPart classes stay on their immediate owner (including the extra iPart CSS-class div). The native .panel-description remains a separate row inside the shell, immediately after .panel-heading. It now uses the body surface (--iqa-surface), muted text (--iqa-muted), italic 12px text with 1.6 line height, and 8px top / 12px bottom / --iqa-inset horizontal padding. A full-width 1px bottom divider using --iqa-divider separates the description from the panel content. The heading keeps its standard padding and bottom divider even when a description follows. Long descriptions wrap and add body height without enlarging the shaded title bar. No extra separator element or markup changes are required. An empty heading remains hidden, with the description retaining rounded top corners and the same 8px top padding. Headers can grow for wrapped titles/actions and enlarged text, so equal height applies while content fits the shared minimum. At 950px or narrower the defaults become 56px for headers and 44px for labelled actions, preserving larger touch targets. Throughout the theme, touch sizes go by window width ((max-width: 950px)), not pointer type: browsers on touchscreen laptops can report the touchscreen as the only pointer even while a mouse or trackpad is in use, which gave docked laptops touch-sized controls. Tablets wider than 950px keep the desktop sizes. Override these tokens together in the client stylesheet to change the shared sizing; us-report-no-styling still opts out. See the live Needs Attention and Staff Bulletin examples in this guide.

Header action order: text links sit on the left of the action group, followed by custom icon actions and available filter/download/expand icons, then labelled action buttons at the far right. The same shared ordering applies to IQA reports. DOM order matches visual order for keyboard users; native controls and handlers are retained. Existing author classes need no changes. Load updated shared CSS and JS together. The action group aligns right with the body content edge using the same --iqa-inset, and wraps on narrow panels. Native heading clearfix pseudo-elements are suppressed on theme-managed flex headers so they cannot add an empty trailing gap after the actions. Upload updated zUnionSuite.css; no author class or markup change is needed.

Busy states — live examples

{{BUSY_DEMO}}

Try success and failure with the busy helper above. In your verified async handler, pass the actual button and a function returning the real operation Promise. Keep success/error messages in an accessible status region.

Async integration — replace saveContact with your real operation
try {
  await UnionSuiteButtons.run(button, () => saveContact(), 'Saving…');
  statusElement.textContent = 'Contact saved.';
} catch (error) {
  statusElement.textContent = 'Could not save. Please try again.';
}

Busy buttons: the simple ring is approved. Place on a decorative span inside the button, with aria-hidden="true". The example above includes copyable markup. Set aria-busy="true" and prevent repeat activation while working; retain meaningful busy text. The action handler must reserve the current width before changing the label, restore availability on success or error, and announce the outcome separately. The theme supplies the ring and the explicit UnionSuiteButtons.run helper. It preserves width and original nodes, guards repeated calls, and restores the control after resolution or rejection. Connect it only to verified asynchronous operations, not unmodified native postbacks. When the loading label and ring cannot fit within the original button width, the helper shows only a centred spinning ring and preserves the accessible name. Icon-only and native input buttons also use a centred ring; input indicators overlay the original control. Do not replace native postback handlers.

Filled orange, navy, danger and success actions now share surface reversal on hover and a tinted pressed state. A subtle inset shadow on hover deepens while pressed, without moving the control. Orange keeps dark hover text for readability. Compact icons keep subtle hover feedback; while pressed they gain a stronger tint, matching border and small inset shadow. Link-style actions retain subtle feedback. Outlined supporting and warning styles are implemented. Use TextButton us-outline-button for supporting commands, or TextButton us-warning-button for consequential non-destructive actions. Apply modifiers to the actual button, not its iPart, and choose one semantic/emphasis modifier per button. Green, red and amber use independent semantic tokens; changing the five brand seeds does not change their meaning.

Navy secondary actions using plain TextButton or btn reverse to a white surface with blue text and a solid blue border on hover. Pressing adds a pale brand tint; keyboard focus adds an outer ring. No additional class is needed. Compact us-icon-button controls, semantic actions and specialised sign-in buttons keep their existing treatments.

TextButton and btn are the base classes. The theme updates their colours while keeping native sizing, grouping, icons and handlers. It supports anchors, buttons and submit inputs, including the native primary/accent paths inside RadGrid.

Existing classes and their colour roles
Native classTreatmentToken mapping
TextButton / btnDefault navy secondary action--brand-800, --text-inverse
PrimaryButtonOrange primary action--accent, --text-on-accent; accent border on hover
AccentButtonExplicit accent; also supported as the native wrapper settingSame accent tokens as PrimaryButton

UsePrimaryButton applies the primary treatment to descendant TextButton controls. AccentButton works directly on a control or as a native wrapper, and wins when combined with primary. Keep base classes on controls rather than adding TextButton or btn to an entire iPart.

Sizes, groups and specialised controls

Native SmallButton, MediumButton, LargeButton, FullWidthButton and their Use…Button container settings retain their existing layout. So do btn-group, vertical/justified groups, toolbars, dropdowns and carets. btn-lg, btn-xs, btn-group-xs, btn-block and btn-check occur in native selectors, but several are only contextual references; this theme does not invent missing Bootstrap utilities.

SignInButton and AddToCartButton keep their context behaviour; controls that also carry a base class receive ordinary colours. Facebook/LinkedIn sign-in and FeatureButton keep their specialised presentation. Upload, popup, pager, combo-box, sprite and Easy Edit controls retain their native skins. See the complete native-class inventory.

Secondary, warning and availability

The inspected native sheets have no dedicated SecondaryButton or WarningButton rule, nor btn-secondary, btn-warning, btn-danger or btn-success variants. These are not implemented aliases. Use the existing LinkButton for a lower-emphasis action. Use the implemented us-outline-button and us-warning-button modifiers for outlined supporting and warning actions.

Danger and success use semantic colours independently of branding. Success uses the darker green for readable white text. Report Find and filled report actions use the accent; Export stays a neutral utility, and banner actions keep their inverse context styles. Native account-banner/toolbar context rules can also take precedence.

Hover/pressed, keyboard focus and native disabled states are covered, including disabled, disabled fieldsets, disabled/aspNetDisabled classes and aria-disabled="true". Native motion preferences remain in place. CSS does not disable a handler: keep native availability logic; custom commands require actual disabled, and unavailable links must have no live destination or handler.

Action builder

Create a popup, custom-function or navigation action with button, iPart heading, report row and Actions menu appearances.

Full action configuration and installationOpen standalone builder ↗

Scroll within the builder to reach all settings and output. Use Save builder draft before closing or reloading; Load draft restores it in either version. The standalone link starts a separate draft.

{{ACTION_BUILDER}}

Use the generated action

  1. Review the validation and Setup tab. Supply the real editor URL or custom function, record context and any permission provider. The preview uses sample data and simulated operations.
  2. Copy JavaScript or download the registration. Append it to client Actions.js, loaded after zUnionSuite.js and Scripts/ActionDefinitions.js. A registration snippet does not replace the whole file.
  3. Copy HTML / class. For a panel heading, paste the class into the iPart’s CSS class field and retain a Title. For a row, menu or standalone control, use the inner author HTML and replace the sample attributes with encoded record values. Select every referenced field if adapting it to an IQA Query Template.
  4. Verify on the site. Check the actual popup/function, server permissions and refresh targets. The builder does not install actions or run live iMIS requests. Use the complete registration template for custom context resolvers and callbacks beyond the form’s common settings.

For a clipboard action with its own icon and success animation, use the reusable copy button.

07b / ACTIONS

One icon for each action.

Implemented styling for Tabler outline icons on existing native buttons. Keep the same meaning in record rows, toolbars and Actions menus.

Where to paste: TextButton us-icon-button goes on the outer button/link. ti ti-pencil, for example, goes on an inner icon element. Both belong in authored HTML/source or a query display template. Add aria-label="Edit [record name]" to an icon-only control and aria-hidden="true" to its decorative icon. Replace bracketed text with the encoded current record value.

The icon and colour do not create an action. Retain the verified iMIS destination, command, permissions and refresh behaviour. Sample commands are disabled in the copied templates until connected. An input control cannot contain an icon; preserve the existing control type rather than replacing its postback behaviour.
{{ACTION_DEMO}}

Presentation rules

Labelled TextButton/btn controls retain native sizing and their selected button colours. Their direct ti child is 18px with an 8px gap. The optional us-icon-button modifier provides a transparent 36px square, increasing to 44px at 950px or narrower. It uses brand text; on hover and keyboard focus the icon turns --icon-hover-colour (the accent's darker shade, --accent-700) on --icon-hover-bg (a 14% accent tint), the one hover every icon-only control shares: row actions, heading icon buttons, copy buttons and contact copy icons. Dark mode re-points both tokens to the lighter accent; DangerButton and SuccessButton keep their semantic colours and tinted hover surfaces. Keep the existing focus and disabled states.

Use View → Edit → More for ordinary rows. Put destructive actions last. Archive retains the record; Unlink removes an association; Delete removes the record. Use a real a with its verified href for navigation, and a button type="button" for a command or popup. An Actions dropdown should include visible text alongside its icon. Adding a ti child to us-banner__menu-item or us-panel-action aligns it with the shared icon size.

Shared zUnionSuite.js supplies a viewport-positioned tooltip for enabled icon-only controls using their aria-label. It appears on hover or focus, stays available while the pointer is over its label, and closes with Escape. The label is pointer-transparent: buttons and links underneath still receive hover and clicks. Moving onto another icon updates the label for that control immediately. The label remains accessible without JavaScript. No tooltip or class guesses a business action, and the tooltip listeners continue to work on newly rendered controls after a partial refresh.

Tabler Icons 3.31.0 is bundled under the MIT licence. This portable guide embeds the font; the live theme loads it through Tabler.css. See the bundled licence.

07c / NAVIGATION

Keep the existing navigation classes.

The Orion sidebar now uses brand-800 for its primary surface, with a slightly lighter submenu surface. Top-level items receive the lighter submenu surface on hover or keyboard focus. The collapsed flyout title also uses --nav-submenu-bg, derived from --brand-800, so client seed overrides colour the title and submenu together. Clickable collapsed root titles (including items without a submenu) use --nav-hover-bg and an underline on hover or keyboard focus, and --nav-selected-bg with inset shading while pressed. Empty, hash-only, JavaScript-placeholder and aria-disabled links do not receive this destination-link feedback. Matching uses the root structure and href, not custom module classes or generated IDs. Its white heading is semibold, with a 1px white divider at 14% opacity inset 20px on each side. These collapsed-only title rules are maintained directly in 99-Orion.css; deploy that file with zUnionSuite.css. Load the client Override.css after the main theme CSS; no additional navigation class or JavaScript is needed. Submenu hover is subtle; the current page uses a stronger tint, semibold text and an inset accent bar. Icons use accent on hover and keyboard focus. Existing indentation, native expand/collapse and flyout positioning are retained. These rules are scoped to SideBarPanel and RadTreeView_Orion; image-based custom icons retain their source artwork. Verify expanded, collapsed and nested navigation live. The custom staff icon mappings from UTStaff.css are now part of zUnionSuite.css. They use the bundled Tabler Icons 3.31.0 1.5px outline font (tabler-icons-300). Upload Tabler/fonts/tabler-icons-300.woff2 with updated Tabler.css and zUnionSuite.css. The separate family applies only to these custom root navigation icons; other Tabler icons retain their standard 2px outlines. The existing standard family is a fallback if the thin font cannot load. The portable guide embeds both fonts and requires no font download. Custom root glyphs render at 32px to match the existing 32 × 32px flex slot. This applies to Home, Organising, Cases, Agreements, Calls, Committees, Travel and Integrations in normal, hover, focus and selected states. Slot width, label alignment, native sprites and expand/collapse chevrons are unchanged. Upload updated zUnionSuite.css; no class or markup change is needed.

Keep Nav-Icon plus one module class in the staff navigation item's CSS class setting, without dots. iMIS applies Nav-Icon-wrapper and the corresponding module wrapper to the root row inside .sub-nav-body .RadTreeView_Orion. Existing configured items need no extra HTML or ti class. These are navigation settings, not banner, page or iPart classes.

Normal glyphs use --brand-100. Hover, keyboard focus within the row, rtSelected and child-is-selected-wrapper use --accent. Icons use a 24px glyph in a fixed 32px slot, at the font's native weight 400. Transparent backgrounds prevent Orion's generic sprite highlight from becoming a solid square behind the glyph. Native navigation labels, flyouts and expand/collapse controls retain their own behaviour. In the minimised sidebar, Orion hides root labels with transparent text, leaving only icons visible; labels return in native hover flyouts or when expanded. The theme deliberately leaves root label colour to Orion so it does not override that hidden state. No extra class or JavaScript is required.

The rules target only these eight custom root wrappers. Native Orion sprite icons—including a configured NotepadLink for Hub Forms—keep their existing rendering. Engagement has no leading icon in the supplied menu examples. There is no blanket Font Awesome replacement: changing the font alone cannot translate its codepoints.

Deploy and maintain

Upload the updated zUnionSuite.css and retain the root Tabler.css, Tabler/tabler-icons.min.css, Tabler/fonts/tabler-icons.woff2 and Tabler/LICENSE. The root shim imports the icon pack through relative paths, so renaming the deployed theme folder does not require changing those paths. This feature requires CSS and the font; it does not need JavaScript.

Maintain the US-STAFF-NAV-ICONS block in the shared theme. Once deployed and verified, remove the duplicated staff-icon rules from any separately loaded legacy UTStaff.css; do not remove unrelated rules from that file. The repository's legacy copy remains a reference. Recheck collapsed and expanded navigation, hover, keyboard focus and selected-child states on the live page.

Icons inside authored HTML Separate from the navigation wrapper recipe

For a decorative icon beside visible text in authored HTML, use both Tabler classes on the icon element and hide the redundant icon from assistive technology. An icon-only action still needs an accessible name.

07d / HEADINGS

An icon beside a section title.

Implemented in shared zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css. Start an iPart's title with an icon name in double square brackets (a Tabler icon, or one of the theme's own icons) and the panel heading shows that icon before the title. Optional: titles without the token are unchanged.

{{PANEL_TITLE_ICONS_DEMO}}

Where to type it: at the very start of the iPart's Title field, not the CSS class field (which iMIS truncates at 100 characters). Use the icon's Tabler name without its ti- prefix, or a theme icon's us- name, in lowercase, then a space and the title. Works on every iPart that renders a panel heading: Query Menu, Query Template Display, Content HTML with a title, and Panel Editor panels.

iPart Title field
[[user]] Contact summary

Recommended icons

One meaning per icon, so the same kind of section looks the same on every page. Click a token to copy it. Any other name from the bundled Tabler set also works; browse them at tabler.io/icons.

Suggested title tokens by kind of section
SectionToken — click to copy

How it works

  • Only a leading token is read: [[, a name of lowercase letters, digits and single hyphens, then ]], at the start of the title. Brackets anywhere else in a title are left alone.
  • The token is always removed. A name the bundled Tabler set (or, for a us- name, the theme's own set) does not have leaves no icon and no gap, rather than showing [[…]]. Check the heading after typing a new name.
  • Decorative only: the icon has aria-hidden="true", so screen readers read the title alone. Everything the theme builds from a title, such as a search box's label, sees the title without the token.
  • Appearance: 16px beside the 14px title, in the muted text colour (--iqa-muted, falling back to --text-muted), 8px before the title. Dark mode follows the token. Headings with a count badge, heading buttons or a panel tone keep them as before.
  • Timing: the US-PANEL-TITLE-ICONS block runs first in zUnionSuite.js and converts titles as they arrive, including panels replaced by partial refreshes and in-place CCO tab switches.
  • Where the raw token shows: places iMIS shows the title without the theme's script, such as Easy Edit (deliberately left raw so authors see the setting) and RiSE's content lists. Accepted by the owner, 26 September 2026. us-report-no-styling on a panel also leaves its title raw.
  • Not covered: banner titles, CCO tab labels, section switcher buttons and Needs Attention headings; they are authored separately.

The theme's own icons

Icons the Tabler set does not have are drawn by the theme as inline SVG and named with a us- prefix, so they never clash with a Tabler name. Use them anywhere a Tabler name is accepted: a title token ([[us-building]]), or theme code through UnionSuiteIcons.create(name, className), which returns a Tabler glyph for any other name. Each is a 1em square in the current text colour, so it takes the size and colour of the text around it like a glyph.

The theme's own icons
IconToken — click to copyUsed by
Organisation record: a block with a lower wingTaskbar search results (company records), coverage rules (Workplace)
Person record: head and shouldersTaskbar search results (person records)

Adding an icon: add an entry to ICONS in the US-ICONS block of zUnionSuite.js: a us- name and the SVG's inner markup (paths, circles), drawn on Tabler's 24px grid in strokes only. The theme supplies the <svg>, stroke colour, round caps and joins, so the new icon sits with Tabler glyphs. Add a row to the table above. Line weight is 2, as Tabler; set --us-icon-stroke on a container to change it (the taskbar uses 1.7). The markup is static; never build it from data.

Deploy by uploading the updated zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css. The icons need the Tabler files the theme already requires (Tabler.css, Tabler/tabler-icons.min.css and its font). Use icons on every panel of a page or on none, so a missing icon never looks like an error.

08 / COPY & ADAPT

Template library

Copy only the snippet for the field indicated. The guide itself is a standalone document, not an iMIS content item to paste.

Shared setup for all banner HTML: load zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js once; put us-banner us-banner-collapsible on the content iPart; optionally put us-banner-page on the page. The outer iPart wrapper is supplied by iMIS, so these snippets begin at us-banner__surface.

Reusable copy button

{{COPY_DEMO}}

Place the snippet in a Content HTML iPart or inside your authored component. Add to a native button type="button". Set to the target element's unique ID, without #. Divs, spans and other text containers are supported; input and textarea targets use their current value. To copy something other than what the target shows, set on the button; the target still flashes (the taskbar's Recents copy the full IQA path while their row shows only the folder). Keep the button outside its target. Supply a meaningful aria-label and title; shared CSS draws the icons, so no icon font or SVG markup is required.

Requires updated zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js once per document; the legacy banner-only embed does not include this control. This static example has no IQA substitutions. In a Query Template, select every field referenced in the target text and any unique ID expressions, and ensure IDs remain unique across repeated results. Whitespace at the ends is trimmed; containers copy plain textContent, including hidden descendant text, without HTML formatting. Choose a target containing only what should be copied. Targets must be in the same document; missing, duplicate, empty or button-containing targets produce an error.

On successful clipboard write, the copy icon fades/scales into a tick for 1.6 seconds and the target receives a 700ms accent outline flash. Screen readers hear “Copied”. Failure retains the copy icon with an error outline, a “Could not copy” title and an announced manual-copy instruction. Pending clicks are ignored; keyboard focus stays on the button. Native disabled and aria-disabled are respected. With reduced motion, the tick changes immediately and the target shows a static outline briefly. Browser clipboard restrictions may prevent copying; the legacy fallback restores selection and focus. The delegated handler supports partial replacements, multiple buttons, repeated clicks and repeated script inclusion.

Appearance follows --text-muted, --bg-sunken, --success, --danger, --accent and --radius-sm. Banner buttons inherit --banner-text and a translucent hover. Their idle copy icon is softened to 55% opacity, returning to full opacity on hover, keyboard focus and success/error feedback. Inside the banner ID chip (us-banner__record-copy) the icon is hidden until the chip is pointed at; see the case/agreement banner. The click target stays the same size. Optional local aliases --copy-colour, --copy-hover and --copy-success customize the button. Runtime data-us-copy-state and us-copy-flash are controller-owned; do not author them. Remove the button to omit copying; no iPart wrapper class is required.

{{SNIPPET_COPY_BUTTON}}

Case banner: two rows from two IQAs

Implemented in the shared theme (US-BANNER-COMPONENT in zUnionSuite.css; the details row is US-BANNER-FACTS in zUnionSuite.js). The case page's banner. The top row holds the case ID, title, the primary party, the status and Actions; it stays when the banner condenses. The second row holds the description and the case's facts, filled from a separate details IQA, so a client changes what it shows by editing that IQA's columns, not the template. Agreements use the same structure.

{{CASE_ROWS_DEMO}}

The example uses the theme's own code with the sample values below. Its details IQA row is a stand-in, and the status menu's items show as not configured because the case actions are not defined in the guide.

iPart settings

Use a Query Template Display iPart whose IQA returns the current case, filtered on the page's CaseID URL parameter. In the iPart CSS class field, enter ; optionally set the page CSS class to for a full-width banner. Leave the native title and description blank and the border disabled. Header and Footer blank, Rows per page 1, No results Case not found.

The parts

  • Surface: the header carries and data-us-record-status="{#query.Status}". The status sets the pill and left rail colour with no colour field: New, Open and In progress amber; Active, Approved and Completed green; Expired, Terminated and Overdue red; anything else neutral. Extend the lists in Override.css for a client's own statuses.
  • Browser tab title: on the header titles the browser tab with the case ID and the selected tab of the page's CCO: C218 - Overview. It follows tab switches, including in-place ones, and titles iMIS writes on postbacks. With no CCO tab it reads the ID and the page's own title. Any record banner can use it with its own ID (US-RECORD-TITLE in zUnionSuite.js).
  • Member line: under the title holds an icon, the name (us-banner__member-name, linked to their record), the muted us-banner__member-line and a standing badge (us-banner__badge us-banner__badge--danger or --warning). An empty line or badge is hidden. It is in the top row, so it stays when the banner condenses, at a smaller size. The iMIS content editor saves the icon's <i> as <em>; the theme keeps it upright.
  • Status menu: a us-actions menu inside us-banner__status, whose toggle is the status pill (us-actions__toggle us-banner__badge us-banner__badge--record-status). It keeps the pill's tint and dot, with a chevron. Each status item is a us-action-cases-set-status button with data-us-case-status set to a status Code. On a case page the site's case actions fill the menu from the case Status value list ($/_i4u_/Core/Cases/Layouts v2/API - Value List - Case Status: Code, Description and Closed Status): its open statuses, each showing its Description and saving its Code through /cases/quick-update/set-status, in place of the template's items, which stay if the list cannot be read. The pill shows the Description and the current status is ticked (aria-current="true"). Closed Status also decides whether the case is closed, which shows Edit resolution and Reopen case in place of the statuses and Resolve case. Closing goes through Resolve case, not a status. Until the site defines the cases.* actions, the items show as not configured.
  • Actions menu icons: each Actions item carries with the icon of the section it adds to or opens, so the menu reads like the page: Add note ti-notes, Add task ti-square-check, Add meeting ti-calendar, Upload attachment ti-paperclip, Add party ti-users, Add staff ti-briefcase, Update case details ti-folder, Resolve case and Edit resolution ti-trophy. Email parties and Reopen case keep their own (ti-send, ti-lock-open). The same actions elsewhere (a section's Add button, a panel's pencil) keep their icons.
  • Details row: us-banner__details with (the details IQA's whole path) and (its filter name; the value is the page URL parameter of that name, or data-us-facts-value). Leave it empty: the theme fills it. A failed or empty query leaves the row out.

Query Template and field definitions

Paste one result's HTML into the Query Template field. Every alias below must be selected in the IQA, including the ones that may be blank. The status menu's four statuses are a fallback: on a case page the theme replaces them with the case Status value list's open statuses. Change the data-us-facts-query path to your details IQA.

{{CASE_ROWS_TEMPLATE}}

The details IQA

One row for the case, filtered on CaseNum (the case's ordinal). The theme reads its columns by name, in IQA order:

  • Description: shown first, under the label Description, cut off after 300 characters with Read more.
  • Every Additional- column is a fact, labelled with the text after the prefix (Additional-Lead staff reads Lead staff). Blank values are left out; ISO dates read 1 Jan 2026; true and false read Yes and No.
  • Tone- plus the same name (success, warning, danger or info) shows that fact as a badge, for example Tone-Priority beside Additional-Priority. Blank shows plain text.

The case page uses Additional-Opened (the date and the case's age), Additional-Type, Additional-Subtype, Additional-Priority with Tone-Priority, Additional-Lead staff with Tone-Lead staff (danger when no one is assigned) and Additional-Last member contact with Tone-Last member contact (warning from 14 days, danger from 28; blank once closed). The priority's tone comes from the I4U_UT_CASE_PRIORITY general lookup table, so it is changed there, not in the IQA. Return each fact as display text: a column cut short in the IQA shows cut short in the banner.

Additional-Opened — a custom SQL expression (replace C.DateOpened and C.DateClosed with your columns)
FORMAT(C.DateOpened, 'd MMM yyyy', 'en-US') + N' · ' +
CASE
  WHEN C.DateClosed IS NOT NULL THEN
    CASE DATEDIFF(day, C.DateOpened, C.DateClosed)
      WHEN 0 THEN 'closed the same day'
      WHEN 1 THEN 'closed after 1 day'
      ELSE 'closed after ' + CAST(DATEDIFF(day, C.DateOpened, C.DateClosed) AS varchar(10)) + ' days'
    END
  ELSE
    CASE DATEDIFF(day, C.DateOpened, GETDATE())
      WHEN 0 THEN 'today'
      WHEN 1 THEN '1 day'
      ELSE CAST(DATEDIFF(day, C.DateOpened, GETDATE()) AS varchar(10)) + ' days'
    END
END

Case / agreement banner template

Static one-row template. For a Query Template case or agreement banner, use the two-row case banner above. Implemented reusable template: Banner-Case-Template.html is the common starting point for both cases and agreements. The sample values and fields are editable per client; shared CSS and JavaScript provide the approved appearance and behaviour.

Status stripe: add to the inner header and set data-us-status-colour="#23845B" there. Use alongside us-banner__badge on the status label. The stripe and badge share one colour; the example uses green for Active. The case/agreement status pill matches the Actions button height in both expanded and compact states: 36px minimum on desktop, 44px at widths of 950px or less. It keeps its 20px line height and centres the text vertically; long labels may wrap and grow rather than clip. For cases and agreements, supply the colour appropriate to the status from your own lookup; labels do not imply an automatic mapping. The existing shared status controller validates six-digit hex colours, darkens light colours for white-text contrast and falls back to neutral grey for missing/invalid values. It updates both on attribute changes and partial replacement. Keep a visible status label. The glass modifier controls Actions only. This static template uses a literal hex value; future IQA templates must select both status text and colour fields. Requires updated shared CSS/JS; copy the complete example below.

Copy record ID: the case banner uses the shared copy button. Its data-us-copy-target="case-banner-id" matches the ID span. Change both IDs together if multiple examples share a document. The button copies only the ID and flashes its tinted badge. Legacy us-banner__copy buttons remain supported by the same controller.

Glass Actions and tinted record ID: add alongside us-banner__surface on the template header. This opts its Actions toggle into the same shared glass styling as the member banner, including hover/open, pressed and keyboard-focus states. Add to a span containing the case or agreement ID inside the eyebrow. The ID is selectable text with a flat 15% white tint, white translucent border and banner text colour; it has no gradient, blur or glass shadows. It stays visible when condensed and has no button behaviour. Both the ID and glass Actions toggle use --radius-sm (4px by default), matching the standard small corner radius. Actions retains its glass highlight and blur over the banner background. Browsers without backdrop-filter retain the gradient and border. Reduced motion removes button transitions. Update shared zUnionSuite.css before using these classes. The complete example below includes both placements.

us-banner__description uses normal font weight, 1.6 line spacing and 78% of --banner-muted over the banner background for a softer reading tone. When the description is directly followed by us-banner__facts inside us-banner__details, the divider sits below the description, above the facts. It blends 85% --banner-border with 15% --banner-text for a slightly clearer divider, with --space-4 spacing. Fact-only banners retain their existing top divider. Apply this class to the description paragraph inside us-banner__details; put the full plain-text description in a span with data-us-description-full inside the paragraph, and set on that paragraph. Updated shared JS generates an excerpt of at most 300 Unicode code points including its ellipsis and an inline Read more / Show less button. It cuts at a word boundary when practical. The full text expands in place; short descriptions have no toggle. While Read more is open, the banner stays expanded during scrolling (sticky behaviour remains active). Show less always moves focus from the folding description to the title without scrolling the page, so a later scroll is not blocked by focus left on the disclosure button. The title’s temporary tabindex is restored on blur. When already scrolled past the collapse threshold, Show less also explicitly requests banner condensation. Near the top it only shortens the description; subsequent scrolling condenses the banner normally. Other focus inside details continues to prevent automatic scroll collapse. Without JS the full text is visible. The button has aria-expanded and aria-controls and retains keyboard focus. Read more / Show less smoothly changes the paragraph height over 240ms; repeated clicks reverse from the current visible height. Reduced-motion preferences or unavailable animation support use an immediate change. The paragraph returns to its natural height when the transition ends, so responsive wrapping is retained. Expansion state survives ordinary refreshes; a replacement description starts collapsed. The complete example below supplies the 682-character sample. Keep the separate Case Details content if desired; the disclosure no longer navigates there. Browsers without colour-mix support retain the solid token colour.

The scrolling case and contact previews reproduce the native full-width iMIS wrapper chain and load foundation, shared theme, client Branding.css, Override.css and finally zzDarkMode.css. The shared full-width rule supplies square corners; the generic embedded samples below retain the normal rounded component default. The contact preview uses real scroll collapse and no comparison CSS. No production styling changes are required.
