Priority sits beside the case ID and copy button inside us-banner__record, remaining visible when the banner condenses. Wrap the ID span and copy button together in to make them one chip: the copy icon sits inside the ID chip at its end, and the button is stretched over the whole chip, so clicking the ID copies it. The chip keeps room for the icon, so it never changes width: at rest the icon is hidden and the ID sits centred; pointing at the chip slides the ID to the start and the icon slides in from the left, fading up. The icon also shows while the button has keyboard focus, during the tick or an error, and always on touch screens, where there is no hover. The member banner puts its contact ID in the same chip after the contact type. It remains one button with its own aria-label, so keyboard and screen reader behaviour is unchanged; at 950px or narrower its target grows to 44px tall around the chip. For the case/agreement layout, the enclosing us-banner__eyebrow sits directly inside the banner surface, before us-banner__summary. The summary contains the title identity, Status and Actions, centring the controls against the title rather than the combined ID/title block. The existing flex layout wraps the controls on narrower screens; no fixed offsets or extra CSS are needed. Keep the explicit priority label; other danger badges retain their usual emphasis. The complete template below includes this modifier. The description and four facts fold away. Opened, Last Updated and the full description belong in a separate Case Details panel below; this reduces banner density without removing record information.

Optional blank-field handling: add to the facts dl. Shared JS hides direct fact rows whose dd text is blank or whitespace and restores them when a value arrives; a completely empty row grid is hidden too. Explicitly hidden populated fields stay hidden. This includes partial replacements and does not treat 0 or a dash as blank. It does not fix missing IQA columns: all referenced fields must still be selected. Without JS, author/server blank handling is required. Existing grid auto-fit and wrapping keep long values readable; the ID/copy pair stays together, while Priority and summary controls can wrap. Update shared CSS/JS for these features.

Contact banner

Standalone tabs trial: Compare tab styling options alongside current banner and horizontal/vertical CCO tabs, including options 7–9 for segmented submenus and matching proposed banner shapes. try the working section-switcher preview. Pinned banner positioning: the shared script measures the site header and banner slot. It writes --us-banner-fixed-top, --us-banner-fixed-left and --us-banner-fixed-width for the fixed surface. Do not hard-code these offsets or override its pinned position: fixed state; the top offset intentionally leaves room for the site header. The us-banner-page layout controls gutters and padding only; the component owns positioning. This also applies to Query Template banners nested inside native panels: setting the surface to relative in a page-layout rule adds the measured offsets to its normal position and shifts it down and right.

Member profile banner — status and Actions

Use in the Query Template Display iPart CSS class field. iMIS inserts this wrapper inside ContentItemContainer; do not copy outer wrappers into the IQA template. Return one result for the current member.

Add alongside us-banner__surface on the inner header. Put on that header, and on its status badge.

Where member status and colour come from

  1. Determine the member status: the _I4u_UT_Contact_BannerType business object (BO) uses its custom SQL expression filter to determine the banner member type/status for each contact ID. Maintain the status rules there.
  2. Assign a colour to that status: maintain the I4U_CONTACTBANNER_TYPES lookup table. Match the member type/status produced by the BO to its corresponding entry and assign that entry a CSS colour code. For this banner, use a six-digit hex value including #, such as #23845B.
  3. Return the values through IQA: for each contact ID, the banner IQA pulls the matching status and lookup colour code into the banner result. Expose them as StatusDescription and StatusColour, or use your actual query aliases in the template. Return one row for the current member.
  4. Render the banner: {#query.StatusDescription} supplies the pill label and {#query.StatusColour} supplies the header's data-us-status-colour attribute. The shared theme adjusts the display colour only where needed for readable white text; it does not change the BO status rules or the stored lookup value.

To change which status a member receives, update the BO's custom SQL expression filter. To change the colour associated with a status, update its matching lookup entry. No status names or business rules need to be hard-coded in the banner JavaScript.

The IQA supplies StatusDescription (plain text) and StatusColour (a six-digit hex, including #). Rename these aliases to match your query. Encode query values for their HTML/attribute context. Any client-defined status is supported; the theme does not determine membership status.

The 15px left rail and pill share the derived display colour. Banner tabs use neutral selection without a status-coloured underline. White pill text always has at least 4.5:1 contrast: lighter colours are darkened without changing the supplied attribute. Invalid or absent colours use #596579; an empty pill reads “Status unavailable”. Dark display colours gain a 2px light inner separator. Pale outer edges are unnecessary after contrast adjustment. Vertical pill padding is 7px expanded and 3px collapsed. The orange top border is removed only for member banners.

Member Quick Actions uses a translucent glass finish with hover/open highlights, inset press shading and a white keyboard focus ring. Reduced-motion preferences remove its transitions. The menu uses the shared action controller and existing member popup mappings; load the site’s popup functions before using commands. No inline onclick is needed.

Optional banner tabs use the darker full-width 6B band, neutral segmented selection and inset pressed shading. Connect explicit page sections using the section-switcher instructions; native CCO loading remains separate. Keep tabs outside the details block so they remain visible when collapsed.

Load the shared CSS and JS once. Status styling works independently of sticky scrolling and refreshes after partial replacement or a changed colour attribute. UnionSuiteMemberStatus.refresh() is available for explicit refresh. Without JavaScript the neutral fallback remains. us-report-no-styling opts out. Component variables are --member-status-colour and --member-status-inner-edge; JS derives them per header, so configure the input attribute rather than overriding these outputs.

09 / CHECK & RESOLVE

When something does not behave as expected

SymptomCheck
Shell, spacing or header actions disappear after adding an iPart classiMIS inserts a div for the CSS class, changing the panel's immediate parent. Check both CSS selectors and JavaScript detection against the direct and wrapped structures. Keep the class in the iPart field and fix the adapter/preview; do not move it into the repeated template.
Banner neither sticks nor condensesCorrect us- classes on the banner content iPart; behaviour script loaded; Easy Edit off; exactly one visible surface. The page class alone is insufficient.
Banner sticks but stays tallUse us-banner-collapsible, not only us-banner-sticky. Scroll beyond 60px. Very short pages or focus in details can intentionally prevent folding.
Banner is still insetCheck the page class, top-level col-sm-12 and rendered wrapper chain. A custom/nested layout may need its own scoped adapter.
Other page content loses paddingRetain native rows, columns and --bs-gutter-x. Remove obsolete blanket div padding resets. Do not wrap every zone to compensate.
Every CCO tab has a horizontal scrollbarCheck the outer row gutter gotcha: a native row needs room for its negative margins. Padding outside an iframe cannot balance a row inside it. Compare row edges with the child viewport before changing overflow.
Brand change does nothingCheck active CSS outside comments, load order, caching and pinned primitive overrides. Native hard-coded rules are not token consumers.
Report Filters icon absentCheck the supported native Query Menu structure, visible native filter inputs and the shared JS include. No opt-in class is required.
Staff navigation icon missing or shown as a squareCheck both configured classes and their root -wrapper output, the updated shared CSS and successful loading of Tabler.css, its imported CSS and WOFF2 font. Refresh cached assets. A tabler font-family declaration without the matching codepoint is insufficient.
Filters reopen or remember an earlier stateWithout us-filters-collapsed, browser-tab preferences are remembered. With the class, initialization/reconciliation starts closed despite saved preferences. Validation can reopen filters.
Expand unavailableCheck the expandable class and supported native grid layout. A disabled control can indicate a split or virtualized grid.
Export absentThe adapter relocates native Export when rendered; it does not create permissions or an export control absent from the native report.
Menu opens but commands/tabs do nothingTemplate commands and tabs are disabled placeholders. Actions need verified handlers/URLs; tabs need the page-sections adapter and matching panel classes.
A section tab is greyed out, or one iPart shows on every tabThat tab has no matching panel, or that iPart's classes do not match a tab. Check the browser console for UnionSuite sections: messages, which name the classes to add. Also check the 100-character iPart CSS class limit, which silently truncates long class lists.
JS breaks after saving HTMLInspect the saved/rendered source. RiSE may strip event attributes or rewrite entities inside script. Use the external JS alternative when needed.
Changes disappear after a refreshVerify shared includes and the ASP.NET lifecycle on the actual page. Do not attach commands only to a node that is replaced without rebinding.
Read-only console diagnostics For maintainers
Check loaded feature APIs
window.UnionSuiteBanners?.getStatus();
typeof window.UnionSuiteIqaFilters?.refresh;

Banner status reports whether the script loaded, Easy Edit state, marked/eligible iParts and active/pinned/compact state without returning record data. undefined indicates the API is absent; zero marked iParts usually points to class placement. The report check should return "function".

Gotcha: outer row gutters can widen the whole page

Native .row uses negative horizontal margins, balanced by half-gutter padding on its columns and the surrounding layout. UltraWave defines --bs-gutter-x:30px; the project Orion foundation keeps a 40px general default and uses 24px for standard page-layout rows. Those layout rows extend 12px beyond each side of their immediate wrapper; ordinary 40px rows extend 20px. Clearing the surrounding horizontal padding, moving a row to an unpadded shell, or embedding it in an iframe breaks this balance even when every width is 100%.

ArrangementEffect
Native CCOThe theme's .RadMultiPage padding and the content row share one document, allowing the padding to accommodate the negative margins.
Nested fields and reportsTheir rows belong to the component's own padded layout. Keep those gutters. Deliberately wide grids can retain their own scroll region without making the whole tab scroll.

Fix the gutter balance at the layout boundary that owns the row. Use matching parent padding where that is the intended layout, or clear only the unbalanced outer row margins. Do not globally reset .row, remove all column padding, or conceal the problem with overflow-x:hidden. Check the actual rendered wrapper chain, including iPart class wrappers, and both desktop and narrow widths.

Maintainer diagnostic — select the affected frame in DevTools first
({
  viewport: document.documentElement.clientWidth,
  content: document.documentElement.scrollWidth
});

If a nested shell has overflow:auto, also compare that shell's scrollWidth and clientWidth; the document alone may appear to fit. Inspect the outer row's bounding rectangle, computed margins, gutter value and parent padding.

Checks after an update

Use a real desktop and narrow layout, keyboard navigation, reduced motion and Easy Edit. For banners, scroll both directions, change sidebar width and test optional blocks. For reports, test native filters, query selection, sorting, Export, page size, paging and Expand/Restore. Trigger a real partial refresh. Local browser fixtures verify presentation and lifecycle simulations; they do not replace live iMIS/Telerik testing.

Banner navigation and standalone submenus

Implemented: banner tabs use the full-width darker 6B navigation area with neutral segmented selections. Standalone submenus use option 6: a 10px rounded track, 6px selected item and small theme-accent marker. Native horizontal/vertical CCO styling is unchanged. Both retain inset shading while pressed.

Standalone track colour: the track is recessed below the page (--us-section-track: --neutral-200, or the page darkened in dark mode) with no visible border, so the white active tab matches the white report cards beneath it. Hover uses a half-surface wash (--us-section-tab-hover) so it never reads as the active tab; background, shadow and colour changes transition over 150ms, instantly with reduced motion. Override either token in the client :root.

{{SECTION_TABS_DEMO}}

This example runs the shared section switcher. Edit a field, switch the banner or submenu, and return: values and DOM nodes remain intact.

Animated selection underline: connected us-section-tabs menus now slide one 20px accent underline to the activated tab over 220ms. Click or use Enter/Space to activate; arrow-key focus alone does not move the selection. The marker follows variable-width labels, resized layouts, nested-menu reveals and horizontal scrolling, including right-to-left menus. Resizing and first display position it immediately; reduced-motion preferences disable the slide and forced colours retain a visible marker. The selected background and content switch immediately.

Upload updated zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js together. Existing connected menu HTML and iPart classes stay the same; no animation class is required. The controller supplies data-us-section-indicator and private coordinate properties on the menu, then removes them when the menu disconnects, opts out or enters Easy Edit. Do not author these runtime values. Visual-only menus retain their static underline; banner and native CCO navigation keep their own selection styling.

Connect a menu

Standalone tab buttons have a 100px minimum width; longer labels expand naturally and narrow menus scroll horizontally. Wrap the menu in inside Content HTML to add 18px top spacing, a full-width --border separator 4px below the tabs and 24px below the separator. Keep this page-layout wrapper outside the switched panels. Upload updated zUnionSuite.css; switching attributes, keyboard navigation and partial-refresh behaviour are unchanged. The wrapper is optional for existing menus; native CCO and banner tabs retain their own layout.

Add to a standalone menu div. For a banner use the existing us-banner__nav / us-banner__tabs structure. On the menu or its enclosing nav set data-us-tabs="member" and . This explicit adapter opt-in is required; legacy visual-only menus do not start hiding content.

Give each button a unique data-us-tab key, with is-active on the initial choice. Use lowercase keys starting with a letter, followed by letters, digits or hyphens. Omit unavailable sections; a disabled button stays disabled and is skipped. One menu per group is supported. Nested submenus use a different group key.

Standalone menu · inner HTML
<div class="us-section-navigation">
<div class="us-section-tabs" data-us-tabs="member"
     data-us-tab-adapter="page-sections" aria-label="Member sections">
  <button type="button" data-us-tab="overview" class="is-active">Overview</button>
  <button type="button" data-us-tab="notes">Notes</button>
</div>
</div>

Mark the sections to show and hide

Use on the Overview section owner and on Notes. Several sibling owners can share the same tab key. Unmarked content stays visible.

For an iPart, put these values in its CSS class field: iMIS inserts the marked div inside ContentItemContainer. The marked div and its children are hidden. Empty native ContentItemContainer, iMIS-WebPart, WebPartZone, column and row ancestors also collapse when every content child is a hidden managed section or collapsed wrapper. This removes spacing from inactive iParts on separate rows without changing active content spacing. Wrappers with unmarked content or text remain visible; scripts, styles and hidden inputs do not count as visible content. A title outside the marked owner remains visible: mark an explicit common section container to include it. Automatic zone-to-tab mapping is not implemented. Keep the menu outside its own switched panels; do not mark native CCO page views. Load updated shared CSS and JS together. Wrapper visibility is restored when its section activates, the menu is removed or Easy Edit is enabled; no new author class is needed.

Explicit sections · Content HTML
<section class="us-tab-panel us-tabset-member us-tab-overview">
  <h2>Overview</h2>
  <!-- Overview content -->
</section>
<section class="us-tab-panel us-tabset-member us-tab-notes">
  <h2>Notes</h2>
  <!-- Notes content -->
</section>

Load zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js once. Each section is checked on its own, so one misconfigured iPart affects only its own tab and the rest of the group still switches. A tab with no matching panel is disabled, with a "No content is connected to this tab" tooltip; arrow keys skip it. A panel that cannot be managed stays visible on every tab. That covers a misspelt or unknown us-tab- key, more than one key, a hidden panel, a no-styling owner, a native CCO page view, and panels that overlap within one group. Each problem is reported once in the browser console as UnionSuite sections: …, and the missing-panel message names the classes to add. Disabled buttons stay disabled and are skipped. Problems with the menu itself still leave all content visible: an invalid or duplicate group, invalid or duplicate keys, buttons in different parents, or no enabled tab that has a panel. Fix the iPart class and the tab enables on the next refresh. Removing a menu restores its original visibility. Easy Edit via the visible on-toggle or EasyEdit body class also releases managed visibility.

Keyboard arrows and Home/End move focus; Enter/Space activates. The controller adds tab roles, panel IDs, labels and aria-controls, including multiple panels per key. Selections survive partial DOM replacement and same-page query-string changes. Changing the pathname or fully reloading resets them. No URL rewriting, lazy loading or data refresh occurs. The script emits us:sectionchange with group, key and visible panels; use a verified widget reflow hook when charts need it, rather than re-running queries.

UnionSuiteSections.refresh() reconnects after class/availability configuration changes or a same-page record-context change. UnionSuiteSections.select('member','notes') selects a connected section and returns whether it succeeded. Content remains mounted, so unsaved values survive switching, but no saving is provided. Hidden fields may still participate in native validation; verify real forms before rollout. Hiding is not access control. CCO keeps its own loading and postbacks; this adapter can control explicitly authored subsections inside a CCO, but does not replace its page views.

Link to a section on another CCO tab

A link can open a section of another tab, for example a one-line summary on Summary that opens Profile › Employment. Give an ordinary link , using the menu's data-us-tabs group and the button's data-us-tab key, and with the CCO tab's title as shown in the rail (matched without regard to case). The section is remembered first, then the CCO tab opens, so the section shows as soon as the tab renders. Without data-us-cco-tab, or when that tab is already open, the section switches in place and its tab receives focus. A native CCO fallback reloads the page, which lands on the tab's default section. UnionSuiteSections.open('profile','employment') does the same from script.

Section link · in a template or Content HTML
<a href="#" data-us-section-link="profile:employment" data-us-cco-tab="Profile">View details</a>

Spinner lifecycle: an ordinary click or keyboard activation shows the existing shared 16px ring without changing the anchor's accessible name, dimensions or native handler. Duplicate clicks on that busy control are suppressed. Modified/new-tab/download clicks and unavailable or us-report-no-styling controls are excluded. Accepted ASP.NET beginRequest/endRequest events keep the spinner until completion, including errors; OBO's delegated select/clear request is associated only with its account menu. A new visible native picker, on/off state change, removed control, pagehide or pageshow clears it. Cancelled Cart navigation clears immediately; a 10-second recovery clears feedback when a cancelled navigation or unrecognised picker supplies no completion signal. This timeout is not a success signal. Native click handling, query permissions and transaction-date values are unchanged.

Presentation is in the native foundation; visibility, separator and busy styles are in shared CSS. Colours use --brand-900/800/700, --brand-50/100, --brand-600, --text-inverse, --bg-surface, --border and --border-focus; shape uses --radius-sm. The shared ring stops rotating for reduced motion; forced colours retain focus and an on-state outline. No polling or per-control event registration is needed after partial updates. This targets the supplied navbar-right markup with native SVG support; real iMIS navigation, postbacks and the site's picker still require live verification.

Taskbar dark mode

Implemented in the shared theme. Load zzDarkMode.css last, after native, shared and client CSS. It owns the dark palette, native/component overrides and toggle styling. The moon/sun switch follows Full Search, to the right of the search bar and before the taskbar's existing boundary separator. Install the updated shared JS and taskbar script as well.

{{TASKBAR_DARK_MODE_DEMO}}
Dark mode installation — use the deployed theme paths
<!-- Keep existing native Orion, Tabler, zUnionSuite.css,
     Branding.css and Override.css entries. Add this CSS LAST. -->
<link rel="stylesheet" href="/App_Themes/YOUR_THEME/zzDarkMode.css">
<!-- No script entries: the theme loader (index.js) loads
     zUnionSuite.js and Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js. -->

Order: native Orion and Tabler → zUnionSuite.css → client Branding.css → client Override.css (or legacy zzClientSpecific.css) → zzDarkMode.css. The dark file is a separately deployed stylesheet, not a build-time bundle or an import. Put all future dark-specific overrides in this file. Its root attribute selectors handle specificity as well as source order; loading last alone cannot override an earlier !important or stronger inline rule. Existing logo images and inverse brand fills are retained.

The generated final child of #injected-taskbar is a button.us-taskbar__theme-toggle, after .tb-search-wrap. It uses type="button", role="switch", a stable Dark mode accessible name, aria-checked and data-taskbar-action="toggle-dark-mode". The Clouds & moon switch uses an outlined 44 × 24px track centred in a 36px-high control to align with native iMIS buttons (44px-high target at 950px or narrower). It appears after the separator: Full Search → separator → appearance switch → native iMIS controls. Its border uses --text-muted in both modes, a sliding sun/moon disc, drifting clouds and fading stars over 480ms. Reduced motion removes transitions. Upload updated Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js and last-loaded zzDarkMode.css, and load Tabler.css. It retains the appearance controller, saved preference, device default and replacement lifecycle. No author class or iPart HTML is required; do not copy the generated wrapper into Content HTML. Cloud/star glyphs use the existing Tabler.css and bundled font. The switch stays hidden when shared appearance JS or the stylesheet's --us-dark-mode-enabled:1 marker is absent.

The switch matches Full Search: a 36px square on all pointer types, with 8px corners and a centred 20px glyph. Its generated TextButton us-outline-button classes use the shared outline border, colours, hover, pressed and focus states; dark colours for both controls are grouped in zzDarkMode.css. Update that stylesheet and Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js together for this matching format. Enter and Space activate the native button; focus is visible, reduced motion removes the transition, and forced colours retain system border/glyph colours. Mobile search remains full-screen. The taskbar's existing lifecycle mounts one switch, restores its state after partial replacement and removes its listeners on teardown. The document preference remains active when the taskbar is destroyed. Print uses light tokens and hides the switch.

The first visit follows prefers-color-scheme. An explicit choice stores light or dark in union-suite:appearance:v1, scoped to this browser and origin, not the iMIS account. Invalid values fall back to the device. Storage errors leave a working page-only choice. Storage events synchronise other tabs and themed popup documents; same-origin themed frames also receive page-only changes and new frames inherit their parent's choice. Each iframe/popup must load the shared CSS, last-loaded dark CSS and shared JS itself. No style is injected into another document; cross-origin content retains its own appearance.

UnionSuiteAppearance.getState() returns the current scheme, explicit preference, enabled and storageAvailable. Use setPreference('light'), setPreference('dark'), reset() (or setPreference('system')), toggle() and refresh() as needed. Changes emit the window event unionsuite:appearancechange with the state in event.detail. The preview's Use device setting button calls reset(); Reload taskbar example exercises replacement. Optional UnionSuiteAppearanceConfig.storageKey must precede shared JS; the offline reference uses a separate preview key so its controls never change a production preference.

The mode resolves when shared JS starts and refreshes after document/style loading. Late or deferred JS can briefly display the light palette before restoring a saved dark choice. The offline reference places the extracted shared appearance block in its head; it is generated from zUnionSuite.js, not a separately maintained implementation.

Dark tokens apply to html[data-us-color-scheme="dark"]: surface, text/link, border/focus, status foreground/background and shadow tokens. Taskbar aliases are explicitly remapped because they are declared on the component itself. Native page/header surfaces, direct and wrapped panels (except a CCO's own panel, which stays a transparent shell as in light mode, so the section's cards are not framed in a second box), text fields, select controls, report rows/headers/selected states, outline/status buttons, popup chrome and autocomplete surfaces receive scoped overrides. The five brand seeds and brand ramps are preserved; dark hover/selected states use --dm-hover, --dm-selected, --dm-accent-bg and --dm-accent-text. Palette edits belong in this file.

Limits: this is initial theme coverage, not certification of every iMIS/Telerik screen. Validate live client pages, rich editors, specialised controls, calendars, custom inline colours, charts and image assets before site-wide rollout. Root tokens are inherited even inside us-report-no-styling; targeted native override rules respect that opt-out, but it does not create a separate light-theme document. Existing CCO layout, iPart wrappers, query permissions and API behaviour are unchanged.

Generate the standalone dark-mode reference with node THeme/UnionSuite-Guides/usage/build/build-taskbar-dark-mode.cjs, then --check. Only fictional content, review controls and page layout remain in prototypes/Taskbar-Dark-Mode.*; all appearance styling is embedded from zzDarkMode.css. The guide remains offline.

Selected rows/nodes, menu focus/hover/disabled states and resize bars retain distinct colours.

{{OBJECT_BROWSER_DARK_DEMO}}

The read-only Directory path has an inline grey background in the supplied markup, so its narrow dark override uses !important. The existing address autocomplete also has inline colours. Other overrides use native classes and specificity. Native geometry, scroll areas, collapse/resize hit areas, hidden/disabled state and iMIS/Telerik event handlers stay with the original controls. Injected taskbar — brand accent colours

Implemented standalone replacement: Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js supplies management shortcuts, Biscuit’s daily greeting, Quick Search with recent searches and records, Full Search, and the optional appearance switch. The separate Go to ID control has been removed. Quick Search retains request cancellation, keyboard result navigation, mobile search and lifecycle cleanup. Component styles live in zUnionSuite.css; dark colours and appearance-switch styling live in the last-loaded zzDarkMode.css.

Pre-release bookmarks trial: when Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar-Bookmarks.js is installed after the taskbar, Go to… can search and open destinations while saved bookmarks load. Pinning and reordering wait for that read to succeed so changes use the existing settings row and preserve its bookmarks. If loading fails, the palette shows Retry and keeps those edits disabled until a successful retry. A late response from a disposed instance cannot replace the current bookmarks.

Header row at every width. The header keeps one row for as long as it fits, then wraps in steps, as the approved taskbar design (Option C) specifies:

  • 1200px and wider: one row with Biscuit beside the logo. The right-hand group takes the space beside the brand, and Quick Search narrows from 384px towards 180px (a 136px field beside Full Search) before anything wraps.
  • Below 1200px: Biscuit is hidden (see Biscuit).
  • Below 1100px: the strip's Add bookmarks and Go to… buttons show their icons only; their accessible names and tooltips stay. The bookmarks bar below keeps its labels.
  • 960px and below: the cart and account menu stay on the logo row and the taskbar takes a full row beneath it, with Quick Search taking that row's spare width.
  • 780px and below: bookmarks lead the taskbar row and Go to…, the appearance switch and the bookmarks-bar toggle end it; Quick Search takes a full row of its own. Results still open full screen at 768px and below.
  • 767px and below (where iMIS shows its bottom menu bar): the cart is hidden, so the account menu sits on the logo row at the right. Quick Search and Full Search leave the header; the bottom bar's search button opens Quick Search full screen in place of the native iMIS search, with recent searches and recently viewed records. Closing it (✕ or Escape) returns focus to that button, and the button reports whether the search is open.
  • 480px and below: the logo's box is capped so the account menu stays beside it; the logo only shrinks on the narrowest phones.

Nothing in the header reaches past the window edge at any width, so phones no longer zoom the page out and the menu bar stays at the bottom of the screen. The steps are viewport widths, measured for the standard 200px client logo with the sidebar open or collapsed. A much wider client logo can need the steps earlier; check it at 961px and 1100px. Upload the updated zUnionSuite.css, Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js and Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar-Bookmarks.js together; no class or setting is needed. On phones the cart and Full Search have no header button.

Biscuit's daily taskbar greeting

Implemented. Biscuit, the floppy-eared puppy, has a dedicated 96px space in the header’s top row, immediately to the right of the client logo, with no separator beside him. His paws rest on the bottom edge of that row — the inside edge of the <header> border, or the line above the bookmarks bar when that row is shown — not the middle of the controls. His golden CSS drawing is 30% larger than the original mascot preview. He peeks up, gives a bouncing wave with a tail wag and tongue-out smile, and follows the pointer with a subtle head turn. His ears are attached to the head and follow its curious tilt; the reserved width leaves room for them throughout the animation. His visit lasts five minutes, ending with the normal slide away unless the user chooses the third-click goodbye first. His space stays reserved while he is away, so navigation and search do not shift.

Replacing Pip with Biscuit: deploy Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js version 1.8, zUnionSuite.css and zzDarkMode.css together. Keep client Config.js before the script and the dark stylesheet last. The existing pipGreeting switch, pipStoragePrefix, playPipIdle(), us-taskbar__pip classes and --us-pip-* tokens retain their names for compatibility and now control Biscuit. His existing daily record is preserved, so replacing the files does not force another greeting that day. No mascot prototype or extra asset is loaded.

{{TASKBAR_DEMO}}

The first visible, authenticated taskbar visit of each local calendar day triggers the greeting. The taskbar reads __ClientContext.loggedInPartyId, not the selected record or OBO party, and stores the date under union-suite:pip-greeting:PARTY_ID in this origin's localStorage. Reloads, subsequent page visits and partial taskbar replacement preserve the daily limit. A returning open tab checks again on focus/visibility, without a midnight timer. This is browser-side daily gating, not a server login event or cross-device preference.

Biscuit waits for a loaded, visible header and a short quiet interval. Typing, search focus/results and open modal dialogs defer the greeting. A hidden page does not consume it. Web Locks coordinate simultaneous tabs; browsers without Web Locks use a short storage claim and ownership check as a best-effort fallback. Blocked storage or lock access suppresses automatic appearances rather than greeting on every page. Clearing browser storage makes the next eligible visit a first visit again.

Arrival: wave. First click: wave. Second click: happy hop. Third click: shy goodbye. Click, tap, Enter or Space uses the same three-step interaction. The automatic arrival wave does not count as a click, and each new visit resets the sequence. The hop has a small squash, lift and soft landing. The third activation ducks Biscuit below the header in 180ms, then he peeks back, looks left and right and faces the viewer. He gives a little startled jump with wide eyes, lifted ears and an open mouth before diving away in 240ms. The complete goodbye lasts three seconds; pointer movement cannot interrupt its gaze sequence. It cancels pending greeting/idle callbacks and keeps the daily limit, so navigating or reloading does not bring him back that day. Rapid clicks advance the sequence by replacing the current reaction; clicks during either exit are ignored.

Five-minute visit. The timer starts at the first peek, runs for 300 seconds, then plays the normal 640ms slide away (fully hidden by 700ms). Clicks and idle previews do not restart it. The third click can dismiss him earlier with the playful goodbye. Switching away from the tab pauses idle reactions while the five-minute clock keeps running; returning before it expires keeps the same next-click action. An overdue visit ends when the tab resumes.

Idle animations every 15 seconds. Biscuit randomly chooses a 2.2-second head scratch or a two-second curious tilt. The scratch lifts one paw to his head; the tilt moves his head and both attached floppy ears together and briefly holds eye contact. Idle actions never advance the click sequence, and clicks can interrupt them. A scheduled idle is skipped during another reaction, typing/search activity, open dialogs or reduced-motion mode; missed beats are not queued. Quiet blinking and occasional ear twitches continue between reactions.

Biscuit never takes focus automatically. If he has focus when either exit begins, focus moves to the first visible management shortcut, or Full Search on small screens. His empty section has no tab stop. Touch does not drive gaze tracking; scratching and tilting hold his gaze still. Reduced-motion mode uses a static raised paw on arrival and the first click, happy eyes on the second, and instant dismissal on the third click or timeout. Automatic idle actions are suppressed; the preview buttons show a static scratch pose or resting head for the curious action. There is no peeking, bouncing, breathing or animated head movement; preference changes apply during the visit and immediately finish either exit. Navigating away, reloading or tearing down the taskbar ends the current visit; the daily limit still applies on the next page. Teardown cancels timers and removes listeners. Print hides the section. Below 1200px he is hidden entirely, because the header row needs his 96px for its own controls (see header row at every width), as the approved taskbar design specifies: the section is removed with display:none and --us-pip-enabled:0, so no greeting appears and none is consumed. Opening the same page in a wider window later that day still gets that day's greeting.

Installation: upload Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js, zUnionSuite.css and zzDarkMode.css together, and load the client Config.js before the taskbar script. Keep zzDarkMode.css last, after client CSS, for Biscuit’s dark palette. The taskbar authentication guard and native search markup remain prerequisites. No iPart class or Content HTML is needed. The production drawing/styles live in shared CSS; greeting timing, header alignment, storage and cleanup live in the taskbar script. Mascot/biscuit-puppy.source.html remains an independent design prototype.

The script inserts Biscuit after the header’s native .navbar-left brand block, as its sibling in #masterTopBarAuxiliary, so the logo keeps its own width and alignment. A header without that block keeps the previous slot at the start of the taskbar. The taskbar then measures the nearest containing <header> (falling back to #hd) and aligns the drawing's clipped bottom with the inside edge of its bottom border. When another row follows the top row inside the header — the bookmarks bar — he rests on that row’s bottom edge instead, so he never covers the bookmarks. Hiding the bookmarks bar returns him to the header edge. From 1200px, a header that still wraps its controls onto further lines — a wide client logo, for example — stops him at the top of the first control that falls beneath his slot instead of letting him stand in front of it. Resize observers follow the header and intermediate wrappers, including a taller logo, responsive wrapping and padding changes; observers and pending layout frames are removed on teardown. The existing header's layout, border and utility controls remain native. A host without either header element falls back to the taskbar's bottom edge.

Generated classes: is his reserved space beside the logo, outside #injected-taskbar; its styles are scoped to #hd; is its greeting button, positioned down to the header edge with extra headroom for the hop. No Biscuit divider is generated. These are runtime inspection hooks, not author classes to add to an iPart. Internal pip-* spans draw the character and are hidden from assistive technology. The button's accessible name describes the next action: Biscuit says hello. Make Biscuit wave, then Make Biscuit hop, then Say goodbye to Biscuit.

The example's Scratch head and Curious tilt buttons trigger the shared idle actions immediately without advancing clicks, resetting the visit timer or consuming another daily greeting. They are disabled while Biscuit is absent, arriving or leaving. These review controls are only in the preview. The optional taskbar API returns true when a visible visit accepts an action; invalid actions, hidden pages, disabled Biscuit and absent visits return false.

Trigger an idle on the current visible visit — no extra taskbar markup
window.UnionSuiteTaskbar.playPipIdle('scratch');
// Or:
window.UnionSuiteTaskbar.playPipIdle('curious');

On/off switch: edit THeme/UnionSuite-Client/Config.js. Set pipGreeting to true for the daily greeting or false to remove Biscuit and his reserved space. The supplied config enables him. Settings are read when the taskbar script loads, so reload after changes. Turning Biscuit off does not clear his daily record; turning him back on the same day does not repeat an already-shown greeting.

// In UnionSuite-Client/Config.js (no script tags in this file).
window.UnionSuiteTaskbarConfig = {
  ...window.UnionSuiteTaskbarConfig,
  pipGreeting: true // false removes Biscuit and his reserved space.
};

Optional window.UnionSuiteTaskbarConfig, defined inline before the loader include, supports queryName, minimumSearchLength (3) and searchDelay (250ms). The default query remains $/_i4u_/Core/Directory/Quick Search/Taskbar Quick Search. It takes one parameter and returns native Items/Properties values. Quick Search requests at most 10 results by including &limit=10 in every IQA search request; use Full Search for the full directory. Result navigation uses /Party.aspx?ID=.... The separate Go to ID control is removed. Activating Full Search replaces its icon with the standard us-button-spinner and announces Opening full search through its accessible label and aria-busy. Its 36px size is preserved. Repeated same-tab clicks are suppressed while busy; modified/new-tab clicks keep native navigation. The icon resets on page restoration, teardown, or after ten seconds if navigation does not complete. Full Search is a standard outline link to /_i4u_/Core/Staff-Site-Layouts/Admin/Directory.aspx, configurable with fullSearchUrl. Preserve API permissions and the native request-verification token.

Descriptive us-taskbar__* classes coexist with legacy tb-* aliases. Actions use data-taskbar-action; result IDs use data-record-id. Arrow keys move through result links, Enter follows a focused link and Escape closes results. Pending requests are cancelled on dismissal or teardown. Duplicate loading is ignored; partial DOM replacement remounts automatically. Public methods: UnionSuiteTaskbar.refresh(), destroy() and initialise(). Authentication context is required; it does not replace server access checks. The offline example uses the actual script with fictional API responses and inactive navigation.

Search results display a building icon when COMPANY_RECORD is Boolean true and a person icon when false. The script unwraps native Value.$value Booleans. The IQA must return COMPANY_RECORD; missing or non-Boolean values, including Recent items without this metadata, have no type icon. Company names use COMPANY; person names use FULL_NAME. Generated us-taskbar__result-kind spans provide accessible Company/Person labels and inline SVGs coloured by --brand-600; no icon font or author class is required. Upload both the taskbar script and shared CSS. Compare result icons with dummy records.

Approved Quick Search: the 36px search shell includes its icon, with a 400ms border/glow transition using --field-focus-colour. The desktop dropdown aligns with the field and matches its width, excluding Full Search. Result and history icon/text columns align. Mobile retains the full-screen search panel. The shared section-loader-spinning-circles appears to the left of Searching… and is hidden from assistive technology; the message retains role=status. Reduced-motion preferences disable these animations, and forced-colour mode retains an outline.

Recent searches stores up to five unique terms in localStorage, keyed by signed-in party ID under union-suite:quick-search-history:. It persists on this browser without cross-device sync. Storage failures fall back to memory. Completed searches, including no-results responses, save automatically; failed or stale/cancelled requests do not auto-save. Edits started within two seconds replace the preceding entry on completion, regardless of request latency. Clearing the input preserves the prior entry; Enter and selecting a result close the window. Rerun, remove and Clear history controls are keyboard accessible. Recently viewed records remains the native iMIS history. Stored terms may include personal search text; Clear history removes the current user's list. No history is sent to a separate service.

Each recent-search remove action keeps an 18px inline × icon inside the shared 36px TextButton us-icon-button DangerButton control, increasing to 44px at 950px or narrower. The × means remove this saved search from history; a trash icon is reserved for actions that delete the underlying item. Shared danger styling supplies a red glyph using --danger, a transparent resting background and pale red --danger-bg on hover. The generated us-taskbar__history-remove class retains a 150ms colour, border and shadow transition; it adds no separate colour override. Pressing uses the shared inset shadow and current-colour border; keyboard focus keeps a visible --field-focus-colour outline. Reduced motion removes the transition. The target stays fixed in place, and the row keeps a compact inset. In the interactive example above, search Morgan and clear the field to try hovering, tabbing to and activating the remove button. Its accessible name includes the search term; it removes only that term and returns focus to the search field. Upload Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js and zUnionSuite.css together, then refresh the page. No author class, HTML, setting or icon font is needed; history storage is unchanged.

Recent-search remove labels use the shared pointer-transparent tooltip. Moving up from a lower remove button reaches the button above even when its hit area overlaps the previous label. Hovering the label keeps it readable without blocking the underlying row, and removing a term clears the old label. Keyboard labels and Escape dismissal remain available. Upload the updated zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js together; this tooltip fix needs no taskbar script, HTML or iPart class changes.

Removing recent searches keeps the dropdown open. Remove and Clear history rebuild the list while returning focus to the visible search field, including the mobile search panel. Removing the final entry shows the empty-history message and retains Recently viewed records. Mouse, touch and keyboard activation work without clicking out of the field and back in. Rerun continues searching in the open panel. Outside clicks, Escape and Close search results still dismiss it. Upload the updated Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js and refresh; this fix needs no CSS, HTML or class changes.

Generated classes us-taskbar__search-shell, us-taskbar__full-search, us-taskbar__history-heading, us-taskbar__history-row and us-taskbar__search-loading require no author markup. Deploy Scripts/UnionSuiteTaskbar.js and zUnionSuite.css together. Approved Quick Search comparison.

Embed a content page without the CCO header

Add to the CCO iPart's CSS class field. This implemented shared-theme modifier hides the outer CCO's tab/navigation strip and optional panel heading, removes its border, background and inner padding, and reclaims the vertical tab rail. The selected content page then sits naturally in the surrounding layout. Use it for a CCO configured with one embedded content page; hiding the menu does not make other tabs accessible or select a different page.

Optional sticky CCO sidebar

Add to the CCO iPart's CSS class field, not the banner, page or individual tab. Load updated zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js. The outer vertical CCO tab list becomes sticky above 600px, bounded by its own CCO. Its full-height rail remains in place. Long menus scroll internally; nested Address/horizontal tabs and the mobile scrolling/All sections layout remain unchanged.

{{CCO_STICKY_DEMO}}
CCO iPart CSS class field
us-cco-sticky-tabs

The adapter measures the visible overlapping #hd, #injected-taskbar and pinned Union Suite banner, adding an 8px gap below them. Resize observation follows banner condensation. When no top chrome overlaps, the gap is 8px. For another fixed toolbar, set --us-cco-sticky-offset on this iPart wrapper to an additional pixel offset. Example:

Optional client CSS — extra toolbar clearance
.us-cco-sticky-tabs { --us-cco-sticky-offset: 40px; }

Class removal, replaced CCO content, resizing and page restoration reconcile automatically. UnionSuiteStickyTabs.refresh() requests a manual refresh. Runtime data-us-cco-sticky and --us-cco-sticky-top/height are managed by the theme; do not author them. Easy Edit, us-report-no-styling, mobile widths and less than 100px available height leave ordinary navigation in place. Print also uses normal flow. Native tab clicks, selection and requests are retained.

Supported layout is the supplied .cco.tabs-wrapper.tabs-vertical structure. CSS sticky remains subject to ancestor overflow/containment: custom scrolling or clipped wrappers need a live check, and the theme does not rewrite their layout. Verify the sticky end boundary, long-menu scrolling, partial refresh and banner overlap on the actual page. This optional behaviour does not resolve or change the deferred grey-background/standalone-navigation design decision.

CCO tabs switch without reloading the page

Implemented automatically; no author class. A native CCO tab click normally posts the whole page back, and iMIS redirects to the page URL for that tab, reloading everything. With updated zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css, every native CCO on every page switches in place instead, including CCOs nested inside a tab and inside an EmbeddedCCO. Only that CCO's content is replaced. The scroll position is kept, and the address bar is updated, so a reload or a shared link opens the displayed tab.

{{CCO_SWITCH_DEMO}}

Loading states. The clicked tab is selected at once and marked aria-busy. After 150ms it shows the same 12px tab spinner as native tab loading, and the tab content is covered by an opaque --bg-surface panel with the shared section spinner, 180px down and kept in view when the page is scrolled. The content cannot be clicked while it loads. A polite status announces Loading and the tab name. Requests that finish within 150ms show no spinner. Reduced motion stops both spinners rotating; the cover follows dark mode through its surface token.

Clicking again while a tab loads. The latest click wins. The selection and tab spinner move to the newest tab straight away, while the content cover stays in place until that tab is ready. A switch that has started always completes first, so the page state is never left half-changed. Clicking the tab already loading, or the tab already displayed, cancels the waiting click. A click during one of iMIS's own partial updates, such as sorting a report, waits for it to finish.

What still works natively: report sorting, paging, page size and filters, panel editors, saves and popups keep using iMIS's own partial updates. iParts that reload the page natively, such as Communication Preferences Save, still reload, and land on the displayed tab.

Placement and prerequisites. The theme recognises the native CCO structure: div.cco.tabs-wrapper directly containing the …radTab_Top strip and the matching …radPage multipage. Other tab strips, such as the Address iPart's, keep native behaviour. Upload zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css together; load zzDarkMode.css last as usual. No iPart setting, template or HTML change is needed.

A CCO keeps native page reloads when:

  • us-report-no-styling is on the CCO iPart or an ancestor;
  • Easy Edit is on;
  • it is not a Telerik tab strip with a multipage, for example a wizard layout;
  • either off switch below is used.

Turn it off site-wide in the client THeme/UnionSuite-Client/Config.js. The supplied file sets enabled: true; false returns every CCO to native page reloads. The setting is read at each click, so the file may load before or after zUnionSuite.js.

Client Config.js — plain JavaScript, no script tags
window.UnionSuiteCcoSwitchConfig = {
  ...window.UnionSuiteCcoSwitchConfig,
  enabled: true // false = every CCO uses native page reloads.
};

Turn it off in one browser tab to diagnose a page without affecting other users. Run this in the browser console. It is kept in sessionStorage, so it survives reloads until the browser tab is closed. A taskbar developer-mode toggle for this switch is planned, not implemented.

Browser console — this browser tab only
UnionSuiteCcoSwitch.disable(); // native page reloads in this tab
UnionSuiteCcoSwitch.enable();  // switch in place again
How a switch works, URL keys and failure handling

Clicking a tab fetches the page URL that native navigation would load, replaces only that CCO's displayed view, and switches the page-level form state (ViewState, event validation, anti-forgery token, PageInstanceKey, form action and the CCO's tab state) to the fetched page. The master page Save/Cancel bar (div.button-panel, outside the CCO beside .main) is replaced with the fetched page's copy too, or removed when that page has none, so a tab such as Login Credentials shows its Save and Cancel buttons after an in-place switch. The fetched page's update panels are registered with PageRequestManager. The view is then initialized in native order: its JavaScript, page-level grid managers, the server's $create blocks, then one native refresh per report lister, which returns the grids with iMIS's own scripts. Last, iMIS's page-level Chosen line (jQuery('.chosen-select').chosen(…)) is replayed for multi-select filters still bare: a report that hides its results until Find has no refresh button, so without it the lists showed as plain list boxes until Find. The view being left has its components disposed and the handlers its initialization registered removed.

URL keys. Each CCO's tab is selected by a URL parameter named with 12 hex characters of its placement key, which the browser does not know. It is taken from the current URL after a native tab click when that is unambiguous. Otherwise the first switch of a CCO sends one background tab-strip postback, as a native click does, and reads the key from the redirect; nothing is applied to the page. Keys are stored in localStorage under UnionSuiteCcoSwitch:keys, per page path and tab strip, for 7 days. A stale key cannot break the page: the fetched page lacks the expected view, the key is discarded and that click uses native navigation.

Failure. If a switch cannot complete (an unexpected or sign-in response, a missing view or a failed discovery), the theme falls back to native navigation to the tab, with the native tab spinner. A page restored with Back resumes with no loading state.

Known limits. DOMContentLoaded and window.load handlers inside tab content do not run, because the page has already loaded; other page-wide startup statements are not replayed. Report rows using RadButton classes may be listed as uninitialized in diagnostics; the live checklist found them working. A switch costs the same server render as a native tab change (0.5–3.5 seconds measured); the saving is the avoided post, redirect and full-page reload.

Diagnostics. UnionSuiteCcoSwitch.report() returns the current setting, the switch in progress and any waiting click, and the last 20 switches with timings, key source, form-state changes, panel registration, initialization results, uninitialized controls and page errors. It never includes ViewState, tokens, row data or URL values other than tab numbers. UnionSuiteCcoSwitch.refresh() reattaches to a replaced PageRequestManager.

Browser console — copy the report for support
copy(JSON.stringify(UnionSuiteCcoSwitch.report(), null, 2));

Runtime hooks, managed by the theme and not for authoring: data-us-cco-switch-busy and data-us-cco-switch-loading on the multipage, data-us-cco-switching on the document root, .us-cco-switch__cover and .us-cco-switch__status. While a switch runs, the theme's report-refresh overlays and native …_UpdateProgress1 images are hidden so the internal lister refreshes do not show a second spinner. The example above reproduces the loading presentation with the production CSS; switching itself is verified by THeme/UnionSuite-Guides/usage/tests/test-cco-switch.cjs and on the live site.

Appearance and responsive behaviour

V5 uses a grey menu with white inactive rows, an accent outline/left edge on selection and a white content panel with a neutral grey border. Above 600px, the vertical strip has no right border, allowing the selected tab to meet the white content surface without a grey seam. Mobile borders are unchanged. The selected tint holds across 78% of the tab and fades to white over the final 22%. H2 uses attached white tabs with an accent top cap. Enabled H2 tabs gain an accent border on hover, matching V5; the selected tab keeps its open bottom edge into the panel. Disabled tabs retain their disabled styling. At 600px or below, both use a single horizontally scrolling row and at least 44px tab targets; V5 uses a solid pale accent fill and top cap. Subtle edge fades indicate overflow. Selecting a tab or resizing reveals the current tab without scrolling the whole page.

Enabled native tabs show an inset shadow and subtle dark shading while pressed. The shared JS tracks pointer activation on native tab strips so Telerik focus transfers do not retain a blue outline; keyboard navigation restores focus outlines. Upload both shared CSS and JS for this behaviour. Horizontal selected tabs overlap the rail border to open into the white content panel; the selected tab returns to its normal accent treatment. Keyboard :focus-visible retains its focus outline, including forced-colour mode. Disabled tabs do not receive the pressed shading. This applies automatically to horizontal, nested and vertical native strips with shared CSS.

Mobile vertical menus include an automatically generated All sections disclosure. Its list shows current and disabled states. Choosing an enabled section invokes that existing native tab's click handler, closes the list and focuses the tab; native validation, cancellation, selection and requests remain in control. Escape closes the list and returns focus to the trigger. Clicking outside closes it. Horizontal and nested Address tabs remain independent.

--accent supplies caps/outlines, --accent-100 the selected tint, --bg-surface the white surface, --bg-sunken the grey rail, --border neutral outlines, --brand-900 selected text and --border-focus keyboard focus. Type uses --font-ui. Forced-colour mode retains a selected outline. Page layout and component styling live in separate US-NATIVE-TABS-PAGE-LAYOUT and US-NATIVE-TABS-COMPONENT sections of the shared stylesheet; mobile enhancement is UnionSuiteTabs in shared JS, independent of banners.

Lifecycle and limits

The adapter follows native selected/disabled attributes and classes, reconciles replaced controls after partial updates, cleans up detached controls, and updates overflow on resize. It does not replace native keyboard handling or switch panels. Native keyboard support must remain installed. If a custom integration changes markup outside the observed lifecycle, call UnionSuiteTabs.refresh(). Without shared JavaScript, CSS scrolling remains available but All sections and automatic reveal are absent. Other skins, right/bottom positioning, multi-level/scroll-button modes and custom tab widgets have not been validated. Verify native requests, cancelled selection, validation and AJAX refresh in the live iMIS installation before rollout; local examples do not make server requests.

Approved example and archived comparisons. V1–V4 and H1/H3 remain reference alternatives. us-tab-study and us-tabs-v1..v5/us-tabs-h1..h3 are archive-only modifiers and must not be added to production pages.

IQA browser: search, favourites and recent

Implemented in the theme; live verification on the dev tenant is pending. On the IQA browser (/AsiCommon/Controls/IQA/Default.aspx), the native Quick find box keeps filtering the open folder as before, and also opens a panel underneath it. The box is widened to 360px and drawn and focused as the theme's other fields; the panel is drawn as the taskbar's contact quick search: banded section headings, ruled rows and the same spinning circles beside Searching… while the server answers. No author classes are needed.

{{IQA_FINDER_DEMO}}

Using it

  • Search every folder: type part of a query's name or path. Every word must match; names rank above paths, and an exact name comes first. Choose a result (or press Enter for the top one) to go to its folder with the query selected; the pencil opens it in the designer.
  • Go to a location: paste or type a path starting $/ or @/ (from Show Usage's copy button, say). A Location row heads the panel; it, or Enter, puts the path in the address box and presses Go. A query's own path goes to its folder with the query selected.
  • Favourites: the star on a result, or on a query row in the folder list (shown on hover, focus or selection, and always on a starred query). Favourites are saved to the user's i4u_UT_UserSettings row (SettingsKey IQA-Favourites), so they follow the user between computers; up to about 100.
  • Recent: with the box empty, the panel shows Favourites, Recently opened (every query opened in the designer, by any route) and Recent searches (searches used to go to or open a query). Recent items stay in this browser.

Keyboard: Down from the box moves into the panel, Up and Down move through it, Escape closes it and returns to the box.

Installation and data

Upload Scripts/IQA-Finder.js, index.js and zUnionSuite.css. The loader loads the script on the IQA browser and the IQA designer only. Searching runs the IQA $/_i4u_/Core/Admin/Query Search - IQA Document Tree Taskbar through /api/query: Document Type Code equals IQD, an optional Document Path contains filter, and the columns Document Version Key, Document Name, Document Path and Updated On. The longest word typed goes to the filter; the rows that come back are ranked in the browser by every word. The filter's name is read once from GET /api/QueryParameterDefinition and remembered. Typing waits a quarter of a second, and a newer search cancels an older one. Favourites and recent items are named from what searches return, or looked up by key through DocumentSummary. Styles are the US-IQA-FINDER section of zUnionSuite.css, using --bg-surface, --bg-subtle, --border, --text-strong, --text-muted, --accent for a starred query, --icon-hover-bg and --shadow-md. Script API: UnionSuiteIqaFinder.find(text) runs a search; .search(entries, text) is the ranking. Tests: node --test tools/test-iqa-finder.cjs.

Limits: the search covers query names and paths, not descriptions or contents. The server returns at most 200 queries containing the longest word, which are then narrowed by the rest. Whatever the search IQA returns is searched, so add a status filter there if recycled queries should be left out. Going to a query uses the page's own address box and Go, so it behaves as typing the folder there.

IQA designer: Show Usage

Implemented in the theme; live verification on the dev tenant is pending. A Show Usage button in the IQA designer header (an icon-only button with a sitemap icon, named in its tooltip, so the header keeps to one line) opens a drawer from the right listing everything that uses the open IQA. The drawer sits over the designer and never moves it. No author classes are needed.

{{WHERE_USED_DEMO}}

What it checks

PlaceWhat counts as a useOpen in new tab
Content pagesAn iPart naming the IQA by path or key (Query Menu, trackers, charts, IQA data sources), a $/ path inside Content HTML, or a Query Menu pointed at the IQA's folder ("Uses the folder").Content page editor
Business object value listsmsprop:ValueListQuery on a business object property.Business Object Designer
Panel source value listsAn IQA value-list rule on a panel source property.None yet
Communication templatesAn IQA named in the template.Communication editor
Alerts and Process AutomationA task's data source or action query.Task editor
Other IQAsAn IQA used as another IQA's source, or named in its display template (Query Template HTML, such as a banner's data-us-facts-query).IQA designer
UnionSuite themeAn IQA path written in a theme script, named by its US-… section or action key.None

Each result names the item, its path and how it uses the IQA, with Copy path (the shared copy button) and Open in new tab. The open IQA is never replaced. Include ASI system content also shows matches in ASI's own folders (iCore, iSamples, Samples, Common, ContactManagement, Fundraising) and ASI's own alerts; it is off by default and remembered per browser. Not checked: Flowz workflows (a CloudToolz flow is planned) and pages outside the page tree, such as ASI's admin pages.

The shared results

The drawer shows the results of the last check, one set shared by everyone, stored gzip-compressed in i4u_UT_Configuration.Payload (varbinary(MAX)) on the row Module System, Category IQA, Code WhereUsedIndex. Its Value says what it holds; LastUpdated and UpdatedBy say when and who, shown as "Last checked 2 hours ago by …". The designer fetches it quietly after loading, so the drawer opens on results.

Check again appears for anyone whose designer shows Save. It looks through every place, three at a time, in about 10–15 seconds, clearing the old results and refilling each place as it is checked. A place still being checked shows Checking and a short moving line along its bottom edge (a still line with reduced motion); both go as soon as it is done. It carries on if the drawer is closed. A check that cannot read a place saves nothing and keeps showing the last check, with a warning naming the place. The first time, the drawer says "Not checked yet" and offers Check now. The save also needs the user's write access to i4u_UT_Configuration.

Installation

Upload Scripts/IQA-WhereUsed.js, index.js, zUnionSuite.css and zzDarkMode.css. The loader loads the script only on /QueryBuilder/Design.aspx, after the shared theme script, and lists every theme script in window.UnionSuiteLoader.modules for the theme scan. Add the Payload varbinary(MAX) property to the i4u_UT_Configuration panel source first. The button sits before the Enhance switch, after Copy Path and Copy Key; it is disabled with a tooltip until the query has been saved.

Rule for templates and Content HTML

Write an IQA path in a Query Template, Content HTML or banner attribute (data-us-facts-query, data-us-iqa and similar) as one whole path, for example data-us-facts-query="$/_i4u_/Core/CA/v2/API - Manage Agreement - Banner Details". A path assembled from query fields, such as "$/…/{#query.Folder}/Details", cannot be found, so the check will not list it. A folder attribute (such as data-us-activity-folder) is shown as "Uses the folder" for the IQAs directly inside it.

Rules for theme scripts

Write every IQA path in a theme script as one whole string, for example '$/_i4u_/Core/Admin/Taskbar/Recent IQAs - User', never assembled from parts. The index finds the theme's IQAs by scanning its scripts, and node tools/check-usage-sources.cjs fails on an assembled path. A new script added to index.js is scanned automatically.

Lifecycle, access and limits

The drawer is a labelled dialog that does not trap focus: Escape or × closes it and returns focus to the button, and the summary line is a polite status. Opening and closing slide over 220 ms and 180 ms; reduced motion makes them instant. Dark mode uses zzDarkMode.css. Tokens: --bg-surface, --bg-subtle, --border, --text-strong, --text-muted, --brand-50/--brand-900 for counts, --danger for warnings and --us-dialog-shadow. Script API: UnionSuiteWhereUsed.open(), .close(), .rebuild(), .readStored() and the pure .lookup(index, target). Tests: node --test tools/test-where-used.cjs. Research and decisions: prototypes/wip/iqa-where-used/.

Dialog title bars and footer dividers

Deferred by owner: Telerik alert, confirm and prompt dialogs have not been encountered in the current workflows. Leave them unchanged until a real example is supplied. Standard browser alert(), confirm() and prompt() dialogs also remain unchanged; no themed replacement is planned at this stage. This does not affect the approved content-window title, icon and footer styling below.

Implemented: the supplied classic table-rendered Bootstrap RadWindow has the theme's popup frame: a 1px --border edge, --us-dialog-radius corners, --us-dialog-shadow, a white (--bg-surface) title bar with one rule under it, and the --us-dialog-scrim backdrop. The corner and side cells keep their native widths and cursors, so the window still resizes from its edges; the wrapper clips them to the rounded corners, and a maximized window is square. The theme dialog shares this frame. The title uses panel-family title typography with consistent reload, maximize/restore and close icons. Navy hover, inset pressed shading and a pressed border distinguish interaction; Close uses red hover/pressed states. Keyboard focus remains visible. The fixture below uses shared production CSS with simplified window geometry and simulated commands.

{{DIALOG_DEMO}}

Load zUnionSuite.css after the native skin in the parent page for window titles and controls. Load the shared CSS and zUnionSuite.js inside popup pages as well for their footer dividers: parent styles cannot cross an iframe boundary. No new classes are required on native windows. JavaScript marks documents whose URL has IsPopup=true with data-us-dialog-page. In those documents, hr dividers become a one-pixel theme border; existing CommandBar, LocalButtonBar and modal-footer top borders use the same colour and width. The supplied capture contains the outer window only, so confirm the actual footer element after deployment; custom divider markup may need the optional class below.

Native selectors are on the generated window, .rwTitlebarControls em for the title, and .rwControlButtons with rwReloadButton, rwMaximizeButton, rwRestoreButton and rwCloseButton. These are native classes, not a recipe for replacing Telerik. Embedded SVG masks need no extra icon font. Text labels and native titles remain present. Existing window dimensions, title truncation width, toolbar allocation, native click handlers, drag/resize handles and backdrop remain in control.

For an authored footer outside automatic popup detection, add to its existing action-bar container. This softens its existing top border or a direct child hr; it does not create an extra separator.

  <!-- Keep existing Save / Cancel controls and their handlers here. -->

Title type uses --font-ui and --text-strong; controls use --text-muted, --brand-50, --brand-900 and --brand-600. Dividers use --border; Close uses --danger and --danger-bg; keyboard focus uses --border-focus. Reduced motion removes colour transitions. Actions menus — approved option 5

Implemented in shared CSS and JavaScript. Neutral white menus, inset hover rows, optional headings/icons, separators and one submenu level. The example runs the production controller; its action handlers only display a message.
