Motion, keyboard and lifecycle

The root menu expands down and collapses up over 400ms. Above 950px on a fine pointer, a submenu opens beside the parent and flips near the viewport edge. At 950px or below, children expand/collapse inline over 400ms, moving subsequent rows. A soft glint draws only the child-row line over 1000ms, once per opening. Chevrons rotate over 220ms. Reduced motion removes these effects and leaves a static line. Touch targets are at least 44px.

Tab follows ordinary buttons and links. Enter/Space activate buttons; Right Arrow opens a focused submenu trigger; Left Arrow/Escape closes the current branch and returns focus. Escape closes the root. Closing content becomes inert immediately. Outside click closes the menu, and leaving by keyboard focus closes it without taking focus back. These are disclosure lists, not ARIA menu widgets; do not add role="menu"/"menuitem" without implementing that separate keyboard pattern.

One document listener handles every menu and observes added/replaced content, including partial postbacks. No per-panel endRequest binding is required. UnionSuiteActions.refresh() is available for explicit reconciliation. Resize and page scroll close menus; desktop parent-list scrolling closes child flyouts to avoid stale placement. One root menu and one sibling branch stay open at a time. Menus initialize closed.

Component variables live on .us-actions: --us-actions-duration:400 is a unitless millisecond value used by JS; --us-actions-glint-duration:1000ms is a CSS duration; --us-actions-surface uses --bg-surface; --us-actions-hover uses --brand-50; --us-actions-line blends --brand-600 with the surface. Glint uses --brand-600 and --accent. Text, border and keyboard focus use the shared semantic tokens. Override component variables in zzClientSpecific.css, not the generated examples.

Supported scope is the supplied menu families and one submenu level. Other Telerik/context menus, transformed containing blocks and unusual third-party dropdown handlers need integration testing. Apply to a menu or ancestor before initialization to exclude it; us-report-no-styling also excludes it. Verify real popup paths, permissions, cancelled commands and partial updates in iMIS before rollout. The local fixture cannot validate server behaviour.

Archived alternatives remain available; option 5 is the approved shared implementation.

10 / NEXT FEATURES

Documented plans, not active switches.

Home page — local layout trial

The My tasks Query Template Display now uses three shared helpers directly. Put in its iPart CSS class field. iMIS inserts that class div inside ContentItemContainer around the native panel. Set a nonempty Title and load the shared CSS/JS as described in Query Template search. The classes supply generic search and its funnel, the task-only Show completed toggle, and the Add task plus icon. No search, toggle or button HTML is needed in the optional template Header.

us-query-search us-task-completed-filter us-action-home-add-task

Include data-us-task-completed="{#query.IsCompleted}" on an inner element of each repeating result, using a verified query alias returning true/1 for completed and false/0 for outstanding. See the copyable task template and working visual. Register home.add-task with class us-action-home-add-task in the client Actions.js file using the verified task editor and signed-in assignee context. The home preview supplies only a sample dialog.

The shared funnel controls start collapsed, slide open/closed over 180ms, and clear the search when closed; the completed-tasks icon remains available in the header and keeps its independent setting. The button exposes Show filters/Hide filters, aria-expanded and aria-controls; hidden/animating controls are inert, opening focuses search if the trigger still has focus, rapid toggles reverse smoothly, and reduced motion skips the transition. The local footer initially reads “5 outstanding, 2 overdue”; these counts track outstanding records in the shared filtered results and update immediately when tasks are completed or reopened. Completed records are excluded from both totals. Hover or keyboard focus on either task control highlights the entire row across the scrolling list, including its side padding, while separators remain inset. Task actions, counts and row layout remain local prototype behaviours.

Under the My tasks header, the description reads “Your outstanding tasks and those completed in the last 30 days.” The shared icon helper generates the plus with an accessible Add task label. The local data controller supplies dated sample records and the fixed preview date of 12 September 2026, including that day and the previous 29 days; completing a sample task records that date. The shared completion toggle filters those supplied records. A live query must use the site's current date and verified completion field to supply the correct date window.

The whole-list count/View all tasks row is still local home-task-footer styling. The shared us-list__footer is an optional divided footer within a list item under us-list or QueryTemplateSet; it does not supply a panel-wide total, navigation destination or fixed footer outside a scrolling list. The shared us-query-footer recipe now provides a whole-query divider and right-aligned link in native Footer HTML; data-us-task-summary optionally receives the filtered outstanding count. The homepage controller still supplies its own overdue count.

Above 1150px, the work and Staff Bulletin columns use a 2:1 ratio after allowing for the gap. From 901px to 1150px, the bulletin retains its 340px width and work takes the remaining space. At 900px and below, work and bulletin stack in one column.

Open the home page prototype: a compact welcome, Needs Attention membership queues, a searchable My tasks list and the approved Staff Bulletin on the right. Search matches task titles, member names and member IDs; Show completed retains that search and includes completed tasks after outstanding tasks. Completed tasks are hidden initially and use muted, struck-through titles, checked green boxes and Actioned dates when shown. Tick a row's separate checkbox to complete it immediately, or untick it to reopen it and restore its original due label. The checkbox has a task-specific accessible name, checked state and Space/Enter support. The title/date/chevron remains a separate detail button; the dialog offers the same complete/reopen actions. The completion filter exposes its pressed state, the result count is announced politely, and no-match searches have a distinct empty message.

Completing a task slides its row left and fades it over 650ms, then collapses the space over 250ms. Counts update immediately. With Show completed off the row stays hidden; with it on, the row appears in the completed group with its actioned date after the exit finishes. Exiting rows are inert; focus moves to the next available checkbox, the preceding checkbox or Search tasks (the header filter button when controls are closed). Rapid completions can animate together. Changing search, the completion filter or the empty preview ends pending exits and renders the current task states. Reopening clears the actioned date without an exit animation. Reduced motion skips the slide/collapse and finishes an active exit when enabled; mobile keeps the checkbox visible, windows 950px or narrower give a 44px target, and forced colours retain the visible check. These behaviours and actioned dates are local simulations; reloading resets the sample tasks.

The shorter task panel aligns with the bottom of the bulletin on desktop. Task rows and five sample bulletin posts scroll within bounded panels while headings, any open search/toggle controls and the task footer stay visible. A bottom fade appears only while more content remains below, clearing at the end or when everything fits; it updates on filtering, task changes and resizing. Scroll areas support keyboard scrolling and visible focus, reduced motion removes the fade transition, and forced colours retain the native scrollbar without a gradient. These layout and scroll behaviours are local trials. Global contact search and recent contacts stay in the persistent taskbar. The preview uses illustrative records, clickable sample task/queue dialogs and an empty-task control; no live queries or writes occur. This optional companion link needs the repository files; the guide itself remains usable offline.

Page composition and demo behaviour live in prototypes/Home/, generated by node tools/build-home-preview.cjs. The home-welcome and home-welcome-date classes are shared theme features; the other home-* classes remain trial-only. Needs Attention now uses the shared us-attention Content HTML component with simulated folder/IQA responses in the home preview. The bulletin uses the shared us-staff-bulletin us-action-home-manage-bulletin classes. The task fixture uses the shared search, completion filter and icon action helpers above, with native result wrappers and the extra iPart class div. Its task completion, animation, row styling and count controller remain local. See the prototype notes for sources, token mapping, keyboard/empty-state behaviour and the iMIS integration boundary. Confirm query fields, permissions and destinations before promoting the layout. The bulletin authoring redesign remains deferred.

Finance dashboard — styling trial

Open the UnionSuite finance prototype for an overview, payment-run detail and declined-payment report using the actual shared banner, badge, button, icon and client-branding styles. The trial applies us-finance to its body and keeps all finance layout under that scope in finance-unionsuite.css. Banner tab switcher

Deferred for a later session at the owner's request. The project TODO records the implementation checklist. The current template remains visual only.

The home prototype follows the supplied dashboard screenshot: evening greeting, Tasks/Search/Stats switcher, queue counts 2/4/0, two sample tasks and three bulletin posts. The shared standalone tabs retain their 100px minimum width, closer greeting spacing and divider 4px below the buttons. Tasks is the default; Search remains an offline placeholder. Stats now composes the implemented Membership Overview Content HTML cards, using the verified 13 September 2026 IQA capture in this local preview. Production data loading and component styles live in zUnionSuite.js and zUnionSuite.css; only page geometry and offline responses remain in prototypes/Home. The six-month history is a labelled placeholder awaiting CloudToolz. There are no selectors, exports or drilldowns: dedicated reports handle those. Stats cards align to the tallest card within each row and retain natural heights when stacked; the page layout remains a local content-only trial without global site chrome or a connected CCO.

Stats data: the shared loader displays blank category, financial-status and group codes as (empty), retaining their counts in reconciliation. Code fields provide row labels; Name fields in these IQAs contain dimension headings. Total members remains a current figure without a historical comparison. Joined and Resigned compare equivalent elapsed days of the current and previous months using the corrected AND date filters; CloudToolz history will be connected later.

Stats data verification: tools/probe-home-stats.js uses GET /api/query with named filters and discovers prompts via GET /api/QueryParameterDefinition?QueryPath=.... It reports ambiguous date-filter names for review rather than executing the dated IQAs without their filters. The probe retains unassigned counts, checks complete paging and reconciles the current member breakdowns. It is a console diagnostic, not a live dashboard loader; avoid the deprecated /api/iqa endpoint for new work.

For lightweight dashboards, connect the banner tabs to ordinary page zones. Render the dashboard once, then show or hide matching zones without a page reload or a new query caused by tab selection.

  1. Keep stable keys in the banner HTML: for example, data-us-tabs="membership" on the nav div and data-us-tab="overview" on its Overview button.
  2. Put matching classes on participating zones. One tab may control several zones; unmarked zones stay visible across all tabs. No custom per-page JavaScript mapping should be needed for ordinary zones.
  3. Resolve the native zone, its separately rendered title and any wholly owned layout containers. Hide inactive content without leaving empty columns or hiding shared content.
  4. Preserve the existing nodes, field/filter values and banner state. Add keyboard semantics, safe focus handling, hidden-widget reflow and ASP.NET refresh recovery.
  5. Hide content only after successful initialization. If pairing fails, the nav is deleted or Easy Edit is enabled, leave the ordinary content available.

All participating zones and their queries still load initially. This approach improves the switching experience; it does not provide lazy loading or access control. Keep page and iPart permissions in place.

A separate CCO adapter may still be useful for pages deliberately using CCO. Its proposed source classes remain us-tabs-source us-tabset-record, with keys mapped to verified native values. It would preserve the native loading/selection mechanism, including any existing reload behaviour. Do not apply both adapters to the same tab set or treat CCO page views as ordinary zones.

The source of truth for the zone-first implementation plan is Banner-Tabs-Plan.md. Both adapters remain planned; the current visual tab buttons stay disabled.

Native business-action discovery — planned

Proposed iPart classes include us-action-create-case, us-action-add-job, us-action-add-address, us-action-add-note, us-action-view-profile and us-action-view-history. These class-based native-action discovery helpers remain planned. The implemented Actions registry above accepts explicitly registered client functions; it does not discover or generate business actions. Verify each native destination/handler, context and permissions before implementing it.

Combined activity feed

Proposed hooks are us-activity-feed on a dedicated zone, us-activity-feed-title on its associated title, us-activity-source on participating iParts and us-activity-item on repeated items. Sorting, metadata, source limits, search/counts and refresh behaviour remain planned.

Other native families—navigation, forms, general panels, trackers, dialogs and CCO tabs—remain governed by their existing iMIS styles unless a documented feature explicitly opts them in. Prototype-only designs are references, not automatically available theme components.

11 / KEEP IT CURRENT

One maintained handbook.

Stylesheet ownership: 99-Orion.css is our editable foundation. Prefer fixing existing native colours, spacing, borders and states there, replacing hard-coded colours with shared tokens where appropriate. Keep tokens and UnionSuite additions (IQA enhancements, spinners, taskbar and banners) in zUnionSuite.css. Generated client branding values belong in THeme/UnionSuite-Client/Branding.css. Deliberate client-specific CSS rules belong in THeme/UnionSuite-Client/Override.css; clients may edit this file as well. Load Branding.css after the shared theme CSS, followed by Override.css. Consolidate redundant overrides as each component is touched, preserving and checking native behaviour; existing overrides have not all been migrated and no blanket rewrite is required. Deploy changed foundation CSS alongside affected shared assets.

This page is the theme's user-facing usage guide. Update it in the same change whenever a feature, class, template, token or installation requirement changes. Reading and sharing the generated HTML needs no server, network connection or package installation. Copy buttons fall back to text selection if the browser disallows clipboard access.

Maintained sourceResponsibility
docs/Usage-Guide.source.htmlExplanations, classes, recipes, feature status and troubleshooting.
docs/usage-guide.css / usage-guide.jsThis guide's presentation, search, token preview, copying and downloads.
../../THeme/UnionSuite-Guides/usage/build/build-theme-usage.cjsEmbeds current tokens, component defaults, templates, shared JS and the combined component preview. Checks script parity and records a source fingerprint.
Usage-Guide.htmlGenerated, portable deliverable. Rebuild it; do not edit it directly.
Run from the project root
node THeme/UnionSuite-Guides/usage/build/build-theme-usage.cjs
node THeme/UnionSuite-Guides/usage/build/build-theme-usage.cjs --check

If banner CSS/JS or the complete banner template changes, first run node THeme/UnionSuite-Guides/usage/build/build-banner-preview.cjs to regenerate its standalone preview and regenerate the legacy compatibility files from shared JS. Then rebuild this guide. Its embedded source is a snapshot, not a live fetch of neighbouring files.

For every implemented element include visual examples, click-to-copy author classes, the exact placement field or HTML element, and relevant HTML in a code block with a Copy button. Also document prerequisites, optional blocks, tokens, keyboard behaviour, lifecycle and limits. Planned and runtime-only hooks must remain clearly identified. Banner CSS and behaviour are maintained in marked shared-theme sections. Legacy fallbacks are generated; deploy and load the shared replacement before retiring old includes.

Project documentation index Product context and constraints Theme setup & integration notes Shared CSS & tokens Native button inventory & mapping Client branding CSS Client custom overrides Shared report JavaScript Banner developer guide Button spinners — simple ring, two aligning rings and compact spinning circles Splitting blocks — six motion alternatives including single- and two-colour playful reassembly with two imperfect fits, success on the third attempt and a faster fluid final 360-degree spin

Selected section animation: spinning circles, using primary brand and accent colours at the slower comparison speed. Buttons retain the simple ring. Native popup and panel loader integration is included in the shared CSS and JS; live verification is still required.

Native popup and panel loading

Install updated zUnionSuite.css and zUnionSuite.js on the parent page. No author class is required: UnionSuiteSectionLoading decorates .rwWindowContent.rwLoading and .RadAjax > .raDiv automatically, including dynamically created overlays. iMIS retains visibility, positioning, dimming, requests and error handling. A popup also shows the spinner from the moment its frame starts loading a page until that page's load event, with aria-busy on the content cell meanwhile, so the wait while iMIS builds a page whose cache has expired (several seconds) reads as loading rather than a blank window. A popup still loading after 2 seconds revs every 2 seconds: its rings whirl an extra turn and stretch, and three accent ripples leave 180ms apart, each fainter than the one before. The ripples are added inside the popup's spinner only; section and panel loaders keep the plain spin. The spinner fades in and out (180ms); with reduced motion it appears and goes instantly, and neither the spin nor the rev runs. The theme only reads the popup's document to tell its initial blank page from the real one; it does not change the popup or add a competing indicator to IQA Find.

For custom HTML, put on a decorative span inside your loading region. The owner must show/hide it and provide accessible status text and aria-busy. Colours use --brand-600 and --accent; reduced motion stops rotation.

Custom section indicator
<div role="status">
  <span class="section-loader-spinning-circles" aria-hidden="true"></span>
  Loading records…
</div>

Native form integration comparison: captured Theme Upload, XML Import, Edit Address, Add Contact and Panel Editor structures. Editable PanelEditorEditForm .PanelField.Top fields automatically have a 6px gap below a direct label followed by PanelFieldValue, keeping the theme focus glow clear of the label. This includes textareas and selects; read-only fields retain their spacing. No author class or JS is required. Native .PanelEditorEditForm fields automatically use a 150px label column, 16px gap and 280px control column when the containing editor column is at least 460px wide. Below that width, labels stack above full-width controls. Sizing follows the panel column, including multi-column editors, rather than the screen. Native text inputs, selects, textareas and CalendarInput2 date-picker wrappers share the available control width; checkboxes and radios stay compact. Right-labelled panel checkboxes use an 18px control column and 12px gap, aligned to the first 20px label line; long labels wrap without pushing the checkbox down. Required markers have a 4px gap. No author class or extra JavaScript is needed. Panel date inputs use the same 36px height, 6px corners, border and typography as ordinary fields, retaining the native calendar trigger. Telerik calendar operation still requires a live check after deployment. The month header centres its chevron alongside the text using inline flex alignment, keeping the compact 4px padding. The active day uses one focus outline on its link; native rcFocus cell decoration is cleared to avoid a double frame on first open. A selected, focused day retains its filled selection with a white inset focus outline. Native IQA result rows use a uniform surface without alternating grey stripes; dividers, hover and selected-row states remain. The native query selector has no enclosing background, border or inset padding. Its label is semibold (600), and the full query row has a subtle bottom divider above the filters. The dropdown retains its normal field border. When its own ContentPanel contains FilterPanelHorizontal, the selector retains a top label and one-third desktop width, matching native col-sm-4 filters; below 768px it fills the available width. The native three-column filter layout is unchanged. Approved native field styling applies automatically through PanelFieldValue; do not substitute us-form globally. Single-line preview fields use 36px height and zero vertical padding to centre their text; textareas retain multiline padding. The organisation autocomplete preview uses one border on its rcbInner wrapper, with a borderless inner input. The address proposal hides deprecated Phone/Fax/Email rows, sets its remaining fields to a consistent 280px width (capped to their container), and uses the same grey chevron and right inset for Country, State and Status. Notes aligns with the other input controls on desktop and retains the stacked layout on small screens and its multiline height. Native select and Telerik dropdown behaviour is preserved; no custom class is required. Theme Upload required labels use an inline asterisk with a 4px gap, replacing the overlapping native background marker and respecting HideRequiredAsterisks. Theme Upload includes a 12px top inset so upload-success messages clear the dialog header, and uses compact spacing around metadata, the responsive setting and file help to keep footer actions within easier reach. Its native filename, Select and Remove controls share one row; scoped overrides counter the editor’s built-in 150px !important upload widths so the filename can expand without clipping Select. Select and Remove use navy secondary styling with independent hover, pressed and keyboard-focus states; hovering the upload row does not highlight both buttons. Select also reflects hover, pressed and keyboard focus from Telerik’s transparent file input. Explicit hover borders and scoped padding overrides keep Remove centred despite native editor rules. The live Telerik file input and upload behaviour remain intact. The preview file chooser uses a white surface and subtle border within the grey upload panel. Dragging a file over the offline upload area highlights a drop zone; dropping one ZIP (Theme Upload) or XML (Import) within the preview size limit selects it locally and announces the filename without uploading. Its offline file chooser replaces uninitialised Telerik duplicates for review; the live theme preserves Telerik Select/Remove and its transparent input. Shared JS highlights the upload area and passes dropped files to the native input/change events; native file validation and upload remain in control. These changes are implemented in the shared theme. No author class is needed; address fields are matched by their native ID endings. Hidden deprecated rows still participate in native submission/validation. Telerik RadUpload and RadComboBox still need separate live checks. The reference includes the approved styles, offline upload demonstration and copyable shared CSS.
